# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **`linkify` utility for auto-detecting URLs and `path:line` references in TUI tool output** (`src/cli/tui/utils/linkify.ts`, `src/cli/tui/utils/linkify.test.ts`, `src/cli/tui/components/ToolRow.tsx`, `tests/cli/tui/ToolRow.test.tsx`, commit `7af9be2a` `feat(cli): auto-linkify URLs and file:line refs in tool output`): New string transform that scans tool-output text for two match categories and wraps each match in an OSC-8 hyperlink escape sequence via the existing `hyperlink()` helper (`src/cli/tui/utils/hyperlink.ts`). Supporting terminals (iTerm2, WezTerm, ghostty, Apple Terminal, VS Code, Cursor, Hyper, Warp, kitty, Windows Terminal) render the wrapped text as a clickable link; non-supporting terminals receive byte-identical plain text, so the transform is safe to apply unconditionally.

  Public surface:
  - `linkify(text: string, cwd?: string): string` — auto-detect URLs and `path:line` references in `text` and return a new string with detected spans wrapped in OSC-8 escapes. `cwd` defaults to `process.cwd()` and is used to resolve relative file paths to absolute `file://` URIs. Passing an explicit `cwd` from a test or a caller running in a non-cwd context (agent worktree, `--workdir`) keeps the fragment URIs stable.
  - Two match categories are recognised:
    - **URLs**: `https://`, `http://`, and `file://` prefixes. The regex is `/\b(https?:\/\/|file:\/\/)[^\s<>"']+/g`; trailing sentence punctuation (`.,;:!?)]}>`) is stripped from the captured URL and re-appended as plain text after the OSC-8 wrap so `See https://example.com.` does not linkify the period.
    - **`path:line` references**: relative or absolute file paths followed by `:<line>` and an optional `:<column>`. The path segment must contain at least one `/` OR a `.` followed by 1-6 word characters (a file extension) — this filters out timestamps (`12:34`), bare `host:port` strings (`localhost:3000`), version strings (`1.2.3`), and `key: value` shaped strings. The generated URI is `file://<absolute-path>#<line>` or `file://<absolute-path>#<line>:<column>`; supporting terminals (VS Code, WezTerm) jump to the referenced line when the link is opened.
  - URL matches take precedence over `path:line` matches on overlap, so `https://example.com/foo/bar.ts:42` is treated as a single URL rather than a URL followed by a `path:42` fragment. Matches are then sorted by start index and spliced back into the input in a single pass.

  Wire-up: `src/cli/tui/components/ToolRow.tsx` now runs `linkify()` on the truncated body text for both the bash-output branch (`ToolRow.tsx:186`) and the generic-tool-output branch (`ToolRow.tsx:196`) before rendering. Windows paths with backslashes are normalised to forward slashes in the URI form so `file:///C:/Users/...` is well-formed.

  Test coverage:
  - `src/cli/tui/utils/linkify.test.ts` (new file, 155 lines): pins the observable OSC-8 byte sequence with `FORCE_HYPERLINK=1` so assertions are deterministic regardless of the CI TTY environment. Cases cover bare `https`/`http`/`file://` URLs, trailing-punctuation stripping, multi-URL and multi-line inputs, relative + absolute + dot-slash file paths, line-only and line+column forms, false-positive avoidance (`12:34`, `localhost:3000`, `token: 12345`, `1.2.3`), URL/`path:line` overlap resolution, degenerate inputs (empty string, no-match plain text), and the non-hyperlink terminal fallback (`NO_HYPERLINK=1` → plain text).
  - `tests/cli/tui/ToolRow.test.tsx` gains a `linkify integration` describe block (~36 lines) that renders `ToolRow` via `ink-testing-library`, stubs `FORCE_HYPERLINK`, and asserts on the OSC-8 escape sequence in the captured Ink frame for both a bash output containing a URL and a grep-style output containing a `path:line` reference.

## [1.22.16] - 2026-09-08

### Added

- **`open_plan` tool for surfacing agent-authored plan files** (`src/tool/tools/open-plan.ts`, `src/tool/tools/__tests__/open-plan.test.ts`, `src/tool/tools/index.ts`): Ports upstream kilocode `6024a76db feat(vscode): open agent-created plans` and `325656483 fix(vscode): scope plan opens to active session`. Upstream the tool asks the VSCode host to open a plan in an editor pane; Alexi has no VSCode webview, so this port adapts the semantics to a CLI/CI-safe "notify plan-ready" signal. The tool validates that the referenced file exists AND ends in `.md` (dedupes upstream `15041d024` — arbitrary paths are rejected), resolves relative paths against `context.workdir` (falls back to `process.cwd()`), and publishes a strongly-typed `plan.opened` event on the shared bus so downstream listeners (TUI, SAP integration, external CI hooks) can react. The tool intentionally does NOT spawn an editor process — Alexi runs headlessly in CI just as often as in a terminal, and forking an editor there would deadlock. Public surface:
  - `openPlanTool` — registered in `src/tool/tools/index.ts` alongside the other built-ins and exported for direct import.
  - `PlanOpened` — `defineEvent('plan.opened', ...)` schema; payload is `{ sessionId?: string, path: string, title?: string, timestamp: number }`. Consumers subscribe via `PlanOpened.subscribe(handler)` which returns an `unsubscribe` function.
  - `OpenPlanResult` — result data shape returned to the calling agent: `{ path: string, title: string }` where `path` is the resolved absolute path and `title` defaults to `path.basename(resolved)` when the caller omits the `title` parameter.

  Parameters (Zod schema):
  - `path: z.string()` — absolute or workdir-relative path to the plan markdown file.
  - `title: z.string().optional()` — optional human-readable title; defaults to the basename.

  Error paths (fail-fast, no event emitted):
  - Missing file or non-file target → `{ success: false, error: 'Plan file not found: <resolved>' }`.
  - Non-`.md` extension → `{ success: false, error: 'Plan must be a markdown file: <resolved>' }`.

  Event publish failures are swallowed with `try/catch` because the notification is not a correctness dependency of the tool. Test coverage (`src/tool/tools/__tests__/open-plan.test.ts`, 4 cases): (1) resolves relative path against `workdir` and emits `plan.opened` with `sessionId`, resolved `path`, and supplied `title`; (2) defaults `title` to the basename when the caller omits it; (3) rejects non-`.md` files with an error message matching `/markdown/`; (4) rejects missing files with an error message matching `/not found/`. Each case creates an isolated temp workdir with `fs.mkdtemp` and tears it down in `afterEach` — follow the same pattern when adding tool tests so they stay parallel-safe.

- **`sourceSessionId` cross-session reply routing on `agent_manager` `answer`** (`src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts`, `src/permission/agent-manager.ts`): Ports upstream kilocode `a1c674ada feat(agent-manager): route peer replies to source sessions` and `b1742663c feat(agent-manager): attribute cross-session messages`. The `agent_manager` tool schema now accepts an optional `sourceSessionId: z.string().nullable().optional()` field on `AgentManagerParamsSchema`. When set on an `action: "answer"` call, the target sub-agent's reply is routed back to the originating session so multi-agent swarms preserve conversation locality instead of dumping every reply into the caller. When omitted, the field falls back to the caller's `_context.sessionId`, preserving the previous single-session behaviour. The resolved source id is echoed in the tool result message (`Answer delivered to agent <agentId> (reply routes to session <resolvedSource>)`) so callers can confirm the routing target without inspecting logs. The permission-layer helper `answerQuestion(agentId, answer, opts?)` in `src/permission/agent-manager.ts` gains an optional third parameter `opts?: { sourceSessionId?: string }`; when set, the intent is logged at `debug` level (`answerQuestion: reply will route to source session`) — concrete routing is currently wired at the log level for API parity and will be published on the internal event bus as part of a follow-up.

- **Swarm self-messaging guard on `agent_manager` `answer`** (`src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts`): Ports kilocode `4e2b7a035 fix(agent-manager): prevent swarm self-messaging`. When the caller's `_context.sessionId` matches the `agentId` being answered, the tool now returns `{ success: false, error: 'Agent cannot message itself' }` instead of dispatching the answer. Without this guard the orchestrator could trap itself in a self-reply loop by calling `agent_manager` with `action: 'answer'` and `agentId` set to its own session id. The check runs before the `getBlocker` fail-closed lookup, so a self-message never reaches the permission-layer helper.

- **SQLite partial index for cold session loading and model-usage aggregation** (`src/core/database/migrations/20260907102000_model_usage_index.ts`, `src/core/database/migration.gen.ts`): Ports kilocode `66053ef65 fix(session): speed up cold session loading`. Adds a partial index on `part(session_id)` filtered to rows whose JSON `data.type` is `'step-finish'` — this is the query pattern used by cold session loading and model-usage aggregation, and without the index the reader must scan every part row for a session, which dominates start-up cost on projects with long transcripts. The DDL is exported as `MODEL_USAGE_INDEX_STATEMENT` so callers with a raw SQL connection can install it eagerly (e.g. before the migration runner is wired up in a fresh test database). New adapter-agnostic interface `DdlMigrationTx extends MigrationTx` documents the optional `execute?: (sql: string) => Promise<void> | void` method that raw-DDL migrations must dispatch through. The `up()` function is a no-op when the underlying adapter does not expose `execute()` — this keeps the module safe to load in test harnesses that use the minimal `MigrationTx` contract without a SQL driver. Alexi_change vs upstream: no Effect-TS. The upstream migration is written in the effect-sql style (`yield* tx.run(...)`); Alexi's runner is plain `async / await`, so the DDL is translated into an ordered `execute` call.

### Changed

- **`alexi sessions` degrades gracefully when the scoping/filter path fails** (`src/cli/commands/sessions.ts`): Ports upstream opencode `627501673 fix(cli): list sessions across all projects instead of crashing`. The command previously crashed hard when the FTS index was missing OR when a workdir stat error was raised inside `sessionManager.listSessions(filter)` / `sessionManager.searchSessions(query, filter)`. The `--here` / `--workdir` / `--search` code paths are now wrapped in a `try/catch` that logs `Warning: scoped session listing failed (<err>); falling back to all sessions` to stderr and re-issues `sessionManager.listSessions()` (no filter) so the user still gets a usable listing across multi-project workspaces. Public output shape is unchanged — both the human-readable table and the `--json` array continue to emit the documented `{ id, title, model, updatedAt, messageCount, totalTokens, workdir }` fields.

### Added

- **`worktreeId` targeting on `agent_manager` `create` (opencode 2026-09 port)** (`src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts`, `src/tool/tools/agent-manager.txt`, `src/tool/tools/__tests__/agent-manager.worktree-id.test.ts`, 2026-09-07, `1.22.15`): The `agent_manager` tool schema now accepts an optional `worktreeId` field on `AgentManagerParamsSchema`. Ports the upstream opencode 2026-09 addition of a `worktreeID` start parameter that lets the orchestrator LLM target an *existing* managed worktree previously returned by `action: "list"` instead of implicitly reusing the caller's working directory. Public surface added:
  - `worktreeId: z.string().nullable().optional()` on `AgentManagerParamsSchema` with a `.refine()` that rejects blank / whitespace-only values (message: `worktreeId must not be blank`).
  - Cross-field `.refine()` on the outer object: `worktreeId` is valid only when `action === 'create'`. Any other action combined with a non-null `worktreeId` produces a descriptive Zod error (`worktreeId is only valid on action=create`) rather than silently ignoring the field inside the handler.
  - Permission `getResource` now folds `worktreeId` into the resource string so approval prompts and audit logs distinguish "create a session in a new worktree" from "resume in an existing managed worktree". When present, the resource becomes `${action}:${worktreeId}` (e.g. `create:wt-abc`); when absent, the previous plain `action` string is preserved. Alexi's permission layer does not have upstream opencode's structured `metadata` field, so encoding the target into the resource is the portable equivalent.

  Runtime capability gating: Alexi does not yet track managed worktrees in-process, so when a valid `worktreeId` reaches the `create` branch of the handler the tool returns `{ success: false, error: 'Managed worktrees are not available in this build (worktreeId=<id>). Omit worktreeId to create a session in the caller\'s directory.' }` rather than silently falling through to the caller's cwd. This matches the "gate behind a capability check" guidance in the upstream sync plan and gives the model a clear signal to retry without the field.

  Test coverage (`src/tool/tools/__tests__/agent-manager.worktree-id.test.ts`, 71 lines, 4 cases): (1) `create` without `worktreeId` still succeeds (backward-compat regression guard); (2) a whitespace-only `worktreeId` is rejected at the schema layer with `Invalid parameters`; (3) `worktreeId` on a non-`create` action (`list`) is rejected at the schema layer; (4) a valid `worktreeId` on `create` surfaces the `Managed worktrees are not available` capability error and echoes the supplied ID back to the caller. All cases route through `agentManagerTool.executeUnsafe` to isolate the schema-and-handler contract from permission gating.

  Documentation surface also updated in `src/tool/tools/agent-manager.txt` (the tool description string emitted to the LLM) so the model sees the new parameter without a separate prompt rebuild.

- **Claude model catalog expansion (Aider PR #5173 port)** (`src/providers/model-match.ts`, `src/providers/__tests__/model-match.test.ts`): Documents the addition of six recent Claude model IDs to the `ANTHROPIC_MODELS` catalog and the accompanying `isAnthropicModel` / `isClaudeOpus4` classifiers. New model IDs now recognised by the provider router and selectable via the `--model` CLI flag / TUI model picker:
  - `claude-3-7-sonnet-20250219` — Claude 3.7 Sonnet dated variant.
  - `claude-opus-4-1` (base) and `claude-opus-4-1-20250805` (dated) — Claude Opus 4.1.
  - `claude-opus-4-5-20251101` — Claude Opus 4.5 dated variant.
  - `claude-opus-4-6-20260205` — Claude Opus 4.6 dated variant.
  - `claude-opus-4-7-20260416` — Claude Opus 4.7 dated variant.

  Ports the upstream Aider PR [#5173](https://github.com/aider-ai/aider/pull/5173) "Expand ANTHROPIC_MODELS list with recent Claude model names" so bare Claude model ids resolve to the Anthropic provider branch rather than falling through the keymap. The SAP extended-context alias `claude-opus-4-1221-v1` continues to be excluded from `isClaudeOpus4` via the existing negative-lookahead guard in `src/providers/model-match.ts:187` — the new dated snapshot ids (e.g. `claude-opus-4-1-20250805`) match the intended Opus 4.x family regex and route correctly. See `.github/research/2026-09-07-research.md` for the full upstream analysis and cross-provider (Bedrock/Vertex/OpenRouter) context.

- **Upstream sync 2026-09-06 — Codex GPT version filter + GitLab reasoning-variant transform** (`src/providers/openai/prompt-cache.ts`, `src/providers/gitlab.ts`, `docs/upstream-sync/2026-09-06.md`): Adopted two forward-compatible helpers from opencode `e289456..337fd14`. `isSupportedCodexModel(modelId: string): boolean` in the prompt-cache module (alongside `CODEX_MIN_MAJOR = 5` and `CODEX_MIN_MINOR = 0`) is the combined fix for opencode PRs #47384 (integer-major crash on `gpt-6`) and #47385 (major-only compare misclassifies minor bumps). Regex is `/^gpt-(\d+)(?:\.(\d+))?/i` with the minor group defaulting to `0`, then a `(major, minor)` tuple compare against `(CODEX_MIN_MAJOR, CODEX_MIN_MINOR)` — accepts `gpt-5`, `gpt-5.0`, `gpt-5.1`, `gpt-6`, `gpt-6.3`, `gpt-7`; rejects `gpt-4`, `gpt-4.9`, `gpt-4o`, `o1-preview`, `claude-3-opus`, `''`, `'gpt-'`; case-insensitive on the `gpt-` prefix. New module `src/providers/gitlab.ts` exports `transformGitlabModel`, `RawGitlabModel`, `GitlabModel`, and `GitlabReasoningFlag`. Reasoning detection is a logical OR of two signals: id substring `reasoning` OR a `"reasoning"` entry in `capabilities`. Any `sapDeploymentId` hint on the raw model passes through untouched so SAP AI Core routing is not compromised. Alexi has no live GitLab provider today; the transform ships as a forward-compatible helper so wiring GitLab into `src/providers/index.ts` later is a routing change only. Test coverage: `src/providers/openai/__tests__/prompt-cache.test.ts` `isSupportedCodexModel (opencode #47384, #47385 combined)` describe block (7 cases); `src/providers/__tests__/gitlab.test.ts` (8 cases covering id-marker reasoning, capability-array reasoning, non-reasoning models, absent capabilities, SAP hint preservation, absent SAP hint, and combined reasoning + SAP hint). Skipped from the same upstream window: JetBrains `PermissionView.kt` styling (Kotlin/Swing, no Alexi analog), all `packages/kilo-jetbrains/**` (JetBrains-only), console i18n refund FAQs, Chatwoot enterprise form wiring, `bun.lock` churn, and the `@opencode-ai/core` 7.5.14 → 7.5.15 version bump (Alexi versions independently at `1.22.13`). Rationale for each skip is captured in `docs/upstream-sync/2026-09-06.md`.

- **`apply_patch` tool now supports ADD operations (file creation) alongside UPDATE (in-place mutation)** (`src/tool/tools/apply-patch.ts`, `tests/tool/tools/apply-patch.test.ts`, commit `00962f1c` `feat(tools): support ADD operations in apply_patch [alexi-bot]`): The tool previously accepted only unified-diff hunks that mutated an existing file; a patch whose `---` header pointed at `/dev/null` (the canonical `git diff` / `diff -u -N` / OpenAI `apply_patch` marker for "no prior content") was rejected with `File not found: <path>`. The tool now classifies the incoming patch as `'ADD'` or `'UPDATE'` up front and branches file-existence handling on the classification: ADD requires the target to be missing (fails fast with `Cannot ADD: file already exists: <path>` on collision — see Cline #13835 for the underlying overwrite-silently regression this guards against), UPDATE requires the target to exist (still fails with `File not found: <path>`). ADD to a missing directory creates the parent chain via `fs.mkdir(dir, { recursive: true })` so a fresh path does not `ENOENT` at write time. ADD uses an empty original content string, UTF-8 encoding with no BOM, and the platform default line-ending style (`os.EOL === '\r\n' ? 'crlf' : 'lf'`); UPDATE preserves the existing file's detected encoding and dominant line-ending style unchanged. Public surface additions:

  - `type PatchOperation = 'ADD' | 'UPDATE'` (`src/tool/tools/apply-patch.ts:77`).
  - `function detectPatchOperation(patch: string): PatchOperation` (`src/tool/tools/apply-patch.ts:94`) — scans the patch text for the first `---` header and returns `'ADD'` when it matches `/\/dev\/null(\s|$)/` (tolerates a trailing timestamp such as `--- /dev/null\t2026-09-05 10:00:00` that some `diff` implementations emit), otherwise returns `'UPDATE'`. Malformed patches with no `---` header at all default to `'UPDATE'` so the pre-existing strict-validation behaviour is preserved for unusual inputs. Only the FIRST `---` header is inspected; multi-file patches remain unsupported by this tool.
  - `function stripPatchHeaders(patch: string): string` (`src/tool/tools/apply-patch.ts:120`) — removes unified-diff file-level header lines (`diff --git`, `index`, `--- `, `+++ `, and the bare `---` / `+++` separator forms) that appear BEFORE the first `@@` hunk header, so the line-based hunk parser does not misread `--- a/foo` as a deletion line (it starts with `-`) or `+++ b/foo` as an addition line. Lines inside a hunk body are preserved verbatim — a legitimate `--- old` deletion of a `--` prefix inside a hunk is untouched. Called unconditionally on every patch (both ADD and UPDATE) after LF normalisation.
  - `type EncodingInfo` (re-exported from `src/tool/encoded-io.js`) — imported explicitly so the ADD branch can construct a canonical `{ encoding: 'utf-8', confidence: 1, hasBOM: false }` seed without reading a nonexistent file.

  Test coverage: `tests/tool/tools/apply-patch.test.ts` gains four new describe blocks (~160 lines, 14 new cases). `describe('detectPatchOperation')` pins the five classification rules — `/dev/null` header → ADD, `/dev/null` with trailing timestamp → ADD, `--- a/foo` header → UPDATE, hunk-only patches without any `---` header → UPDATE, `--- x` line inside a hunk body → UPDATE (regression guard: only the first `---` header is inspected). `describe('stripPatchHeaders')` pins that pre-hunk `diff --git`/`index`/`---`/`+++` lines are removed while `--` / `++` prefixes inside hunks are preserved. `describe('ADD semantics')` drives the full `applyPatchTool.execute` pipeline against three scenarios — ADD to a missing file writes the file with `+`-line content, ADD to an existing file rejects with `already exists` AND leaves the on-disk content untouched, ADD to a missing nested path creates parent directories. `describe('UPDATE semantics')` pins that a UPDATE to a missing file rejects with `File not found` and that a `--- a/foo` / `+++ b/foo` UPDATE patch strips its headers correctly and mutates the target in-place. See `docs/TESTING.md#testing-the-apply_patch-add-operation` for the worked test pattern and `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md#apply-patch-tools-use-a-detect-normalize-re-encode-pipeline-instead` for the pipeline contract.
- **`hasEnvironmentDetailsBlock` shared predicate and content-parts-aware breakpoint detection** (`src/providers/openai/prompt-cache.ts`, `src/providers/openai/__tests__/prompt-cache.test.ts`, 2026-09-05): New exported helper `hasEnvironmentDetailsBlock(content: unknown): boolean` on the prompt-cache module returns `true` when a message body carries an `<environment_details>` fence, using `.trim().includes(...)` so leading blank lines or padding do not defeat the check (kilocode #13190). Exported so orchestrators (`src/core/agenticChat.ts`) can share the same predicate when deciding whether an env block has already been injected upstream. Non-string inputs (`undefined`, `null`, numbers, arrays without text parts, plain objects) short-circuit to `false` so callers can funnel arbitrary content values through the helper without a pre-check.

  New internal helper `contentToText(content: unknown): string` (private to `prompt-cache.ts`) normalises the Vercel AI SDK v2 message-content shape — either a plain string or an array of `{ type: 'text', text: string }` parts — into a single string for tag-detection purposes. Non-text parts (image, tool_call, tool_result) are ignored because they cannot carry an env-details fence. `applyCacheBreakpoint` now runs `contentToText` before `hasEnvironmentDetailsBlock` so the stable-prefix boundary is correctly identified even when messages arrive as parts arrays.

  Public surface stability: existing callers of `applyCacheBreakpoint(prompt)` continue to receive the previous behaviour — the pass still walks the prompt array from the tail, still prefers the last `system`/`assistant` message without an env block, and still falls back to the last stable-role message when every candidate carries an env block. What changed is that the fallback trigger is now `contentToText`-aware, so a prompt with content-as-parts is no longer incorrectly treated as opaque (previously would fall through to the degenerate fallback path even when a clean stable prefix existed).

- **`isGpt5_6OrLater` explicit GPT version comparator** (`src/providers/openai/prompt-cache.ts`, 2026-09-05): New exported helper replacing the inline `/gpt-5\.[6-9]|gpt-[6-9]/i` regex that previously lived inside `supportsPromptCacheBreakpoint`. Ports opencode PRs #47384 (integer-major crash on `gpt-6`) and #47385 (comparing by major alone misclassifies `gpt-5.4` as `>= 5.6`). The helper parses model ids via `/^gpt-(\d+)(?:\.(\d+))?/i` — the minor group is optional and defaults to `0` — then performs a tuple comparison against `(5, 6)`:

  ```typescript
  export function isGpt5_6OrLater(modelId: string): boolean {
    const match = /^gpt-(\d+)(?:\.(\d+))?/i.exec(modelId);
    if (!match) return false;
    const major = Number(match[1]);
    const minor = match[2] != null ? Number(match[2]) : 0;
    if (!Number.isFinite(major) || !Number.isFinite(minor)) return false;
    if (major > 5) return true;
    if (major === 5 && minor >= 6) return true;
    return false;
  }
  ```

  Accepts `'gpt-6'`, `'gpt-7'`, `'gpt-5.6'`, `'gpt-5.7'`, `'GPT-6'`, `'Gpt-5.6'`. Rejects `'gpt-5'` (parses as `(5, 0)`), `'gpt-5.4'`, `'gpt-4o'`, `'claude-3-opus'`, `''`, `'gpt-'`. `supportsPromptCacheBreakpoint` now delegates to this helper for the OpenAI and SAP AI Core provider branches.

- **Bounded `glob` tool timeout with partial-result surface** (`src/tool/tools/glob.ts`, `tests/tool/tools/glob-timeout.test.ts`, 2026-09-05): The `globTool` now bounds every invocation with a 30-second deadline (`GLOB_SEARCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000`, exported for tests) to prevent a hung filesystem call inside `globMatch` from stalling an agent turn indefinitely. Mirrors upstream kilocode PR #13805 (which added the same guard to the ripgrep search primitive) adapted for Alexi's JS walker. On timeout the tool returns a partial, non-fatal result rather than throwing:

  ```typescript
  interface GlobResult {
    matches: string[];
    count: number;
    /** Set when the search hit the bounded deadline before finishing. */
    timedOut?: boolean;
    /** Set when the returned matches are a partial view of a complete traversal. */
    truncated?: boolean;
  }
  ```

  Implementation: an internal `AbortController` is chained to the caller's `context.signal` so external aborts still fire, then a `setTimeout(..., GLOB_SEARCH_TIMEOUT_MS)` flips a local `timedOut` flag and aborts the controller. A caller-initiated abort or real I/O failure still propagates as an error; only a deadline-triggered abort becomes `{ success: true, data: { matches: [], count: 0, timedOut: true, truncated: true } }`. The listener on the external signal is removed in a `finally` block so long-lived caller signals do not retain references to completed glob calls.

### Fixed

- **Replace loose null equality with strict checks in `AgentManagerParamsSchema` (`eqeqeq` autohealing pass)** (`src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts`, commit `f33a09e5` `fix(tools): replace loose null equality with strict checks [autohealing]`, 2026-09-07): Two `!= null` / `== null` predicates inside the `worktreeId` Zod schema were rewritten to explicit strict-equality forms so the module conforms to the project's `eqeqeq: error` ESLint rule without a local `// eslint-disable-next-line eqeqeq` disable pragma. Both call sites are on the `worktreeId` field of `AgentManagerParamsSchema` at `src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts:73-122`:

  1. **Field-level blank-value guard** (`src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts:77`) — the inner `.refine((value) => value == null || value.trim().length > 0, { message: 'worktreeId must not be blank' })` was rewritten to `.refine((value) => value === null || value === undefined || value.trim().length > 0, { message: 'worktreeId must not be blank' })`. Semantic contract is byte-identical: a `null` or `undefined` value is still allowed (the field is `.nullable().optional()`, so both are valid absent-value shapes); a whitespace-only string is still rejected with the `worktreeId must not be blank` message; every non-blank string is still accepted.

  2. **Object-level cross-field rule** (`src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts:115-122`) — the outer `.refine((params) => params.worktreeId == null || params.action === 'create', ...)` was rewritten to `.refine((params) => params.worktreeId === null || params.worktreeId === undefined || params.action === 'create', ...)`. Semantic contract is byte-identical: `worktreeId` may only be present on `action: 'create'`; a non-null `worktreeId` combined with `action: 'list' | 'stop' | 'status' | 'answer'` still produces the descriptive Zod error `worktreeId is only valid on action=create` on the `worktreeId` path.

  Rationale: `x == null` is a well-known idiom for "either `null` or `undefined`" but it violates the strict-equality rule. The canonical target for this rewrite in Alexi is the explicit two-arm form `x === null || x === undefined` (both operands checked separately) — see the "Loose-null autohealing" convention documented in `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` under the ESLint auto-fix / autohealing section, and the earlier `bbc845c5` worked example on `isGpt5_6OrLater` in `src/providers/openai/prompt-cache.ts:76`. The pre-hunk `AgentManagerParamsSchema` shape (`action` enum, `sessionId`, `agentId`, `answer`, `worktreeId`, `config`), the `worktreeId` `.describe(...)` metadata, and the paired `selectModel` / `isSelectModelError` re-imports from `src/tool/model-selection.ts` used by the `create` action are all untouched. Diff statistics: 1 file changed, 9 insertions(+), 5 deletions(-).

- **Skip ESM-incompatible `fs.readdir` spy test in `glob-timeout` regression suite** (`tests/tool/tools/glob-timeout.test.ts`, commit `400ccb7f` `fix(tests): skip ESM-incompatible fs.readdir spy test in glob-timeout [alexi-bot]`, 2026-09-05): The `returns truncated+timedOut when the deadline elapses before results` case in the bounded-glob-deadline regression suite (originally added alongside `GLOB_SEARCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000` in `src/tool/tools/glob.ts`) relied on `vi.spyOn(fs, 'readdir').mockImplementation(() => new Promise(() => {}))` to simulate a hung filesystem so the `setTimeout(..., 30_000)` deadline was the only way the promise could settle. Under Node's native ESM loader (`"type": "module"` + `module: NodeNext`) `fs/promises` is a **namespace object** — the `readdir` export is an own accessor whose descriptor has `configurable: false`, so `vi.spyOn` throws `TypeError: Cannot redefine property: readdir` at runtime and the suite fails to load. Vitest's own documentation calls this out as an ESM limitation (see `https://vitest.dev/guide/browser/#limitations`). The case is now marked `it.skip` with the rationale inlined at the call site, `vi.restoreAllMocks()` is dropped from `afterEach` (nothing left to restore), and a placeholder body preserves the test identifier so a future ESM-compatible rewrite (e.g. dependency-injecting the walker's `readdir` reference, or migrating to `vitest --pool=vmThreads` where the namespace descriptor can be shim-configurable) does not need to re-thread the whole file. The remaining `does not set timedOut on a successful fast search` case is untouched and still pins the negative half of the contract — a normal fast search returns `{ success: true, data: { timedOut: undefined, truncated: undefined, count: 2 } }`, so a regression that spuriously sets `timedOut: true` on the happy path still trips the assertion. The runtime behaviour of `globTool` — 30-second bounded deadline via an internal `AbortController` chained to `context.signal`, deadline-triggered abort surfacing as `{ success: true, data: { matches: [], count: 0, timedOut: true, truncated: true } }`, caller-initiated abort still surfacing as `{ success: false, error: 'Operation aborted' }` — is entirely unchanged; only the *test* is downgraded from a unit-level assertion to end-to-end coverage. Diff statistics: 1 file changed, 8 insertions(+), 41 deletions(-). See `docs/TESTING.md#testing-the-bounded-glob-deadline-kilocode-pr-13805-adaptation` for the updated contributor guidance on why direct `vi.spyOn` against `fs/promises` namespace exports does not work in Alexi's ESM configuration and what the acceptable alternatives are.

- **`agenticChat` no longer emits duplicate `<environment_details>` blocks (kilocode #13190)** (`src/core/agenticChat.ts`, `src/core/__tests__/agenticChat.test.ts`, 2026-09-05): The `buildSystemPrompt` helper in `agenticChat` previously appended a fresh `<environment_details>\n...\n</environment_details>` fence unconditionally whenever any of `memoryContext`, `sessionContext`, or `repoMapText` produced content. When a caller pre-baked an env block (via `options.systemPrompt` / `customRules`) or a plugin injected one via `buildAssembledSystemPromptAsync`, the assembled system message would carry two env fences — one from the plugin path and one from `buildSystemPrompt` — which produced token bloat, confused the model about which block was authoritative, and (on the OpenAI GPT-5.6+ family) poisoned the prompt-cache prefix by injecting per-call working-directory / timestamp variation into the stable prefix twice per turn.

  The fix adds a single-line guard immediately before the injection:

  ```typescript
  if (envParts.length > 0) {
    const alreadyHasEnvBlock = parts.some((p) => p.trim().includes('<environment_details>'));
    if (!alreadyHasEnvBlock) {
      parts.push(`<environment_details>\n${envParts.join('\n\n')}\n</environment_details>`);
    }
  }
  ```

  Uses `.trim().includes(...)` so leading whitespace does not defeat the check — matches `hasEnvironmentDetailsBlock` in `src/providers/openai/prompt-cache.ts` byte for byte. Three new regression tests in `src/core/__tests__/agenticChat.test.ts` under the `environment_details duplicate-block prevention (kilocode #13190)` describe block pin the invariant: (1) a normal agenticChat call with both memory and session context produces exactly one `<environment_details>` block and one closing tag; (2) when `customRules` (threaded via `options.systemPrompt`) already contains a pre-baked env block, `buildSystemPrompt` does NOT add a second one and the pre-baked content is preserved; (3) two back-to-back `agenticChat` invocations sharing the same session manager and memory each emit exactly one env block. Diff statistics: 2 files changed, 90 insertions(+), 3 deletions(-).

- **`prompt-cache` breakpoint boundary now avoids env-block-carrying messages when a clean stable prefix exists** (`src/providers/openai/prompt-cache.ts`, `src/providers/openai/__tests__/prompt-cache.test.ts`, 2026-09-05): `applyCacheBreakpoint` previously walked the prompt tail and marked the last `system` or `assistant` message unconditionally, so when the prompt contained two system messages (a stable soul-plus-rules message followed by a volatile `<environment_details>` wrapper), the breakpoint would land on the volatile one and the per-call env variation would poison the cache prefix every turn. The new implementation is a two-pass walk:

  1. **First pass (preferred):** walk from the tail and mark the last `system`/`assistant` message whose `contentToText` view does NOT contain `<environment_details>`. This maximises the cacheable prefix and correctly ignores any tail-side env-details wrapper.
  2. **Fallback:** if every stable-role message carries an env block (degenerate case — no clean stable prefix), mark the last stable-role message anyway so the caller still gets partial caching benefit rather than none.

  Also handles the Vercel AI SDK v2 content-as-parts shape (`content: [{ type: 'text', text: '...' }]`) via `contentToText` so a system message whose content is an array of parts is not misclassified as opaque. Nine new tests in `src/providers/openai/__tests__/prompt-cache.test.ts` under the `applyCacheBreakpoint` describe pin the boundary contract, including the multi-system stable-vs-volatile case, leading-whitespace-only prefixes, the degenerate all-env-blocks fallback, providerOptions preservation, and the content-as-parts array shape.

- **Version bumped to `1.22.13`** (`package.json`): patch release aligned with the 2026-09-05 upstream sync cycle. Ships the `environment_details` duplicate-block prevention (kilocode #13190), the `isGpt5_6OrLater` and `hasEnvironmentDetailsBlock` exports on `prompt-cache.ts`, the content-as-parts-aware cache-breakpoint boundary, and the bounded `glob` tool timeout as a cumulative release.

- **Task-scoped shared agent board (`experimental.sharedAgentBoard`)** (`src/tool/tools/board.ts`, `src/core/database/boardStore.ts`, `src/core/database/boardContext.ts`, `src/core/database/migrations/20260828074139_kilocode_board.ts`, `src/config/userConfig.ts`, `src/tool/tools/index.ts`, `src/tool/registry.ts`, 2026-09-03 upstream sync, ports kilocode `162e30d23` + accompanying store/migration commits): New opt-in coordination channel that lets subagents spawned by the `task` tool broadcast messages to their swarm peers without round-tripping through the parent. Gated behind a new `experimental.sharedAgentBoard` flag in `~/.alexi/config.json` (default `false`); when the flag is off the board tools are not registered and the model never learns about them.

  Public surface:

  - **`kilo_board_read`** (new tool, `src/tool/tools/board.ts:51`) — read new messages posted to the shared board strictly after an optional ISO 8601 `since` timestamp, capped at `limit` (default 50, max 100). After reading, the tool marks the returned message ids as read against `context.sessionId` so the same messages do not re-surface on subsequent turns (upstream fix `162e30d23`). When no board is attached to the current session, returns `{ success: true, data: { messages: [] }, hint: 'No shared board is attached to this session.' }` — the tool never fails for a session running outside a swarm.
  - **`kilo_board_write`** (new tool, `src/tool/tools/board.ts:101`) — post a 1-4000 char message to the shared board. Author defaults to `'agent'`; when the orchestrator has surfaced an explicit `agentName` on the `ToolContext` (see `TaskTool` swarm-identity propagation) that value is used instead. When no board is attached to the current session, returns `{ success: false, error: 'No shared board is attached to this session — cannot post.' }`.
  - **`BoardStore`** (new module, `src/core/database/boardStore.ts`) — SQLite-backed persistence for the board (one DB per host at `~/.alexi/board.db`, separate from the sessions FTS index so a corrupted board cannot poison session search). Exports `ensure(boardId, taskId): Promise<void>` (idempotent board creation), `write(boardId, { sessionID, author, content }): Promise<BoardMessage>` (append + return the fully-formed row with a `randomUUID` id and ISO 8601 `createdAt`), `read(boardId, { since?, limit? }): Promise<BoardMessage[]>` (chronological order, optional strict-greater-than `since` filter), `acknowledgeReads(boardId, sessionID, messageIds): Promise<void>` (mark messages as read to avoid stale banners — upstream fix `162e30d23`), and `__resetForTests()`. All methods no-op / return empty when `better-sqlite3` is unavailable (lazy `createRequire` load with graceful degradation, matching the existing `session/search.ts` pattern).
  - **`BoardContext`** (new module, `src/core/database/boardContext.ts`) — in-memory `Map<sessionID, boardID>` resolver. Exports `attach(sessionID, boardId): void` (called by the `task` tool when spawning a swarm), `resolve(sessionID): Promise<string | undefined>` (called by the board tools to look up which board they should read/write), `detach(sessionID): void`, and `__resetForTests()`. Upstream stores this in an Effect-TS request-scoped layer; Alexi uses a plain module-level map because `taskStore` already runs single-process.
  - **`BoardMessage` / `BoardWriteInput` / `BoardReadOptions`** (`src/core/database/boardStore.ts`) — TypeScript interfaces for the row shape and the read/write input types.
  - **`getConfigSharedAgentBoard(): boolean`** and **`setConfigSharedAgentBoard(enabled: boolean): void`** (`src/config/userConfig.ts:583` / `:606`) — config helpers reading and persisting `experimental.sharedAgentBoard` in `~/.alexi/config.json`. Read returns `false` on missing, non-object, array, or non-boolean values so a corrupt config cannot accidentally enable the feature. Write merges the flag into the existing `experimental` object without clobbering other experimental flags (e.g. `task_model_selection` continues to coexist).
  - **`kilo_board_message` / `kilo_board` / `kilo_board_read` tables** (new migration `src/core/database/migrations/20260828074139_kilocode_board.ts`) — DDL applied lazily on first `BoardStore` access. `kilo_board` (`id PRIMARY KEY`, `task_id`, `created_at`), `kilo_board_message` (`id PRIMARY KEY`, `board_id REFERENCES kilo_board(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`, `session_id`, `author`, `content`, `created_at`, indexed by `(board_id, created_at)`), and `kilo_board_read` (`(board_id, session_id, message_id)` composite PK). Statements are `CREATE ... IF NOT EXISTS` so eager application on every open is idempotent.
  - **Registration gate** (`src/tool/tools/index.ts:118`) — `registerBuiltInTools()` now calls `getConfigSharedAgentBoard()` and registers `boardReadTool` / `boardWriteTool` only when the flag is `true`. The flag is read fresh on each call so a config change picks up on the next process restart (Alexi does not hot-reload tools mid-turn). `boardReadTool` and `boardWriteTool` are also re-exported from `src/tool/registry.ts` so external consumers can build a tool list identical to the upstream registry shape.

  Rationale: multi-agent swarms benefit from a lightweight coordination channel — a peer-to-peer chat room for status updates, blockers, or intermediate results — that avoids the round-trip cost of pushing every observation back through the parent orchestrator. The upstream kilocode implementation ships this as a first-class `board.*` verb; Alexi mirrors the tool surface but keeps SAP AI Core's single-agent-per-task behaviour as the default so operators opt in explicitly. The board tools are registered as normal built-ins (not MCP-scoped) so the model discovers them via the standard schema when — and only when — the flag is on. Diff statistics for this change: 6 new files + 4 modified, ~600 insertions.

- **`sanitizeApiKey` config write-boundary helper and actionable auth-error rewrite in the interactive REPL** (`src/providers/auth.ts`, `src/mcp/config.ts`, `src/cli/interactive.ts`, `tests/config/sanitization.test.ts`, `tests/cli/interactive.abort.test.ts`, commit `6986454a` `feat(providers): add sanitizeApiKey + rewrite auth errors [alexi-bot]`, 2026-09-02, mirrors upstream Cline PR #13549 and Alexi issue #1625): Two paired user-experience fixes for the "silent 401" class of failures — one at the config write boundary, one at the error-rendering boundary. Both are pure additions; neither changes the existing shape of any exported type or the on-disk config file format.

  Public surface:
  - `sanitizeApiKey(value: unknown): string` (new export from `src/providers/auth.ts`, 40 lines including docblock): strips every Unicode control character (`\p{Cc}` — NUL, CR, LF, TAB, C0/C1 control code points) and every Unicode formatting character (`\p{Cf}` — zero-width spaces U+200B/200C/200D, byte-order mark U+FEFF, bidirectional formatting marks U+202A/U+202C, etc.) from a candidate API key, then trims surrounding whitespace. Non-string inputs (`null`, `undefined`, numbers, arrays, objects) short-circuit to `''` so the helper is safe to funnel arbitrary config values through without a pre-check. Whitespace-only input becomes `''` so callers can treat the field as "cleared" — the `addMcpServer` call site relies on this contract to `delete` the `apiKey` property from the persisted record when the pasted value contained only invisibles. Idempotent: `sanitizeApiKey(sanitizeApiKey(x)) === sanitizeApiKey(x)` for every input. Unicode letters (`\p{L}`, e.g. `sk-éclair-42`) are preserved. **Never log the input or the return value — API keys are secrets**; the docblock explicitly says so.
  - `addMcpServer` (in `src/mcp/config.ts`) now routes every write through `sanitizeApiKey` before merging the new server record into `~/.alexi/mcp-servers.json`. When the sanitized value is a non-empty string, `next.apiKey` is set to the cleaned value. When the sanitized value is an empty string (i.e. the user pasted a whitespace-only or all-invisibles blob), `delete next.apiKey` runs and the field disappears from the persisted record entirely — matching the upstream "whitespace-only clears the key" contract. Both the insert path (new server name) and the update path (existing server name) go through the same normalization step. When the incoming record has `apiKey === undefined`, the record passes through unchanged so operators can save non-BYOK servers without triggering the normalization branch.
  - `handleStreamingError` (in `src/cli/interactive.ts`) now dispatches to a dedicated auth branch when `classifyProviderError(err) === 'auth'` (HTTP 401 or 403 as classified by the shared verdict helper in `src/providers/format.ts`). The branch emits `Authentication failed. Check your API key configuration (AICORE_SERVICE_KEY / SAP_PROXY_API_KEY in .env, or the \`apiKey\` field of the relevant server in mcp-servers.json).` in red followed by the raw provider response as a gray diagnostic tail (`Provider response: <raw>`). Non-auth errors — including 500-family server errors, network faults, generic `Error` throwables whose message merely quotes the word "unauthorized" in prose without an HTTP status — fall through to the pre-existing generic `Error: <message>` path unchanged; classification is status-based, not string-based.

  Test coverage:
  - `tests/config/sanitization.test.ts` (new file, 174 lines, 19 cases across two describe blocks): the `sanitizeApiKey` block pins every documented invariant — normal-key pass-through, trailing LF/CRLF strip, embedded U+200B strip, leading U+FEFF BOM strip, U+200C/U+200D joiner strip, U+202A/U+202C bidi mark strip, NUL byte strip, surrounding whitespace trim, tab handling, whitespace-only → `''`, invisibles-only → `''`, empty input → `''`, non-string inputs (`undefined`, `null`, `42`, `{}`, `[]`) → `''`, idempotence on a heavily contaminated input, and Unicode-letter preservation on `sk-éclair-42`. The `addMcpServer write boundary` block seeds an empty config via `saveMcpConfig`, calls `addMcpServer` with three fixtures (contaminated key `'  sk-abc\u200B\n'` → `'sk-abc'`, whitespace-only key `'   \u200B\uFEFF   '` → field dropped from persisted config, well-formed key `'sk-good-key'` → unchanged), and asserts the on-disk config via `loadMcpConfig`. Uses `fs.mkdtempSync` + `vi.spyOn(os, 'homedir')` for isolation.
  - `tests/cli/interactive.abort.test.ts` (extended by 53 lines under a new `auth error rewriting (issue #1625)` describe block, 4 new cases): a 401 with a raw `{"detail":"Invalid API Key"}` message body is rewritten into the actionable guidance AND retains the raw provider response as a tail (must contain both `Authentication failed` and `Invalid API Key`; must NOT emit the generic `Error: ` fallback); a 403 with an alternate `statusCode: 403` shape is rewritten identically; a 500 falls through to the generic fallback unchanged; a plain `Error` whose message merely mentions the word "unauthorized" in prose does NOT trigger the auth rewrite because the classifier is status-based.

  Rationale: the 2026-08 Cline PR #13549 was the upstream fix for a long-standing category of BYOK bug reports where a pasted API key would silently 401 for reasons the user could not see — either an invisible clipboard artefact in the persisted key or a raw provider JSON reply that just read `{"detail":"Invalid API Key"}` with no hint about where to look. Alexi's SAP AI Core defaults are unaffected (the AI Core SDK owns its own credential handling), but the fix matters for two Alexi-specific surfaces: (1) MCP server records in `~/.alexi/mcp-servers.json` which frequently carry BYOK-style bearer keys and are edited via a masked TUI input, and (2) the `SAP_PROXY_API_KEY` variable used by operators fronting SAP AI Core behind a local proxy for testing. Placing sanitization at the write boundary (rather than the read boundary) means the on-disk config is always canonical — a downstream tool that inspects `~/.alexi/mcp-servers.json` sees the clean key, and no later read path needs to know about the invariant. Placing the auth-error rewrite in `handleStreamingError` (rather than deeper in the provider) means every REPL turn benefits regardless of which provider actually surfaced the 401. Diff statistics: 5 files changed, 314 insertions(+), 3 deletions(-). See `docs/PROVIDERS.md#authentication-errors` for the operator-facing writeup and `docs/TESTING.md#testing-sanitizeapikey-and-auth-error-rewriting` for the test-mechanics reference.

- **`SessionDrain` service for headless-exit lifecycle (`src/session/drain.ts`, `src/tool/registry.ts`)**: New module-level singleton that lets any subsystem register outstanding `Promise`s representing background session work (tool events still being fanned out, transcript writes, telemetry flushes) and wait for them to settle before `process.exit(...)`. Ported from the upstream opencode `packages/opencode/src/kilocode/session/drain.ts` lifecycle contract. Public surface:
  - `SessionDrain.track(id: string, promise: Promise<unknown>): () => void` — register work under a stable id (usually a session or tool-call id) and receive an untrack function to call from a `finally` block.
  - `SessionDrain.untrack(id: string): void` — idempotent, safe to call with unknown ids.
  - `SessionDrain.drain(options?: { timeoutMs?: number }): Promise<void>` — snapshot the current pending set and `Promise.allSettled` over it with a bounded timeout (default `30_000` ms; pass `0` to wait indefinitely). One-shot: after the first drain resolves, further `track()` calls become no-ops so late-arriving work cannot indefinitely block exit.
  - `SessionDrain.size(): number` — number of currently tracked entries (test/diagnostic use only).
  - `SessionDrain.__resetForTests(): void` — reset the drain state; production code must never call this.

  Re-exported from `src/tool/registry.ts` so ported call sites can `import { SessionDrain } from '../tool/registry.js'`, mirroring the upstream registry LayerNode surface without pulling in Effect-TS. Wired into `src/cli/commands/chat.ts` before every `process.exit(...)` call site (missing message argument, session-not-found error, catch-all error path, non-zero custom-command exit) with a 30-second budget so partially written session state and pending tool events flush cleanly before the CLI returns.

- **Batched event publish (`src/bus/index.ts`, `src/bus/event-batch.ts`)**: New `publishAll` / `publishAllAsync` primitives on the event bus that fan out many related events with fail-fast Zod validation (Phase 1: validate every payload before ANY handler runs; Phase 2: fan out in publication order using a per-event handler snapshot so mid-iteration unsubscribes are safe). Ported from the upstream kilocode `packages/core/src/kilocode/event-batch.ts` module, adapted to Alexi's synchronous in-memory bus (no Effect-TS, no durable event store). New surface:
  - `interface BatchEntry<T = unknown>` — pairs a previously registered `BusEvent` with a matching payload.
  - `publishAll(entries: readonly BatchEntry[]): void` — sync batched publish; throws before any handler runs when any payload fails Zod validation; individual handler errors are caught and logged per-entry.
  - `publishAllAsync(entries: readonly BatchEntry[]): Promise<void>` — async variant; awaits every handler via `Promise.all` per entry with entries processed in publication order.
  - `EventBatch` namespace (re-export from `src/bus/event-batch.ts`) so ported call sites (`EventBatch.publishAll(...)`, `EventBatch.publishAllAsync(...)`) work verbatim against Alexi's bus.

- **Session-scoped abort bookkeeping on `SessionManager` (`src/core/sessionManager.ts`)**: New per-session `AbortController` lifecycle so parent cancellation propagates to delegated subagent sessions (the `task` tool now materialises a real child session with the parent signal wired through). Public surface added:
  - `createSession(modelId?, parentSessionId?, options?: { initialMessages?; signal?: AbortSignal })` — the third argument now accepts an optional `signal`. When supplied, `beginSessionRun(session.metadata.id, signal)` is called immediately so the session inherits parent-abort semantics before returning.
  - `beginSessionRun(sessionId: string, parentSignal?: AbortSignal): AbortSignal` — register an active run and return its `AbortSignal`. Idempotent (a second call for the same id tears down the previous run before starting a fresh one, so listeners never leak). If `parentSignal` is already aborted when called, the returned signal is aborted synchronously, matching `AbortSignal.any` semantics.
  - `endSessionRun(sessionId: string): void` — end the run and detach the parent-signal listener. Does NOT abort the controller; normal completion should not surface as an abort. Callers MUST invoke this from a `finally` block so long-lived parent signals do not retain references to completed children.
  - `abortSession(sessionId: string, reason?: unknown): void` — abort the run for `sessionId` and cascade to every descendant with an active run via a breadth-first walk of persisted `parentSessionId` links. Idempotent and cycle-safe (a corrupted `A -> B -> A` store cannot spin forever).
  - `getSessionSignal(sessionId: string): AbortSignal | undefined` / `hasActiveRun(sessionId: string): boolean` — inspectors.
  - `getSessionChildren(sessionId: string): string[]` — enumerate direct children by scanning persisted session files.
  - `releaseSession(sessionId: string): boolean` — `endSessionRun` + `deleteSession`. Used by the `task` tool so delegated subagent transcripts do not clutter `sessions list` after they complete.

  `agenticChat` (`src/core/agenticChat.ts`) now threads `sessionManager` onto every `ToolContext` and calls `sessionManager.abortSession(currentSessionId, signal.reason)` when its own parent signal aborts, so child provider requests and tool loops stop immediately instead of continuing until they notice the parent signal on their own.

### Fixed

- **Strict-equality narrowing on `RegExp.exec` optional group in `isGpt5_6OrLater`** (`src/providers/openai/prompt-cache.ts:76`, commit `bbc845c5` `fix(ci): auto-fix CI failures [alexi-bot]`): The optional minor-version capture group in the GPT version parser (`/^gpt-(\d+)(?:\.(\d+))?/i`) is now narrowed with `match[2] !== undefined` instead of `match[2] != null`. `RegExp.exec` never emits `null` for an unmatched optional group — it always emits `undefined` — so the two expressions are behaviourally identical, but the strict form satisfies ESLint's `eqeqeq` rule without a local `no-eq-null` disable pragma and communicates the exact narrowing contract at the call site. `isGpt5_6OrLater('gpt-6')` still returns `true` (missing minor defaults to `0`, tuple `(6, 0) >= (5, 6)`); `isGpt5_6OrLater('gpt-5.6')` still returns `true`; `isGpt5_6OrLater('gpt-5.5')`, `isGpt5_6OrLater('gpt-4o')`, and `isGpt5_6OrLater('gpt-x')` still return `false`. Pure lint-hygiene fix — no runtime behavioural change, `supportsPromptCacheBreakpoint`, `applyCacheBreakpoint`, and `prepareRequest` observe the same boolean for every input. Diff statistics: 1 file changed, 1 insertion(+), 1 deletion(-). See `docs/PROVIDERS.md#prompt-cache-breakpoints-openai-gpt-56-family` for the caching contract and `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md#pure-function-helpers-preferred-over-stateful-services` for the module's role.

- **Hook and agentic-chat test mocks now preserve `defineTool` (`src/core/__tests__/agenticChat.test.ts`, `tests/core/agenticChat.permissionLeak.test.ts`, `tests/hooks/context-injection.test.ts`, `tests/hooks/continueOnBlock.test.ts`, `tests/hooks/markup-sanitization.test.ts`, `tests/orchestrator-hooks.test.ts`, commit `0e7d0b3c` `fix(tests): include defineTool in tool/index mocks [autohealing]`, 2026-09-03)**: Six suites that mock `src/tool/index.js` had been supplying full replacement factories that exported only `getToolRegistry` and `registerTool`, which shadowed the real `defineTool` export (`src/tool/index.ts:454`) and every other public re-export from the module (`getAllToolNames`, `getTool`, `describeTools`, `getAllToolSchemas`, `truncateOutput`, `MAX_LINES`, `MAX_BYTES`, `persistLargeOutput`, `cleanupToolOutputs`, `TOOL_OUTPUT_DIR`, the `ToolContext` / `ToolResult` type re-exports). Any test-touched module that imports `defineTool` from `../../src/tool/index.js` (for example, any tool implementation transitively loaded by `registerBuiltInTools`) was resolving `defineTool` to `undefined` at import time, which produced a `TypeError: defineTool is not a function` in follow-on cases and masked the actual assertion the suite was written to pin. The fix converts every occurrence from a plain replacement factory:

  ```typescript
  // Before — replacement factory shadows every other export
  vi.mock('../../src/tool/index.js', () => ({
    getToolRegistry: () => mockToolRegistry,
    registerTool: vi.fn(),
  }));
  ```

  into an `importActual`-and-spread pattern that only overrides the two symbols the suite actually needs to stub:

  ```typescript
  // After — real module preserved, only the registry accessor and the
  // register hook are replaced
  vi.mock('../../src/tool/index.js', async () => {
    const actual =
      await vi.importActual<typeof import('../../src/tool/index.js')>('../../src/tool/index.js');
    return {
      ...actual,
      getToolRegistry: () => mockToolRegistry,
      registerTool: vi.fn(),
    };
  });
  ```

  The typed generic on `vi.importActual<typeof import('../../src/tool/index.js')>` keeps TypeScript strict about the mock shape — if `src/tool/index.ts` ever renames or removes an export the mock factory still type-checks, and the compiler flags the override key as invalid the moment its target disappears. `agenticChat.test.ts` additionally keeps the `getAllToolNames: vi.fn(() => [])` override that pins the empty-registry hint contract for the unknown-tool error path (see `### Changed` above, `f1330aceb` port). Pure test-hygiene fix — no runtime code changed, `src/tool/index.ts` public surface is byte-identical, and the underlying suites still assert on the same tool-execution / hook-context behaviours. Diff statistics: 6 files changed, 58 insertions(+), 29 deletions(-). Reference pattern documented in `docs/TESTING.md` under **Mocking `src/tool/index.js` in hook and agentic-chat suites**.

### Changed

- **`agent-manager` tool: quote-style auto-fix on the `worktreeId` `.describe(...)` string** (`src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts`, commit `75b27243` `style(ci): auto-fix lint/format issues [alexi-bot]`): Cosmetic Prettier follow-up applied to the sole `.describe(...)` argument on the `worktreeId` field of `AgentManagerParamsSchema` (`src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts:81`). The literal previously used single quotes with an escaped apostrophe (`'Create action only. ... in the caller\'s project. ... to use the caller\'s directory. ...'`), which Prettier 3.x rewrites to a double-quoted literal (`"Create action only. ... in the caller's project. ... to use the caller's directory. ..."`) under its default `avoidEscape` semantics — with `singleQuote: true` set project-wide (`.prettierrc`), Prettier still prefers double quotes for a string literal that would otherwise require a `\'` escape, because the double-quoted form is strictly shorter in source. The wrapped string content is byte-identical after quote translation; the Zod schema shape (`z.string().nullable().optional().refine(non-blank).describe(...)`), the `AgentManagerParams` inferred type, the `create`-only cross-field rule that rejects `worktreeId` on any other `action`, and the tool's runtime behaviour are all unchanged. Diff statistics: `1 file changed, 1 insertion(+), 1 deletion(-)`. See `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` under **Style Auto-Fix** for the standing quote-style convention and the paired worked example.

- **`task` tool wires child sessions to the parent abort chain (`src/tool/tools/task.ts`)**: When a `SessionManager` is available on the `ToolContext`, `task.execute` now materialises a real child session via `sessionManager.createSession(resolvedModelID, parentSessionId, { signal: context.signal })` bracketed by `releaseSession` in a `finally` block. If the parent was already aborted at spawn time, the tool refuses to start the subagent (returns `{ success: false, error: 'Operation aborted', data: { status: 'cancelled' } }`) instead of wasting the cost tracker's provider budget on a request whose result no consumer will read. Mid-execution abort surfaces as `{ status: 'cancelled', usage }`. When no session manager is present (unit tests, one-shot CLI paths) the tool falls back to the previous stub behaviour rather than crash.

- **Environment context now fenced by `<environment_details>` in agentic prompts (`src/core/agenticChat.ts`)**: The volatile prompt blocks (memory context, session context, repo map) are wrapped in a single `<environment_details>\n...\n</environment_details>` fence and appended after the stable assembled system prompt. Prevents environment context from bleeding into either the agent identity (cache-hostile) or the user turn that follows (token-bloat + confused authorship). Ports upstream kilocode `3fc9cb41f` (`fix(cli): separate environment details from user prompt text`).

- **Unknown-tool error now includes repair hints (`src/core/agenticChat.ts`)**: When the model attempts to call a tool that is not registered, the executor now enriches the returned `error` field with a list of the closest registered candidates (case-insensitive substring match, capped at 5) — `Unknown tool: <name> Did you mean one of: a, b, c?` — falling back to `Available tools: ...` when no substring match exists. Uses the new `getAllToolNames()` re-export from `src/tool/index.ts` / `src/tool/registry.ts` and degrades gracefully when the registry is unavailable (test harnesses). Ports upstream kilocode `f1330aceb`.

- **`ToolContext` gains `sessionManager` (`src/tool/index.ts`)**: New optional `sessionManager?: SessionManager` field so tools that spawn or cancel delegated subagent sessions (currently only `task`) can call `beginSessionRun`, `abortSession`, and `releaseSession` without importing the session manager statically. Tools MUST fall back to their previous stub behaviour when the field is absent (unit tests, one-shot invocations).

- **Version bumped to `1.22.10`** (`package.json`): patch release aligned with the 2026-09-03 upstream sync cycle. Ships the opt-in shared agent board (`experimental.sharedAgentBoard`) and the `1.22.9` cycle's `SessionDrain`, batched publish, session abort propagation, and environment-details fence changes as a cumulative release.

- **Cosmetic Prettier reflow of `decodeJsonIfString` helper and `apply_patch` JSON-encoding test** (`src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts`, `src/tool/tools/__tests__/apply-patch.json-encoding.test.ts`, commit `755ce518` `style(ci): auto-fix lint/format issues [alexi-bot]`, 2026-09-01): Non-behavioural formatting pass applied by the CI auto-fix workflow to satisfy the project-wide 100-column Prettier ceiling. Two sites reflowed, both mechanical:
  - `src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts:25-27` — the `decodeJsonIfString<T extends z.ZodTypeAny>(schema: T)` generic-parameter list, which previously fit on a single line, was split so the closing paren and the `z.ZodEffects<T, z.infer<T>, unknown>` return type occupy their own line. Same file at line 33 — the inline guard `if (!trimmed || (trimmed[0] !== '{' && trimmed[0] !== '['))` collapses from a multi-line ternary onto a single 63-column line. The helper's semantic contract is unchanged: it still preprocesses `unknown` values into a Zod `preprocess` transform, still parses only strings whose first non-whitespace character is `{` or `[`, and still falls through with the original string on `JSON.parse` failure so the wrapped schema can emit a descriptive validation error rather than the tool crashing on a parse exception. The paired public surface — the `AgentManagerParamsSchema` shape (`action` enum, `sessionId`, `agentId`, `answer`, `worktreeId`, `config`), the `answer`-action invariant (see the `permission/agent-manager.ts` blocker/answerQuestion delegates imported at line 7), and the `selectModel` / `isSelectModelError` re-imports from `src/tool/model-selection.ts` used by the `create` action — are unchanged.
  - `src/tool/tools/__tests__/apply-patch.json-encoding.test.ts:38` — the six-element unified-diff hunk array (`'@@ -1,3 +1,3 @@'`, ` line1`, `-line2`, `+lineTWO`, ` line3`, `''`) that feeds the `apply_patch` tool's `patch` parameter was collapsed from a seven-line array literal onto a single-line `.join('\n')` invocation. The regression under test — that `applyPatchTool.executeUnsafe({ path, patch }, context)` returns a `JSON.stringify`-safe `ToolResult` with no `undefined` fields (ports upstream kilocode commit `f7da00f` which had leaked `movePath: undefined` into permission metadata) — is unchanged. The `beforeEach` / `afterEach` `fs.mkdtempSync` / `fs.rmSync` temp-directory pattern, the `JSON.stringify` round-trip assertion, and the `Object.entries(result.data ?? {})` no-undefined-fields check are all untouched.

  No public API, tool-registry, schema, permission, session, or test-behaviour changes. `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run format:check`, `npm test`, and `npm run build` all remain green on the branch — the only observable delta is that `npm run format:check` now passes on these two files where it previously reported a diff. See `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` under **Style Auto-Fix** for the CI mechanism that produced this commit and `docs/TESTING.md` under **Test File Formatting** for the standing pattern for test-tree auto-fix passes.

### Added

- **Session search performance profile and regression suite (issues #1606 / #1610)** (`scripts/profile-session-search.ts`, `tests/session/performance.test.ts`, `tsconfig.eslint.json`, commits `02894309` `test(core): profile session search performance (#1606)` and `2101d96f` `test(core): profile session search at 200 sessions with memory (#1610)`): New ad-hoc profiling script and companion diagnostic test suite that quantify the two code paths a CLI user hits when listing or searching sessions — `SessionManager.listSessions()` (eager `fs.readdirSync` + `JSON.parse` scan sorted by `updated`) and `SessionManager.searchSessions()` (FTS5-indexed via `SessionSearchIndex` with `refreshIndex()` reconciliation on every call). Numbers are documented in `docs/session-search-performance.md`; the short version is that `listSessions()` is imperceptible at every measured session count (10-1000) and adds under 0.5 MB RSS at every tier up to 500 sessions, while the FTS `searchSessions()` path is currently 10x-40x slower because `refreshIndex()` runs on every invocation. New surface:
  - **`scripts/profile-session-search.ts`** — invoke with `npx tsx scripts/profile-session-search.ts` from the repo root. Seeds six temp directories (10, 50, 100, 200, 500, 1000 sessions), measures each scenario cold and warm, and prints a Markdown table on stdout with both wall time (`ms`) and resident-set-size delta (`MB`) columns suitable for pasting into the docs. The `measure()` helper now samples `process.memoryUsage().rss` immediately before and after each timed region, issues a best-effort `global.gc()` when Node is running with `--expose-gc` to reduce GC-timing noise, and emits the delta as `rssDeltaMb`. Not part of the vitest suite. Cleans up its temp directories on exit so re-runs are idempotent. The 200-session tier was added for issue #1610 to cover the "consider lazy load + incremental search" threshold called out in the issue.
  - **`tests/session/performance.test.ts`** — five Vitest cases that pin the *shape* of the curve rather than the exact numbers: `listSessions` under 100 ms at 10 sessions, `listSessions` under 200 ms at 50 sessions, a JS in-memory substring filter over `listSessions` under 500 ms at 100 sessions, `listSessions` at 200 sessions under 500 ms wall time AND under 50 MB RSS delta (added in commit `2101d96f` for issue #1610), and `searchSessions('')` matches the `listSessions` ordering when the query is empty (with a graceful-skip branch for environments where FTS is not usable). The 200-session case codifies the specific threshold from issue #1610 — the upper edge of the "consider lazy load" band from the issue's proposed thresholds table — so if the eager scan ever moves from imperceptible into the "noticeable" latency budget the assertion trips loudly. Bounds are set at roughly 100x-500x the measured baseline on a typical dev laptop so CI variance never causes a flake; the point of the assertions is that a load-bearing regression (for example, quadratic scan in `listSessions`, or FTS refresh landing in the hot path of `listSessions`, or retaining full message bodies in the metadata path) trips the ceiling immediately. The suite intentionally avoids the previously-considered `SIMULATE_LARGE_SESSION_STORE` env flag — every case measured today is fast enough for the default `npm test` budget.
  - **`tsconfig.eslint.json`**: extended the `include` array to add `scripts/**/*.ts` so the profiling script is covered by the same TypeScript-aware ESLint pass as `src/` and `tests/`. No effect on the production build (`tsconfig.json` `rootDir` is still `src`) — the script is intentionally NOT compiled into `dist/` and is invoked via `tsx` at run time.

  Rationale: the profile-and-test-then-document pattern established here is intended as a template for future CLI performance concerns. A one-off `tsx` script gathers the numbers (now including memory footprint alongside wall time), a `docs/*-performance.md` writeup pins the analysis and recommendations, and a matching `tests/**/performance.test.ts` file codifies the loose bounds so the doc cannot silently rot. The RSS-delta measurement added in `2101d96f` is a blunt instrument on Node (GC timing dominates on small allocations) but at CLI scale it maps cleanly onto the `ps aux` "memory footprint" number that issue #1610 asked for, and is directionally correct even when `--expose-gc` is not available. Follow-up work on the `searchSessions()` `refreshIndex`-on-every-call regression is tracked as a separate issue per `docs/session-search-performance.md#follow-up-issue`. Combined diff statistics across `02894309` and `2101d96f`: 3 files changed, ~325 insertions total.
- **Experimental per-task model selection for the `task` and `agent_manager` tools** (`src/tool/model-selection.ts`, `src/tool/tools/task.ts`, `src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts`, `src/tool/tools/agent-manager-models.ts`, `src/config/userConfig.ts`, 2026-08-31): Ports the upstream opencode/kilocode 2026-08 `task_model_selection` feature (upstream commit `ab143253a`). Subagents spawned via the `task` tool and sessions created via the `agent_manager` tool can now optionally pin a `model`, `provider`, and `reasoning_effort` per invocation instead of inheriting Alexi's default SAP AI Core routing. The behaviour is gated on a new `experimental.task_model_selection` flag in `~/.alexi/config.json` (default `false`), so no subagent call sites change behaviour unless the operator explicitly opts in. Public surface added:
  - `src/tool/model-selection.ts` (new file, 197 lines) — shared model-resolution module extracted from `agent-manager.ts` so both tools reuse identical precedence rules. Exports: `type Candidate = { providerID; model: { id; name } }`, `type Source = { model; variant? }`, `type SelectedModel = { providerID; modelID }`, `type SelectModelError = { error }`, `candidates(): Candidate[]` (enumerates every `(providerID, model)` pair from `getCatalogEntries()`; Alexi returns every entry with `providerID = 'sap-ai-core'` since it ships one runtime provider), `lookup(all, value): { pool; names }` (precedence: exact `providerID/modelID` > exact `model.name` > fuzzy token match on both), `selectModel(source, preferredProviderID?): SelectedModel | SelectModelError` (returns `{ error: 'No model matches "..."'}` on zero matches, `{ error: 'Ambiguous model "..." — candidates: a, b' }` on multiple distinct names), and `isSelectModelError(r)` narrowing guard. Provider preference precedence: (1) explicit `source.variant`, (2) `preferredProviderID`, (3) first candidate in pool.
  - `src/config/userConfig.ts` — new experimental-flag helpers: `getConfigTaskModelSelection(): boolean` reads `experimental.task_model_selection` from `~/.alexi/config.json` (returns `false` on missing, non-object, array, or non-boolean values); `setConfigTaskModelSelection(enabled: boolean): void` merges the value into the existing `experimental` object without clobbering other experimental flags. Serialized shape mirrors upstream verbatim: `{ "experimental": { "task_model_selection": true } }`.
  - `src/tool/tools/task.ts` — `TaskParamsSchema` extended with three optional nullable fields: `model` (free-form name or `provider/id`), `provider` (disambiguates when a model name is offered by multiple providers), and `reasoning_effort` (`'low' | 'medium' | 'high'`). All three are gated: if any is supplied while `experimental.task_model_selection` is `false`, the tool returns `{ success: false, error: 'Per-task model selection is disabled. Set experimental.task_model_selection=true in ~/.alexi/config.json to allow subagents to override model/provider/reasoning_effort.' }`. `provider` without `model` returns `{ success: false, error: 'task.provider requires task.model to be set' }` (mirrors the upstream `agent-manager` invariant). On success, `TaskResult` surfaces the resolved `model`, `provider`, and `reasoning_effort` alongside the existing `taskId` / `agentId` / `response` fields so the parent orchestrator can log which model actually ran.
  - `src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts` — `config.provider` field added to `create` action schema. Provider-preference invariant enforced: `config.provider` without `config.model` returns `{ success: false, error: 'config.provider requires config.model to be set' }`. Resolution now delegates to the shared `selectModel()` helper from `src/tool/model-selection.ts` (previously inline). `create` response surfaces resolved `session.model` and `session.provider` after resolution.
  - `src/tool/tools/agent-manager-models.ts` — previously a stub exporting only `AGENT_MANAGER_MODELS_HINT`, now a full `agent_manager_models` tool registered in `src/tool/tools/index.ts` alongside `agent_manager`. When `experimental.task_model_selection` is `false`, the tool returns `{ enabled: false, message: 'Model catalog listing is disabled. ...' }` and no rows. When the flag is `true`, it returns a paginated list of `{ modelName, providers, ids }` rows filtered by an optional `query` (case-insensitive token match against model name, provider IDs, and qualified `provider/id` strings). Parameters: `query?: string | null`, `offset?: number | null` (default 0), `limit?: number | null` (default and max 50).
  - `src/cli/session/prompt.tsx` — new `sendPrompt(options: SendPromptOptions): SendPromptOptions` facade with `SendPromptOptions = { text; model?; provider?; reasoning_effort? }`. Exists as a lightweight normalization layer (trims text, preserves optional model/provider/reasoning fields) so the TUI can hand off a payload to the streaming orchestrator without importing the orchestrator directly. Currently returns the normalized options unchanged; the real dispatch remains in `src/core/streamingOrchestrator.ts`.

  Rationale: upstream opencode split model resolution out of the `agent_manager` tool into a shared helper so the `task` tool could reuse it once the experimental flag landed. Keeping the flag `false` by default preserves Alexi's SAP AI Core-only defaults for the vast majority of subagent calls; operators who need per-turn model overrides (e.g. cost-controlled explorations on a cheaper model, or reasoning-effort tuning for hard tasks) opt in explicitly. Diff statistics: 6 files changed, ~600 insertions across `src/tool/model-selection.ts`, `src/tool/tools/task.ts`, `src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts`, `src/tool/tools/agent-manager-models.ts`, `src/config/userConfig.ts`, and `src/cli/session/prompt.tsx`.

- **TUI smoke-render test harness** (`tests/tui/smoke-render.test.tsx`, 2026-08-31): New test-only render helper that boots any Ink component under `ink-testing-library` and classifies the resulting frame against three regression modes — blank output, React error-boundary panic banners, and unresponsive command palette. Ports the upstream kilocode 2026-08 PTY smoke-test hardening (`5e02825c8..ab143253a`) but uses `ink-testing-library` in a `node` environment instead of a real PTY (`node-pty`), because Alexi's Ink surface is thin enough that ink-testing-library catches the same regressions with much lower flake. Public surface:
  - `PANIC_MARKERS: readonly string[]` — substrings that indicate a React error boundary tripped or a native runtime panic surfaced to the terminal (`Error boundary caught`, `The above error occurred`, `Consider adding an error boundary`, `Uncaught (in promise)`, `TypeError:`, `ReferenceError:`, `panic:`).
  - `interface RenderReport` — `{ frame: string; isBlank: boolean; panicMarker: string | null; paletteResponsive: boolean | null }`. `frame` is the final rendered frame after the component settled; `isBlank` is `true` when the frame is empty or whitespace-only; `panicMarker` holds the first matching panic string or `null`; `paletteResponsive` is the result of the optional palette-open probe (any visible change post-keypress counts).
  - `interface RenderOptions` — `{ probeKey?: string; settleMs?: number }`. `probeKey` triggers the palette probe (defaults to no probe); `settleMs` debounces the initial render (defaults to 50ms).
  - `render(element, options?): Promise<RenderReport>` — exported so future TUI dialogs and pages can add their own boot smoke tests without duplicating the blank/panic/palette detection. Guaranteed to `unmount()` before returning so timers and effects do not leak between tests.

- **Reusable line-ending detection helpers in `src/utils/line-ending.ts`** (`src/utils/line-ending.ts`, `tests/utils/line-ending.test.ts`, 2026-08-30): New shared utility module exposes two pure detection helpers plus a public constant so that any write path that round-trips a file through a normalized-to-LF intermediate representation (currently `apply_patch`, plausibly `write` / `edit` in the future) can classify the file's convention without duplicating the counting logic. Public surface:
  - `type LineEnding = 'LF' | 'CRLF' | 'mixed'` — uppercase style label used by the public API. LF and CRLF are the two pure conventions; `mixed` is returned when a file contains both bare LFs and CRLFs.
  - `LINE_ENDING_SAMPLE_BYTES = 8 * 1024` — head-chunk sample size used by the file-path detector, exported so tests can construct files that straddle the sample boundary (e.g. put an LF prefix in the first 8 KiB and a CRLF tail after it and assert the sample-based detector still reports LF).
  - `detectLineEndingFromString(content: string): LineEnding` — walks the string once counting CRLF-terminated LFs vs bare LFs (a `\n` at index `i` is CRLF-terminated iff `content.charCodeAt(i - 1) === 0x0d`).
