Returns `'CRLF'` when only CRLFs are present, `'LF'` when only bare LFs are present, `'mixed'` when both occur, and `'LF'` as the safe default for content with no line endings at all. A lone `\r` (old MacOS 9 style) is deliberately NOT treated as a line ending — the helper only reports LF / CRLF / mixed.
  - `detectLineEnding(filePath: string): Promise<LineEnding>` — opens the file, reads at most `LINE_ENDING_SAMPLE_BYTES` bytes from offset 0 via `handle.read`, decodes the sample as UTF-8 with `fatal: false` (so a chunk that ends mid-multibyte-codepoint still yields a usable string for counting; `\r` and `\n` are always single-byte in UTF-8), and delegates to `detectLineEndingFromString`. Uses a `try / finally` around `handle.close()` so the file handle is released even on a decoder throw. An empty file resolves to `'LF'`.

  Rationale: `apply_patch` previously carried its own inline majority-count detector (`detectLineEndingStyle` — still exported for backward compatibility because it applies a platform-default fallback via `os.EOL` that the pure helper does not). Splitting the pure detection into a shared module makes it reusable for any tool that needs cheap, path-based classification without loading a multi-megabyte file into memory, and pins the sample-window contract behind an exported constant so tests can verify the fast path directly instead of trusting the majority-of-file fallback.

  Test coverage: 15 cases in `tests/utils/line-ending.test.ts` — 8 cases for `detectLineEndingFromString` (pure CRLF, pure LF, mixed both orderings, no line endings, empty string, lone `\r` treated as no line ending, single trailing `\r\n`, single trailing `\n`) and 7 cases for `detectLineEnding` (CRLF file, LF file, mixed file, empty file, sample-window boundary — pure LF in the first 8 KiB with CRLF appended after so the detector must report LF, non-existent file rejects with `/ENOENT/`, and the temp-directory `fs.mkdtemp` / `fs.rm` teardown pattern). Diff statistics: `1 file changed, 101 insertions(+)`.

- **Minify-safe telemetry detection pattern** (`src/utils/telemetry.ts`, `tests/utils/telemetry-minify.test.ts`, commit `d98d5e22` `feat(tools): add minify-safe telemetry detection pattern`): New structural detection surface for the telemetry module designed to survive bundler minification. Production bundlers (esbuild, Bun, terser, swc) routinely rename local class identifiers to single letters, silently breaking any `obj.constructor.name === 'TelemetryService'` check that ships in a minified build. The telemetry module now exposes three surfaces consumers can rely on regardless of minification state:
  - `interface TelemetryServiceLike` — structural public surface declaring the four required methods (`setEnabled(enabled: boolean): void`, `track(event: string, properties?: Record<string, unknown>): void`, `getEvents(): TelemetryEvent[]`, `clear(): void`). The concrete `TelemetryService` class is now declared `implements TelemetryServiceLike` and remains unexported precisely because its identity must not be part of the public contract.
  - `function isTelemetryService(obj: unknown): obj is TelemetryServiceLike` — structural, minify-safe type guard. Returns `true` only when `obj` is a non-null object carrying all four methods; deliberately rejects single-method matches (`{ track: fn }` alone) because generic event emitters expose `track` and would otherwise be false-positives. Serves as the canonical example for future telemetry integrations (OpenTelemetry, Sentry, Datadog, Langfuse).
  - `const telemetryInstance: TelemetryServiceLike` — exported reference to the singleton service, so consumers that need identity-level detection can do `obj === telemetryInstance` (the cheapest and most robust minify-immune check).

  The pre-existing `Telemetry` facade (`track`, `setEnabled`, `getEvents`, `clear`) is unchanged; only the underlying detection surface expanded. Test coverage: 8 cases in `tests/utils/telemetry-minify.test.ts` load `src/utils/telemetry.ts` through `esbuild.transform` with `minify: true`, import the result via a `data:text/javascript` URL, and assert (1) the minifier actually renamed the internal class (the source no longer matches `/class\s+TelemetryService\b/`), (2) `isTelemetryService` and `telemetryInstance` are still exported after minification, (3) `isTelemetryService(telemetryInstance)` returns `true` against the minified singleton, (4) partial shapes and non-objects are correctly rejected, (5) duck-typed hand-rolled shapes are accepted, (6) `constructor.name` of the minified instance is NOT `'TelemetryService'` (documents why the structural approach is required), (7) the `Telemetry` facade round-trips `setEnabled` + `track` + `getEvents` + `clear` after minification, and (8) cross-boundary duck-typing works both ways — the unminified `isTelemetryService` accepts an instance from the minified module, and vice versa. Diff statistics: `2 files changed, 222 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)`. Rationale and reference pattern documented in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md#minify-safe-patterns`; test mechanics documented in `docs/TESTING.md#testing-minify-safe-telemetry-detection`.

- **Expanded rules file discovery with configurable custom paths** (`src/config/rulesDiscovery.ts`, `src/agent/system.ts`, `tests/rulesDiscovery.test.ts`, `src/agent/system.test.ts`, commit `874caa58` `feat(config): expand rules file discovery to support custom paths`): New `rulesDiscovery` module extends prompt-assembly rule loading beyond the historical `<workdir>/.alexi/rules/*.md` scan to also cover common alternative directory conventions used by neighbouring tools (`.kilo/rules/`, `.kilocode/rules/`, `.opencode/rules/`, `.cline/rules/`, root-level `rules/`), the user-level `~/.alexi/rules/`, and a fully configurable `rulesPath` field in `.alexi/config.json` (project or global). Public surface exported from `src/config/rulesDiscovery.ts`:
  - `DEFAULT_PROJECT_RULE_DIRS: readonly string[]` — precedence-ordered list of default project-relative directories scanned for `*.md` rule files (`.alexi/rules`, `.kilo/rules`, `.kilocode/rules`, `.opencode/rules`, `.cline/rules`, `rules`).
  - `DEFAULT_USER_RULE_DIR = '.alexi/rules'` — default user-level rules directory resolved against `os.homedir()`.
  - `interface DiscoveredRule` — `{ filePath, fileName, ruleKey, source: 'project' | 'user' | 'custom', originDir, sizeBytes, content }`. `ruleKey` is the filename minus `.md` extension and serves as the conflict key.
  - `interface RuleConflict` — `{ ruleKey, winner, shadowed: string[] }`, one record per rule key with more than one match.
  - `interface RulesDiscoveryResult` — `{ rules, allFiles, conflicts, scannedDirs }`. `rules` is the post-conflict-resolution emission order; `allFiles` includes shadowed duplicates for logging.
  - `interface DiscoverRulesOptions` — `{ workdir?, homedir?, customPaths?, silent? }`. `customPaths` bypasses `.alexi/config.json` reads (useful for tests and CLI overrides); `silent` suppresses INFO/warn logs.
  - `discoverRules(options?): RulesDiscoveryResult` — main discovery entry point. Resolves custom paths from project + global `.alexi/config.json`, appends default project directories, appends the user-level directory, walks each existing directory, loads every `*.md` file, resolves conflicts by rule key (first-seen wins), and returns the bundle. Missing directories, unreadable files, malformed JSON, and non-string `rulesPath` shapes all degrade to safe defaults rather than throwing.
  - `logRulesDiscovery(result: RulesDiscoveryResult): void` — emits `Loaded rules from <path> (<bytes> bytes)` for every winning rule, `Discovered <N> rules files` summary, and `Rule '<key>' redefined in <path> (using <winner>)` warnings for each shadowed duplicate.
  - `normalizeRulesPathValue(value: unknown): string[]` — coerces the raw config value into a trimmed non-empty string array. Accepts a single string (`"custom/rules"`) or an array of strings; every other shape yields `[]`.
  - `resolveCustomRulesPaths(workdir: string, homedir: string): string[]` — merges project (`<workdir>/.alexi/config.json:rulesPath`) with global (`~/.alexi/config.json:rulesPath`) custom paths, deduplicating while preserving the project-first precedence order.

  Precedence order (lower index wins on conflict): (1) project `.alexi/config.json:rulesPath` entries, (2) global `~/.alexi/config.json:rulesPath` entries, (3) `.alexi/rules`, (4) `.kilo/rules`, (5) `.kilocode/rules`, (6) `.opencode/rules`, (7) `.cline/rules`, (8) `rules`, (9) `~/.alexi/rules`. Paths starting with `~/` are expanded against the resolved home directory; relative paths are resolved against the workdir; absolute paths are used as-is.

  Integration in `src/agent/system.ts:loadInstructionFiles`: the historical inline `fs.readdirSync('<workdir>/.alexi/rules')` block is replaced by a `discoverRules({ workdir, silent: true })` call. Every winning rule is emitted into the assembled system prompt as `<rule file="<basename>">\n<content>\n</rule>`, matching the pre-existing wrapper shape so downstream consumers see no behavioural change beyond the expanded discovery surface. A new module-level `loggedWorkdirs: Set<string>` cache ensures the `Loaded rules from ...` / `Discovered N rules files` summary is emitted at most once per workdir per process — the prompt assembler runs on every message turn and would otherwise spam the console. A new `resetRulesDiscoveryLogCache(): void` export lets tests reset the cache between cases; production callers should never need it.

  Test coverage: 24 cases in `tests/rulesDiscovery.test.ts` cover `normalizeRulesPathValue` shape coercion (6 cases), `resolveCustomRulesPaths` project/global merging (4 cases), full-discovery precedence and conflict resolution (10 cases including default paths, user-dir fallback, `~` expansion, shadow reporting, and malformed-config resilience), and `logRulesDiscovery` output shape (4 cases). 4 integration cases in `src/agent/system.test.ts` under the `Rules discovery integration` describe block cover the end-to-end path via `buildAssembledSystemPrompt`: rules from `.alexi/rules/` land in the prompt with the `<rule file="...">` wrapper, alternative directory conventions (`.kilo/`, `.kilocode/`, `.opencode/`, `.cline/`, `rules/`) all appear in the prompt, `rulesPath` from project `.alexi/config.json` is honored, and a custom rules path wins over the default `.alexi/rules` on conflict. Combined diff: `4 files changed, 935 insertions(+), 16 deletions(-)`.

### Changed

- **Cosmetic Prettier reflow of `decodeJsonIfString` in `agent_manager` tool** (`src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts`, commit `a7a217aa` `fix(ci): auto-fix CI failures [alexi-bot]`): Non-behavioural formatting pass over the `decodeJsonIfString<T extends z.ZodTypeAny>(schema: T)` preprocessor helper at the top of the `agent_manager` tool module. The previous three-line signature carried an explicit return-type annotation (`): z.ZodEffects<T, z.infer<T>, unknown> {`); Prettier's auto-fix pipeline collapsed the declaration onto a single line and dropped the explicit return type in favour of TypeScript's inference. The inferred return type is byte-identical (`z.ZodEffects<T, z.infer<T>, unknown>`) so every downstream caller — the `config`, `sessionId`, `agentId`, `answer`, `worktreeId` fields on `AgentManagerParamsSchema`, and every wrapped inner Zod object — resolves to the same shape it did before. The preprocessor's runtime contract is unchanged: strings whose trimmed first character is `{` or `[` are `JSON.parse`d and handed to the wrapped schema; primitive strings, non-string values, and parse failures pass through unchanged so the wrapped Zod schema still emits a descriptive validation error rather than crashing on a raw `SyntaxError`. `npm run typecheck` and `npm test` produce byte-identical output; the only observable delta is that `npm run lint` and `npm run format:check` now pass on the file. Diff statistics: `1 file changed, 1 insertion(+), 3 deletions(-)`.

- **Cosmetic Prettier reflow of model-selection helpers** (`src/tool/model-selection.ts`, `src/tool/tools/agent-manager-models.ts`, commit `c09f38b9` `style(ci): auto-fix lint/format issues [alexi-bot]`): Non-behavioural formatting pass over the two modules that wire the shared model-resolution helpers (`Candidate`, `Source`, `SelectedModel`, `SelectModelError`) into the `agent_manager_models` tool. Three signatures in `src/tool/model-selection.ts` were reflowed to fit the project-wide 100-column Prettier ceiling: (1) the `name` field assignment inside `candidates()` — `(entry.metadata as { name?: string } | undefined)?.name ?? entry.id` collapses from a three-line ternary onto a single line; (2) the `lookup(all: Candidate[], value: string): { pool: Candidate[]; names: string[] }` signature collapses from a three-line parameter list onto one line; (3) the `isSelectModelError(r: SelectedModel | SelectModelError): r is SelectModelError` type-guard signature collapses from a three-line declaration onto one line. In `src/tool/tools/agent-manager-models.ts`, four sites reflow the same way: (a) the `defineTool<typeof ParamsSchema, AgentManagerModelsResult>({...})` generic parameter list moves onto a single 91-column line; (b) the query-branch `matches` assignment (`all.filter((row) => matchesQuery([row.modelName, ...row.providers, ...row.ids], query))`) collapses from a three-line arrow function onto one line; (c) the `nextOffset` ternary collapses onto one line. The `Candidate`, `Source`, `SelectedModel`, `SelectModelError` types, the `matchesQuery` / `candidates` / `lookup` / `selectModel` / `isSelectModelError` public API, the `ParamsSchema` Zod shape (`query`/`offset`/`limit` all `.nullable().optional()`), the `MAX_LIMIT = 50` pagination ceiling, the `AGENT_MANAGER_MODELS_HINT` string, and the `experimental.task_model_selection` opt-in gate are all unchanged. `npm run typecheck` and `npm test` produce byte-identical output; the only observable delta is that `npm run lint` and `npm run format:check` now succeed on these two files. Diff statistics: `2 files changed, 6 insertions(+), 19 deletions(-)`.

- **`apply_patch` line-ending detection now uses an 8 KiB sample fast path** (`src/tool/tools/apply-patch.ts`, `tests/tool/tools/apply-patch.test.ts`, 2026-08-30): The `apply_patch` tool now calls `detectLineEnding(filePath)` from `src/utils/line-ending.ts` BEFORE reading the full file into memory. When the 8 KiB head-sample classifies the file as pure `'LF'` or pure `'CRLF'`, that result drives the write-back convention directly; only when the sample returns `'mixed'` does the tool fall through to the existing full-content majority-count detector (`detectLineEndingStyle`). The full read + `decodeWithEncoding` + `normalizeToLf` pipeline is unchanged, so the patch parser continues to see LF-normalized input and the encoding-preservation path is unaffected. Net effect: on multi-megabyte files with a consistent convention (the common case) the tool no longer pays the cost of counting every `\r\n` / `\n` occurrence across the whole file just to pick a re-encoding style. `detectLineEndingStyle`, `normalizeToLf`, and `applyLineEndingStyle` remain exported from `src/tool/tools/apply-patch.ts` for backward compatibility with existing tests and for callers that already have the full content in memory. Two new regression tests in `tests/tool/tools/apply-patch.test.ts` `describe('line ending preservation')` pin the sample-detector code path directly: `preserves CRLF via the file-path sample detector on a pure CRLF file` (constructs a file that classifies as `CRLF` via the sample, applies an LF-encoded patch, asserts the output is uniformly CRLF and contains no bare LF) and `preserves LF via the file-path sample detector on a pure LF file` (mirror case, asserts no `\r\n` in the output). These sit alongside the existing mixed-file and cross-style tests that continue to exercise the majority-count fallback path. Diff statistics: `2 files changed, 73 insertions(+), 4 deletions(-)`.

### Fixed

- **`apply_patch` tool preserves original file line-ending style** (`src/tool/tools/apply-patch.ts`, `tests/tool/tools/apply-patch.test.ts`, commit `3adb7ec8` `feat(tools): preserve CRLF line endings in apply_patch`, 2026-08-29): The `apply_patch` tool now detects the dominant line-ending style of the target file BEFORE parsing the patch, normalizes both the file and the incoming patch to LF for the line-based hunk parser, then re-encodes the patched output to the original style before `fs.writeFile`. Rationale: the previous implementation split on `'\n'` only, which left a stray `\r` on every context and deletion line when the file was CRLF, causing every hunk on a Windows-normalized file (or a repo with `core.autocrlf=true`) to fail with a spurious `PatchHunkError` (`expected "foo", got "foo\r"`). Symmetrically, a CRLF-encoded patch applied to an LF file would emit `\r\n` on the additions and produce a mixed-ending output. Three new pure helpers are now exported from `src/tool/tools/apply-patch.ts` for direct unit testing and reuse:
  - `detectLineEndingStyle(content: string): 'crlf' | 'lf'` — walks the string once, counts CRLF vs bare LF occurrences, and returns the majority style. When the content has no line endings at all, falls back to the platform default (`os.EOL === '\r\n' ? 'crlf' : 'lf'`). Mixed-ending files resolve to whichever count is strictly greater; a tie (equal CRLF and LF counts) resolves to `'lf'` because the strict `>` comparison excludes the equal case.
  - `normalizeToLf(content: string): string` — collapses every `\r\n` sequence to `\n`; a no-op on already-LF content and idempotent under repeated application.
  - `applyLineEndingStyle(content: string, style: 'crlf' | 'lf'): string` — re-encodes LF-only content back to the target style. The `lf` branch is a no-op; the `crlf` branch relies on the invariant that the caller passed content already normalized to LF (guaranteed by the tool's own pipeline via `normalizeToLf`), so it can safely `content.replace(/\n/g, '\r\n')` without producing `\r\r\n`.
  Integration in `src/tool/tools/apply-patch.ts:292-323`: after `decodeWithEncoding(buffer, encoding)`, the tool calls `detectLineEndingStyle(rawOriginalContent)` on the raw decoded content, then normalizes both the original content and `params.patch` to LF via `normalizeToLf`. The existing `applyPatchToContent` parser runs unchanged on LF-only strings. Before the encoded buffer is passed to `encodeWithEncoding`, the patched content is re-encoded through `applyLineEndingStyle(patchedContent, lineEndingStyle)`. The change is orthogonal to the encoding-preservation pipeline (UTF-8 / UTF-16 / legacy code pages via `detectEncoding` / `encodeWithEncoding`), and the `PatchHunkError` contract, the diff summary, and the `linesChanged` count all continue to operate on the LF-normalized copies so their values are stable regardless of the file's on-disk EOL style. Test coverage: 3 helper cases in `tests/tool/tools/apply-patch.test.ts` `describe('line ending helpers')` cover CRLF-majority, LF-majority, mixed with CRLF winning, and mixed with LF winning; plus 3 end-to-end cases in `describe('line ending preservation')` cover LF-only patch applied to a CRLF file (asserting `/(?:^|[^\r])\n/.test(updated)` is false), CRLF-encoded patch applied to an LF file (asserting no `\r\n` in output), and a mixed-ending file (3 CRLF + 1 LF → CRLF wins, output uniformly CRLF). Diff statistics: `2 files changed, 159 insertions(+), 4 deletions(-)`.

Prettier reflows in `ModelPicker.tsx` collapse the initial-state ternary onto three lines and the subscription-callback ternary onto one, plus tighten the header/footer legend strings (`● live ○ static`, `[↑↓] Navigate [Enter] Select [Esc] Cancel`) to single-space separators. `completer.ts` reformats the model-catalog import onto multiple lines and the `getCatalogStatus()` ternary in `completeModelName` onto three lines to fit the 100-column limit. `modelPicker.ts` drops the unused `getAvailableModels as getCatalogModels` re-import that the picker never referenced (the live path uses `getCatalogEntries` directly). `sapOrchestration.ts` removes a stale `// eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-var-requires` comment above the lazy `require('./modelCatalog.js')` in `isOrchestrationModel` — the rule is no longer configured against that call site so the disable is dead. No public API, routing, tool-execution, or provider behaviour changes; `npm run typecheck` and `npm test` produce byte-identical output. The only observable delta is that `npm run lint` and `npm run format:check` now succeed on these five files.

### Added

- **Documented Dynamic Model Catalog surface in `docs/PROVIDERS.md`** (2026-08-29): New section `## Dynamic Model Catalog` covers the `modelCatalog.ts` public API (`CatalogStatus`, `CatalogEntry`, `refreshModelCatalog`, `subscribeCatalog`, `getCatalogStatus`, `getAvailableModels`, `getLiveModels`, `getCatalogEntries`, `isAvailableModel`, `getModelMetadata`, `isModelLive`, `invalidateCatalog`) with a Mermaid startup-flow diagram, deployment-identification rules (static exact match + eight known provider prefixes), the 5-minute background refresh schedule (`CATALOG_TTL_MS`, `setTimeout` + `unref`), and integration points in the TUI (`ModelPicker.tsx`), CLI tab-completion (`completer.ts`), and the router guard (`isOrchestrationModel`). Documents the lazy-import pattern used to break the `modelCatalog.ts` -> `sapOrchestration.ts` -> `modelCatalog.ts` reverse dependency, and the testing hooks (`invalidateCatalog`, exported `CATALOG_TTL_MS`).

- **MCP per-server timeout independence regression tests** (`tests/mcp/client-timeout.test.ts`, issue #1532, 2026-08-25): New `per-server independence` describe block adds two cases that pin the concurrency contract of `McpClientManager.callTool` (`src/mcp/client.ts:1417`). Each connected server owns a dedicated `AbortController` created inside `withRequestTimeout(serverName, 'callTool', run)` (`src/mcp/client.ts:537`), so a slow peer must NOT delay a call on a fast peer. Case 1 (`slow server times out independently without blocking fast server`) fires concurrent `callTool` invocations against `fast-server` (5000 ms budget) and `slow-server` (30000 ms budget), asserts the fast call resolves without advancing any timer, drains pending microtasks and asserts the slow call is still pending, then advances 30000 ms and asserts the slow call aborts with `/^MCP callTool timed out after 30000ms /` and `(request timeout for server 'slow-server')`. Case 2 (`falls back to the 60s default when per-server timeout is not specified`) advances 59000 ms and asserts the promise is still pending, then advances the last 1000 ms and asserts a timeout at exactly the 60000 ms boundary with `(request timeout for server 'default-server')` — no breaking change to the pre-existing default. Diff statistics: `1 file changed, 122 insertions(+)`.
- **Platform-native line-ending normalization for the `write` tool** (`src/tool/eol-normalizer.ts`, `src/tool/eol-normalizer.test.ts`, `src/tool/tools/write.ts`, `src/tool/tools/__tests__/write.eol.test.ts`, commit `d0dec417`, 2026-08-25): New EOL-normalization helpers ensure that files written by the `write` tool use the host platform's native line-ending convention (`\n` on POSIX, `\r\n` on Windows via `os.EOL`) for **new** files, and preserve the existing file's line-ending style when **overwriting**. Rationale: on Windows repositories using `core.autocrlf`, LF-only writes from the model would previously show up in `git diff` as an entire-file change; symmetrically, overwriting a CRLF file with LF content flipped the file's EOL style and produced spurious diffs. Public surface exported from `src/tool/eol-normalizer.ts`:
  - `type LineEnding = '\n' | '\r\n'` — narrow union of the two supported endings.
  - `detectLineEnding(content: string): LineEnding` — returns `'\r\n'` if any `\r\n` sequence is present in the content, otherwise `'\n'`. Empty content and content without any line endings both resolve to `'\n'` as a safe default. Mixed-ending files prefer CRLF (a single `\r\n` anywhere in the file classifies the whole file as CRLF).
  - `normalizeNewFileLineEndings(content: string, eol?: LineEnding): string` — normalizes to the target EOL for **new** files. Internally collapses any pre-existing `\r\n` to `\n` first (guard against double-CR when the caller already passed CRLF), then rewrites to the target. Defaults to `getPlatformEol()` when `eol` is omitted. Idempotent: re-applying the same target is a no-op.
  - `preserveExistingLineEndings(newContent: string, existingContent: string): string` — used when **overwriting** an existing file. Detects the existing file's EOL via `detectLineEnding(existingContent)` and rewrites `newContent` to match. Defaults to LF when the existing file has no line endings at all.
  - `getPlatformEol(): LineEnding` — resolves `os.EOL` to the `LineEnding` union. Any value other than `'\r\n'` (defensive) is treated as `'\n'`.
  Integration in `src/tool/tools/write.ts:84-103`: the tool now branches on the `exists` flag it already computes for the `created` result field. For a new file, `finalContent = normalizeNewFileLineEndings(finalContent)` is applied after BOM handling and before encoding. For an existing file, the tool reads the existing bytes, decodes them as UTF-8 for EOL sniffing (safe because `\r` and `\n` are ASCII in every encoding the tool supports), and calls `preserveExistingLineEndings(finalContent, existingText)`. If the existing-file read fails for any reason (permissions, race, exotic filesystem), the tool falls back to platform-native EOL rather than blocking the write. Result: a Windows user running Alexi on a repository with `core.autocrlf=true` no longer sees whole-file diffs for LF-only model output, and existing CRLF files stay CRLF (and existing LF files stay LF) across overwrites regardless of what the model emitted. Test coverage: 5 cases in `src/tool/eol-normalizer.test.ts` cover `detectLineEnding` (LF-only, CRLF-only, empty, no-ending, mixed), 6 cases cover `normalizeNewFileLineEndings` (LF→CRLF, LF→LF, CRLF→CRLF idempotence, CRLF→LF, double-application idempotence, platform-default), 3 cases cover `preserveExistingLineEndings` (existing CRLF, existing LF, no-ending fallback), and 1 case guards `getPlatformEol()`. 5 integration cases in `src/tool/tools/__tests__/write.eol.test.ts` cover the tool wiring end-to-end: new-file platform EOL, stray-CRLF collapsing on LF platforms, preserving CRLF when overwriting a CRLF file, preserving LF when overwriting an LF file, defaulting to LF when overwriting a file with no line endings, and a simulated-Windows suite that mocks `getPlatformEol` to return `'\r\n'` and asserts new files are written with CRLF (with the expected `bytesWritten` reflecting the extra bytes).

### Fixed

- **Circular ESM import between `sapOrchestration.ts` and `modelCatalog.ts`** (`src/providers/sapOrchestration.ts`, commit `544ba4ef` `fix(providers): replace require() with globalThis registry pattern`, 2026-08-29): Replaced the lazy `require('./modelCatalog.js')` call inside `isOrchestrationModel()` with a `globalThis`-keyed function-pointer registry that `modelCatalog.ts` populates at its own module-load time. Rationale: `modelCatalog.ts` imports `ORCHESTRATION_MODELS` and `ORCHESTRATION_MODEL_METADATA` from `sapOrchestration.ts`, so a top-level `import { isAvailableModel } from './modelCatalog.js'` in `sapOrchestration.ts` would close a circular ESM cycle. The previous fix used a synchronous `require('./modelCatalog.js')` inside a `try / catch` (a CJS interop hack) which is flagged by `@typescript-eslint/no-require-imports` and behaves inconsistently across strict-ESM Node loaders. New public/internal surface in `src/providers/sapOrchestration.ts:1987-2007`:
  - `type IsAvailableModelFn = (id: string) => boolean` — signature of the guard function.
  - `const _catalogRegistry = globalThis as unknown as { __alexiCatalogIsAvailable?: IsAvailableModelFn }` — typed handle to the well-known registry key on `globalThis`. The `__alexi` prefix keeps the key out of the way of unrelated dependencies.
  - `export function _registerCatalogGuard(fn: IsAvailableModelFn): void` — `@internal` setter called by `modelCatalog.ts` after it defines `isAvailableModel`. Idempotent per module load; the last registered function wins.
  - `export function isOrchestrationModel(modelId: string): boolean` — unchanged signature and semantics. Fast path is still the static `ORCHESTRATION_MODELS.includes(...)` check; the delegation now resolves via `_catalogRegistry.__alexiCatalogIsAvailable?.(modelId) ?? false` instead of the `require`/`try`/`catch` block. When `modelCatalog.ts` has not loaded (e.g. a test that mocks the provider layer wholesale), the guard collapses to the static-only check.
  Wiring on the other side (`src/providers/modelCatalog.ts:342`): the module now imports `_registerCatalogGuard` alongside `ORCHESTRATION_MODELS`/`ORCHESTRATION_MODEL_METADATA` from `sapOrchestration.ts`, and calls `_registerCatalogGuard(isAvailableModel)` as a top-level side effect after `isAvailableModel` is defined. Net effect: the ESM linker sees a strict DAG (only `modelCatalog.ts` → `sapOrchestration.ts`); the reverse edge is expressed as a function-pointer lookup at guard-call time rather than an `import` at link time. No behavioural change to `isOrchestrationModel` for any caller — the fast path, the fallback path, and the return type are all preserved. Diff statistics: `1 file changed, 21 insertions(+), 12 deletions(-)`.

## [1.22.8] - 2026-09-01

### Added

- **JSON-encoded params tolerance in `agent_manager` tool** (`src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts`, `src/tool/tools/__tests__/agent-manager.json-config.test.ts`, 2026-09-01, ports upstream kilocode `02df76976` `fix(agent-manager): decode JSON-encoded task arrays`): The `agent_manager` tool now accepts either a native `config` object or a JSON-encoded string for the same field, decoding transparently before Zod validation. Some LLM providers — Anthropic in particular — emit structured tool-call parameters as a JSON string rather than the native object shape, which previously failed schema validation. Public surface added:
  - `decodeJsonIfString<T extends z.ZodTypeAny>(schema: T): z.ZodEffects<T, z.infer<T>, unknown>` — internal preprocessor that wraps a Zod schema. If the input is a string that begins with `{` or `[` after trimming, `JSON.parse()` is attempted and the parsed value is handed to the inner schema. Non-string values, empty strings, primitive-looking strings, and strings that fail to parse pass through unchanged so the inner schema can still produce a descriptive validation error. Currently applied to the `config` field of `AgentManagerParamsSchema`.

  Test coverage (`src/tool/tools/__tests__/agent-manager.json-config.test.ts`, 64 lines, 3 cases): (1) `create` with `config` as a `JSON.stringify(...)` string succeeds and returns the expected `session.status`; (2) `create` with a native object regression case still succeeds; (3) `list` accepts both a missing `config` and an explicit `null`. No behaviour change for callers already passing native objects.

- **JSON-encoding regression guard for `apply_patch` result payload** (`src/tool/tools/__tests__/apply-patch.json-encoding.test.ts`, 2026-09-01): New regression suite (`68` lines, 1 case) that pins the `apply_patch` tool's `ToolResult` payload as fully JSON-round-trippable. Constructs a temp workdir with `fs.mkdtempSync`, writes a sample file, applies a small hunk, then asserts `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(result))` does not throw AND that no field in `result.data` is `undefined` (a `movePath: undefined` regression would be silently erased by `JSON.stringify` and would fail the `not.toBeUndefined()` loop). Ports the intent of upstream kilocode `f7da00f`, guarding against future regressions where the tool leaks `undefined` fields into permission metadata or downstream event buses.

- **Shared read-only bash allow entries constant** (`src/agent/index.ts`, 2026-09-01, ports upstream kilocode `e096d3ab7` `refactor(cli): share common bash permission entries`): The read-only bash allowlist used by the `ask` agent and `plan` mode now lives in a separate `readable: Record<string, 'allow'>` constant that is spread into the wider `readOnlyBash: Record<string, 'allow' | 'ask' | 'deny'>` map. Callers that need "just the pure read-only allow set" (without the git-deny rules baked into `readOnlyBash`) can reuse `readable` directly, and any new read-only command is added in one place instead of drifting between mirrored maps. No public API surface change — `readable` is a module-local constant. The default deny (`'*': 'deny'`) invariant for the `ask` agent is preserved: unknown commands are still denied, not "ask"ed, so the read-only agent can never modify the filesystem.

### Changed

- **`agent_manager` tool `config` schema now uses the `decodeJsonIfString` preprocessor** (`src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts`): The `config` field of `AgentManagerParamsSchema` is now wrapped in `decodeJsonIfString(...)`, giving it JSON-string tolerance without changing the accepted shape. The existing `mode`, `model`, `provider`, and `excludeLocalState` sub-fields keep their `nullable().optional()` semantics; the `config.provider` requires `config.model` invariant introduced in 1.22.7 remains enforced. The change is transparent to callers already emitting native objects — the preprocessor only reshapes the input when it is a string.

- **`apply_patch` success payload is now constructed defensively** (`src/tool/tools/apply-patch.ts:361-370`): The tool now builds an intermediate `data: ApplyPatchResult` object (`{ path, diff, linesChanged }`) with only defined fields before returning `{ success: true, data }`, ports upstream kilocode `f7da00f`. Rationale: passing a shape with `undefined` values through downstream permission metadata / event buses breaks JSON schema encoding and confuses consumers that treat missing-vs-null as a semantically distinct signal. Alexi's `apply_patch` does not currently emit an optional `movePath` field, but this rewrite prevents that class of regression from being reintroduced silently.

- **Package version bump to 1.22.8** (`package.json`): Patch release covering the daily upstream sync, JSON-encoding tolerance, and shared read-only bash constant.

### Maintenance

- **Upstream sync marker refreshed** (`.github/last-sync-commits.json`, 2026-09-01): Advances `kilocode` to `b6a2979e5911c2e6cf575fdc6efffb67dd5c94e7` and `opencode` to `ebece6efd7b11401cf1e7390b5a22991b6608cc4`; `claude-code` advances to `f275fa282e76c5e5456912268f2c367a7f4f4797`. Tracking metadata only; no source change beyond the ports listed above.

## [1.22.0] - 2026-08-24

### Added

- **`--image` flag for chat command** (`src/cli/commands/chat.ts`, PR #1509, commit `db2dd69b`, 2026-08-24): New `--image` flag enables image generation in chat sessions. Integrates with existing image_gen tool to provide multimodal capabilities via SAP AI Core.

### Changed

- **Dependency updates** (2026-08-24):
  - `puppeteer`: `25.7.0` → `25.8.0` (PR #1521) — patch update; used by webfetch tool for headless browser-based page fetching.
  - `@sap-ai-sdk/orchestration`: `2.14.0` → `2.15.0` (PR #1523) — minor update; core provider SDK for SAP AI Core integration.
  - `@inquirer/prompts`: `8.5.2` → `8.6.0` (PR #1520) — minor update; used by interactive CLI prompts.
  - `hono`: `4.13.2` → `4.13.3` (PR #1519) — patch update; used by embedded HTTP server.
  - `@vitejs/plugin-react`: `6.0.5` → `6.1.0` (PR #1517, dev-only) — minor update; vitest plugin for React/Ink TUI test environment.
  - `vitest`: updated in dev-dependencies group (PR #1516, dev-only) — test framework for unit and integration tests.

### Maintenance

- **Documentation updates** (2026-08-24):
  - Planning brief 2026-08-24 (docs/ci, commit `86661244`)
  - Architecture review 2026-08-24 with directory structure refresh (docs/core, commit `d38e1f06`)
- **Agent factory runs** (2026-08-24):
  - Consulting agent run (commit `cdface73`)
  - Security agent run (commit `6c6cab9e`)

### Added

- **`displayRole` per-message override for hidden instrumentation** (`src/core/sessionManager.ts`, `src/cli/tui/components/MessageArea.tsx`, `src/cli/session-replay.ts`, `src/core/agenticChat.ts`, `tests/orchestrator-hooks.test.ts`, `tests/cli/tui/transcript-display-role.test.tsx`, issue #1466, commit `cc2f76af`, 2026-08-21): New optional field `displayRole?: 'system' | 'user' | 'assistant'` on the `Message` interface overrides how a message is presented in user-facing transcripts (TUI `MessageArea`, `sessions export`, session replay) WITHOUT changing how the message is delivered to the model. Providers still receive the message with its logical `role`. Callers that want a message to reach the model but stay out of the transcript stamp it with `displayRole: 'system'`. `SessionManager.addMessage(role, content, tokens?, options?)` accepts either the raw `displayRole` string or an options object (`{ displayRole }`) as its fourth argument so existing three-argument call sites continue to compile. Auto-title generation (`activeSession.metadata.title` from the first user message) now skips messages carrying any `displayRole` value, so internal instrumentation cannot end up as the session title. `agenticChat` uses this for hook `contextModification` messages: the payload is still pushed onto the in-memory `messages` array delivered to the provider on the next iteration, and additionally persisted to the session with `displayRole: 'system'`. `MessageArea` filters out any `displayRole: 'system'` messages before rendering (empty-state placeholder appears when only hidden messages exist). `SessionReplay.replay(messages, opts)` also hard-hides `displayRole: 'system'` messages even when `showSystemMessages: true` — real `role: 'system'` messages are unaffected. New test files: `tests/cli/tui/transcript-display-role.test.tsx` (+167) and `tests/orchestrator-hooks.test.ts` `+55` (persistence + hidden-only rendering paths).

- **`InstanceWatcher` for per-instance filesystem watcher scoping** (`src/core/filesystem/watcher.ts`, `tests/core/filesystem/instance-watcher.test.ts`, port of kilocode `b8984e468`, 2026-08-21): The filesystem watcher's `Map<directory, disposer>` state is now scoped to an `InstanceWatcher` object rather than a module-level singleton. Two concurrent Alexi sessions (multiple SAP AI Core workspaces in the same process, or a headless `agent` command running alongside the interactive TUI) can now own independent watch sets — a `stop()` from one session no longer tears down the peer's watches. Public API: `class InstanceWatcher` with `start(location, subscribe): (() => void) | null`, `stop(directory): boolean`, `has(directory): boolean`, `size(): number`, `setDebounceTimer(directory, timer): void`, and `dispose(): void`. `start()` is idempotent per directory (a second call for the same directory returns the existing disposer) and honours the same VCS-guard + `ALEXI_EXPERIMENTAL_FILEWATCHER=1` flag as `maybeStartFileWatcher`. Backwards compatibility is preserved via a module-level `defaultInstance` and two shims: `startWatcher(location, subscribe)` (delegates to the default instance) and `getDefaultWatcherInstance()` (test-only accessor). New test file `tests/core/filesystem/instance-watcher.test.ts` (+152) exercises isolation between two instances, VCS-guard skips, experimental-flag skips, `stop()` scoping, and the debounce-timer replace semantics.

- **`InstanceWatcher.setDebounceTimer` shared debounce table** (`src/core/filesystem/watcher.ts`): New `setDebounceTimer(directory, timer)` method on `InstanceWatcher` gives watcher backends a shared per-instance debounce timer registry keyed by directory. Setting a timer for a directory clears any previously stored timer for the same directory (via `clearTimeout`). `dispose()` clears every pending timer alongside the watches. Rationale: previously each backend maintained its own `Map<string, NodeJS.Timeout>` privately and instance-level cleanup could leak timers across `dispose()`.

- **`InstanceWatcher.dispose()` idempotent teardown** (`src/core/filesystem/watcher.ts`): Iterates a snapshot of `watchers.values()` (each disposer mutates the map during iteration) so multiple `dispose()` calls on the same instance are safe. All pending debounce timers are cleared first, then every disposer is invoked in insertion order, then both maps are cleared. Safe to call from teardown paths that cannot easily know whether an instance was ever started.

- **`isXAICapacityError` and `isRetryableError` transient-error classifiers** (`src/core/error-backoff.ts`, `src/core/__tests__/error-backoff.test.ts`, port of opencode `71d08e9`, 2026-08-21): `isXAICapacityError(err)` returns true when `err.message` matches `/xai.*capacity|capacity.*exceeded/i` — xAI (and some SAP proxies fronting xAI-family models) occasionally emits a mid-stream "capacity exceeded" error that is semantically the same as a 5xx transient overload; retrying with backoff clears it. Detection is intentionally structural (message regex) because the upstream API does not attach a stable machine-readable code. `isRetryableError(err)` is a coarse "is this transient?" check that returns true for rate limits (`isRateLimitError`) OR xAI capacity errors, false for `null`/`undefined` and for permanent auth failures. Extend cautiously — a false positive here means real config failures get retried and waste provider budget. Fifteen new test cases (`src/core/__tests__/error-backoff.test.ts` +44) cover canonical / generic / case-insensitive matches, unrelated errors, rate-limit dispatch, permanent auth failures, and null / undefined inputs.

- **`agent` command headless permission auto-responder scaffolding** (`src/cli/commands/agent.ts`, upstream fix opencode `08faeb3`, 2026-08-21): The non-interactive `agent` command now subscribes to `PermissionRequested` on the event bus and publishes a `PermissionResponse` for every request — `granted: true` when `--yolo` or `--dangerously-skip-permissions` was passed, `granted: false` otherwise. Without this, a `PermissionRequested` event from a subagent (spawned via the `task` tool) has no listener in headless mode and the agent loop hangs waiting for a response that never arrives. A `subagentSessionIds: Set<string>` is populated (currently empty — the `task` tool does not yet spawn distinct sessions, so the wiring is reserved for a future real-subagent implementation that will gate the auto-response on sessionId membership without a second refactor). The subscription is cleaned up on `process.once('exit', ...)` so it does not leak into subsequent invocations under tests.

- **Experimental MCP Apps surface** (`src/mcp/apps.ts`, `src/mcp/index.ts`, port of kilocode `36c57c12c` with follow-up hardening in `c02134ab4` and `b7069922d`, 2026-08-21): New thin API wrapping `McpClientManager` presents each connected server as an "app" with two verbs — `mcpAppsListResources(server, manager?)` (enumerate what the server exposes) and `mcpAppsCallTool(server, tool, args, manager?)` (invoke a tool by qualified name). Gated behind the `ALEXI_EXPERIMENTAL_MCP_APPS=1` environment flag (`MCP_APPS_ENV_FLAG` and `isMCPAppsEnabled()` are always exported so callers can feature-detect without a dynamic import); the intent is to stabilise the shape before wiring it into a permanent HTTP surface. Error handling is tightened relative to raw `callTool`: any thrown value is wrapped in an `MCPAppsError` carrying `operation: 'listResources' | 'callTool'`, `server`, optional `tool`, and the underlying `cause` so upstream HTTP handlers can render a stable JSON envelope without inspecting the raw cause. `listResources` gracefully returns an empty array when the connected server does not implement the optional method. The module intentionally does not spawn its own MCP transport — it borrows the connection already owned by `McpClientManager`. `MCPResource` interface (`{ uri?, name?, mimeType?, [key: string]: unknown }`) is deliberately loose because the MCP resource contract has been evolving; callers pass this straight through to the HTTP client.

- **Cerebras completion-token cap plugin (builtin)** (`src/plugin/cerebras.ts`, port of opencode `e49772a`, 2026-08-21): Cerebras enforces a hard cap of `32_768` on `max_completion_tokens`; if a caller passes a higher value, the provider silently truncates the response without an error. The `CerebrasPlugin` (registered from the plugin bootstrap path) clamps any explicit `maxTokens` on a Cerebras-routed call to the documented ceiling so we never make a request that will be silently truncated. Public API: `CEREBRAS_MAX_COMPLETION_TOKENS = 32_768`, `isCerebrasTarget(providerOrModel): boolean` (recognises both direct provider ids and prefixed model ids like `cerebras/llama-3.1-70b`), `clampCerebrasMaxTokens(providerOrModel, maxTokens): number | undefined` (returns the original value unchanged for non-Cerebras targets or when `maxTokens` is undefined / already within bounds). Applicability to Alexi: since Alexi routes exclusively through SAP AI Core, there is no direct Cerebras provider under normal operation. The plugin ships as a builtin because (1) SAP AI Core proxy deployments MAY expose a Cerebras-family model id (`cerebras-*`) which the guard catches, and (2) users running the fork behind a custom proxy that adds Cerebras get the cap for free. No-op for every non-Cerebras provider.

- **Native OS notifications for task completion** (`src/core/notifications.ts`, `src/core/streamingOrchestrator.ts`, `src/tool/tools/bash.ts`, issue #1449, 2026-08-20): New module wraps `node-notifier` behind a permission-gated, best-effort dispatch layer that surfaces desktop notifications when a streaming chat completes cleanly or when a long-running bash command finishes. Public surface: `sendNotification(title, message, options?)` returns `Promise<boolean>` and never throws (dispatch failures resolve `false` after `logger.debug`); `notifyInBackground(title, message, options?)` is the fire-and-forget wrapper used by the orchestrator and bash tool; `getNotificationDecision()` / `setNotificationDecision(decision)` read and persist the `notifications` key in `~/.alexi/config.json`; `isInteractiveEnv()` returns `false` under `CI`, `ALEXI_NO_NOTIFICATIONS=1`, or when either standard stream is not a TTY; `LONG_RUNNING_THRESHOLD_MS` is exported at `30_000` so callers and tests share the same boundary. Type `NotificationDecision = 'allow' | 'deny' | 'ask'` — malformed values coerce to `'ask'`. Decision precedence resolves on every call so a user who flips `deny` to `allow` mid-session sees the change on the next completion event. On `'ask'` from a TTY, the module invokes an `@inquirer/prompts` confirm and persists the answer; a read-only home directory falls through to an in-memory decision without crashing. `node-notifier` is loaded via a cached dynamic import so users who deny never pay the native-binary probe cost. `SendNotificationOptions` supports `icon`, `sound`, `wait`, and two test-only escape hatches (`__notifierOverride`, `__askOverride`) that unit tests use to drive the dispatch layer without touching the real desktop environment. `streamChat` fires `notifyInBackground('Alexi', 'Task completed')` only on `completedCleanly` (aborts and provider errors stay silent); the bash tool fires `notifyInBackground('Command finished', description ?? command)` when either (a) the command detached and then exited, or (b) the foreground elapsed time crossed `LONG_RUNNING_THRESHOLD_MS`. `node-notifier@^10.0.1` and `@types/node-notifier@^8.0.5` were added as runtime and dev dependencies respectively.

- **PowerShell fail-fast bootstrap in `shellSpawnArgs`** (`src/tool/tools/shell/id.ts`, `src/tool/tools/bash.ts`, `src/tool/tools/shell.ts`, issue #1456, Cline PR #13358 pattern, 2026-08-20): The PowerShell branch of `shellSpawnArgs(info)` now returns `prefixArgs: ['-NoProfile', '-Command', "$ErrorActionPreference='Stop'; & {"]` and `suffixArgs: ['}']`, so the bash and shell tools spawn `[...prefixArgs, userCommand, ...suffixArgs]` with the user's script wrapped in a `& { ... }` scriptblock. This gives every PowerShell command fail-fast semantics — the first non-terminating error terminates with a non-zero exit and a single error record — while preserving three properties: the user command is byte-identical (no in-place string mutation), `param(...)` scripts still work because `param` retains its first-statement position INSIDE the scriptblock braces, and per-cmdlet opt-out via `-ErrorAction Continue` / `-ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` still restores the old non-fail-fast behaviour. The return type of `shellSpawnArgs` gained an optional `suffixArgs?: string[]` field; the `cmd.exe` and POSIX branches continue to return only `{ file, prefixArgs }`. Precedent: GitHub Actions prepends `$ErrorActionPreference = 'stop'` to every `powershell` / `pwsh` step, so model-authored PowerShell commands already ran under these semantics in CI — aligning the bash tool matches that contract. Deliberate tradeoff documented in the source: `Stop` promotes every non-terminating error, so `Get-ChildItem -Recurse` across an access-denied junction now aborts at the first denial instead of completing with warnings; on Windows PowerShell 5.1, in-script stderr redirection (`2>&1`, `2>file`) of a succeeding native command wraps each stderr line in `NativeCommandError` and terminates the script (PowerShell 7.2+ exempts this per PowerShell/PowerShell#3996). New end-to-end suite `tests/tool/tools/shell/powershell-fail-fast.test.ts` regression-guards the fail-fast, single-error-record, `-ErrorAction Continue` opt-out, and `param(...)`-compatibility behaviours; it self-skips when no `pwsh` / `powershell.exe` is on PATH so POSIX runners stay green.

- **Nullable-friendly Zod schema on `agent_manager` tool** (`src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts`): The `sessionId`, `worktreeId`, and every field of `config` (`mode`, `model`, `excludeLocalState`) now accept both `undefined` (omitted) AND explicit `null` at the schema layer. Strict providers (OpenAI structured output, SAP AI Core in strict mode) may emit `null` for absent optional fields; previously these payloads were rejected at the tool boundary and required provider-specific pre-processing. The `create` handler treats `config?.excludeLocalState ?? false` so both shapes mean "use default". The `config` object itself is also `.nullable().optional()`.

- **`todowrite` tool incremental-update guidance** (`src/tool/tools/todowrite.ts`): The tool description now explicitly states that (1) the tool replaces state — every call must send the FULL updated list, (2) exactly ONE task must be in `in_progress` at a time (mark the previous one `completed` in the same call you start the next), (3) completed tasks must be kept in the list so the user sees the trail of finished work, and (4) newly discovered follow-up tasks should be added as new `pending` items rather than editing the currently-running task's content. No behavioural change — the state machine was already like this — but the description now matches enforcement.

- **`background_process` tool sleep / wait semantics documentation** (`src/tool/tools/background-process.ts`): The tool description gained an explicit "Sleep / wait semantics" section that documents four facts models kept getting wrong: (1) the tool is NOT a sleep primitive — it returns as soon as the child has spawned and cannot be used to poll or wait; (2) port detection is asynchronous and the initial result may return before ports are populated; (3) the child process is detached and unref'd — it survives the tool call but will be terminated by `killAllTracked` on CLI shutdown; (4) callers who need to wait for readiness must poll from a separate shell / tool call. No behavioural change.

- **Real-time bash / shell command output streaming** (`src/tool/tools/bash.ts`, `src/tool/tools/bash-streaming.ts`, `src/bus/index.ts`, `src/cli/tui/context/ChatContext.tsx`, `src/cli/tui/hooks/useToolEvents.ts`, PR #1446, commit `350d1549`, 2026-08-19): The bash tool now emits incremental `stdout` / `stderr` chunks on the event bus as they arrive from the underlying process, so the TUI renders progress bars, `npm install` output, and long test runs live instead of the previous head-of-line-blocked spinner-only path (issue #1442). New surface:
  - **Event**: `BashOutputChunk` in `src/bus/index.ts:325` (event name `bash.output.chunk`) with payload `{ toolId: string; logId: string; stream: 'stdout' | 'stderr'; chunk: string; timestamp: number }`. `toolId` matches `ToolExecutionStarted` / `Completed` for row correlation; `logId` matches the id stored in the command-log registry and survives PID reuse. Fires strictly between `ToolExecutionStarted` and `ToolExecutionCompleted` for the same `toolId`. The final aggregated `stdout` / `stderr` are still returned in the normal `ToolExecutionCompleted` payload; consumers who don't care about live output can ignore this event entirely without changing behaviour.
  - **Command-log registry** (`src/tool/tools/bash-streaming.ts`): process-local store keyed by a synthetic `logId` (nanoid) rather than the OS PID, so a subsequent process that happens to reuse the same PID cannot collide with an earlier log entry. Retention: finished logs are kept for `COMPLETED_LOG_RETENTION_MS` (60s) after `markCommandLogFinished` so a TUI hub that briefly loses its subscription (probe outage, hot reload) can still fetch the tail on reconnect; entries are then dropped by `cleanupCompletedLogs` (auto-triggered on each new registration). Bounded memory: each log has an in-memory append buffer capped at `MAX_LOG_BYTES` (32 KB) — once the cap is reached, oldest bytes are dropped snapping forward to the next newline (within 1 KB) and a `[... older output evicted from streaming buffer ...]` marker is written in their place. Public API: `registerCommandLog`, `appendCommandLog`, `markCommandLogFinished`, `getCommandLog`, `getCommandLogByPid` (requires both `pid` AND `startedAt` for PID-reuse defence), `cleanupCommandLog`, `cleanupCompletedLogs`, `listCommandLogIds`.
  - **TUI wiring**: new `APPEND_TOOL_CALL_OUTPUT` reducer action (`src/cli/tui/context/ChatContext.tsx:117`) and `appendToolCallOutput(id, chunk)` context method live-append chunks to the running tool row's `output` without moving the entry between active / completed buckets. A chunk for a tool that already completed is silently dropped. On completion the `ToolExecutionCompleted` handler replaces `output` with the final aggregated payload from the tool result (which may be truncated / normalised differently — carriage returns, head-and-tail elision — than the raw streamed chunks). `useToolEvents` (`src/cli/tui/hooks/useToolEvents.ts`) subscribes to `BashOutputChunk` alongside the existing `ToolExecutionStarted` / `Completed` / `Failed` subscriptions.
  - **Bash tool integration** (`src/tool/tools/bash.ts`): registers the command log BEFORE any `data` handler fires, threads the `logId` through `stdout` / `stderr` decoders (`StringDecoder('utf8')`) so partial multi-byte sequences do not corrupt streamed chunks, publishes each decoded chunk via `BashOutputChunk`, and cleans up on `close` / `error` / abort. Telemetry-safe: a failing bus subscriber cannot tear down the running command — publish is wrapped in `try { … } catch { /* never let telemetry take down a running command */ }`.

- **SQLite connection PRAGMA helper** (`src/core/database/database.ts`): New adapter-agnostic module that produces the canonical ordered PRAGMA sequence for opening a shared SQLite database from multiple processes (e.g. the Alexi CLI and daemon running concurrently). The `CONNECTION_PRAGMAS` constant is a `readonly` frozen list: `PRAGMA busy_timeout = 5000` (installed FIRST so the busy handler is armed before WAL recovery can race), `PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL`, `PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL`, `PRAGMA cache_size = -64000`, `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON`, `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(PASSIVE)`. `configureConnection(db, options?)` applies the sequence via a minimal `PragmaRunner` interface (`{ run(sql: string): Promise<unknown> | unknown }`) so any binding — better-sqlite3, effect-sql, node:sqlite — can be wired up. `ownsWalInit` (default `true`) skips the `journal_mode = WAL` step for adapters that already ran it. SAP AI Core deployments frequently run the daemon and CLI in parallel against the same session store, so this ordering is a required stability guarantee.

- **State directory writability probing with sticky fallback** (`src/core/global/paths.ts`): New module resolves the Alexi state directory transparently on containers, restricted user profiles, and VS Code Server on Windows where the preferred `$XDG_STATE_HOME/alexi` is often unwritable. Previously the CLI crashed on startup with an opaque `EACCES` / `EROFS`. `resolveState(preferred, fallback?)` and its convenience wrapper `resolveStateDir(dataDir, preferred)` probe writability via an exclusive-mode temp file (`wx` flag, `.alexi-write-<pid>-<uuid>`, mode `0o600`) and fall back to `<dataDir>/state` when the preferred location fails. The fallback is **sticky**: once selected it is preferred on subsequent runs even when the primary location becomes writable again, so the resolved state directory does not flap between locations across restarts. When `$XDG_STATE_HOME` was explicitly set by the user, no fallback is provided — the user's explicit choice wins and any failure surfaces rather than silently redirecting. Mirrors the kilocode upstream fix for issue #13115.

- **Sandbox git-write escalation** (`src/kilocode/sandbox/git.ts`, `src/tool/tools/shell.ts`): On macOS `sandbox-exec` and analogous restricted environments (signalled by `ALEXI_SANDBOX=1`), git subcommands that mutate the working tree, index, refs, or config are now escalated through the interactive permission prompt so the user is aware their sandbox is about to be punched through. `isGitWrite(command)` classifies a command by walking past global flags (`-C`, `-c`, `--git-dir`, `--work-tree`) before checking the subcommand token against a broad allowlist of write-shaped git subcommands (`add`, `am`, `apply`, `branch`, `checkout`, `cherry-pick`, `clean`, `commit`, `config`, `fetch`, `gc`, `init`, `merge`, `mv`, `pull`, `push`, `rebase`, `reflog`, `remote`, `reset`, `restore`, `revert`, `rm`, `stash`, `submodule`, `switch`, `tag`, `worktree`). Read-only subcommands (`log`, `status`, `diff`, `show`, `ls-files`, `rev-parse`, …) are NOT included. `requiresSandboxEscalation(command, sandbox)` combines this with the sandbox flag so the shell tool can gate the prompt without pulling in permission plumbing.

- **Persistent snapshot-disable flag** (`src/core/snapshot.ts`): Users who disable snapshotting now have their choice survive a CLI restart. Previously this was an in-memory flag only. Stored in `~/.alexi/state/snapshot.json` under the JSON key `disabled`; a missing or unreadable file is treated as "not disabled" (snapshots on by default) so an unwritable state directory degrades gracefully rather than silently disabling snapshots. New exports: `disableSnapshots()`, `enableSnapshots()`, `shouldSnapshot()`, `SNAPSHOT_DISABLE_STATE_KEY` (`kilocode.snapshot.disabled`). The paired `pruneSnapshots(sessionId, keep = 20)` helper cleans stale snapshot / truncation files by `mtime`, oldest first.

- **Read-only mode enforcement in permission evaluator** (`src/permission/index.ts:63-109`): Modes that promise read-only behaviour to the user (`ask`, `plan`) now deny write-shaped tools (`write`, `edit`, `patch`, `shell`, `bash`, `kilo_edit`, `kilo_write`, `apply_patch`) even when a broad wildcard rule like `"*": "allow"` would otherwise match. An explicit per-tool `allow` still wins so operators can opt individual tools back in without regressing on the read-only guarantee. New export `evaluate({ tool, mode, rules })` returns the resolved decision and defaults to `'ask'` when no rule matches so behaviour is safe by default. Important for SAP AI Core compliance workflows that rely on `ask` / `plan` for reviewable read-only runs.

- **`toolId` threaded through tool execution context** (`src/tool/index.ts:38-46`): The synthetic tool-execution id assigned by `executeUnsafe` (carried by `ToolExecutionStarted` / `Completed` / `Failed`) is now exposed on `ToolContext` as `toolId?: string` so streaming-capable tools (bash / shell) can correlate incremental output chunks (`BashOutputChunk`) with the row the TUI is already rendering. `undefined` when a tool is invoked outside the standard registry (tests, one-shot calls).

- **`image_gen` tool — streaming progress events, inline base64 payloads, and error classification** (`src/tool/tools/image-gen.ts`, `src/bus/index.ts`, `src/tool/tools/__tests__/image-gen.test.ts`, PR #1431, commit `8d6b44cb`, 2026-08-18): The image-generation tool now consumes the provider stream progressively, emits an `ImageGenerationChunk` bus event (`image.generation.chunk`) for every image payload as it arrives, and supports an opt-in `returnBase64: true` parameter that returns the raw base64 blob inline on the result instead of writing a file to disk. New surface:
  - **Event**: `ImageGenerationChunk` in `src/bus/index.ts:329` with payload `{ model: string; index: number; kind: 'url' | 'base64'; mimeType?: string; sizeBytes?: number; timestamp: number }`. Subscribers see images the instant they materialise, before the provider stream ends. TUIs, MCP progress meters, and tests can attach without changing the tool call site.
  - **Parameter**: `returnBase64?: boolean` on the Zod schema. When `true`, decoded base64 payloads are returned inline on `ImageGenResultEntry.data` (a base64 string) and NOTHING is written to disk. When `false` or omitted, base64 payloads are decoded and written under `outputPath` / `$TMPDIR/alexi-images` and the result carries `path` only. URL payloads are unaffected by this flag. The `write` permission bucket is still evaluated against `outputPath` in inline mode so a caller cannot switch modes to bypass a disk-write deny rule.
  - **Result field**: `ImageGenResultEntry.data?: string` — new optional inline base64 payload, populated only when `returnBase64: true`. Exhaustive JSDoc keeps the discriminator behaviour (`kind: 'url'` vs `kind: 'base64'`) explicit at the type level.
  - **Helper**: `persistImageChunk(chunk, outputDir, inlineBase64 = false)` — the third parameter is new; when `true` the helper returns the base64 payload inline instead of persisting.
  - **Error classifier**: `classifyImageGenError(message)` (exported) — maps a raw provider error into `'rate-limit' | 'quota' | 'model-unavailable' | 'aborted' | 'other'`. First-match rules on lower-cased message: `rate limit`/`rate-limit`/`too many requests`/`429` -> `rate-limit`; `quota`/`insufficient_quota`/`billing`/`payment required`/`402` -> `quota`; `model_not_found`/`deployment_not_found`/`unknown model`/`not available`/`404` -> `model-unavailable`; otherwise `other`. Tool results carry a `hint` string built from the classification so agents and CLI users see an actionable message without re-parsing the raw error.
  - **Partial-success contract**: If the provider stream begins to emit images and then throws, the tool returns `success: true`, `truncated: true`, the images that DID arrive, and a hint of the form `"<kind hint> Partial result: N image(s) delivered before \"<raw error>\"."`. Callers that treat any error as fatal must also inspect `truncated`. Abort mid-stream surfaces `success: false` with `error: /aborted/i` and `data.images` containing any partial results captured before the abort landed.
  - **Test surface** (`src/tool/tools/__tests__/image-gen.test.ts`, `+241`): new suites cover the streaming event fan-out, partial-success on mid-stream failure, rate-limit / quota / model-unavailable / abort classification, inline base64 mode (asserts no files are written), URL-mode unaffected by `returnBase64`, every branch of `extensionForMimeType` (png/jpg/gif/webp/svg/bin), and a table-driven `classifyImageGenError` matrix.
