### Fixed

- **`InstanceWatcher` regression test import path corrected** (`tests/core/filesystem/instance-watcher.test.ts`, commit `b4fcb19a`, `fix(tests): correct import path in instance-watcher test [autohealing]`, 2026-08-21): The `import { InstanceWatcher, isExperimentalFileWatcherEnabled, getDefaultWatcherInstance, startWatcher, type WatchLocation } from '../../core/filesystem/watcher.js'` statement at `tests/core/filesystem/instance-watcher.test.ts:15` was pointing at a path that does not exist. The test file lives at `tests/core/filesystem/instance-watcher.test.ts` (three levels deep under the repository root), so the two-parent relative prefix `../../` resolves to `tests/core/filesystem/watcher.js` — a nonexistent sibling of the test itself, not the runtime module in `src/`. The correct prefix for a test at this depth is `../../../src/`, which walks out of `tests/core/filesystem/` back to the repo root and then into `src/core/filesystem/watcher.js`. Vitest's module resolver reported `Failed to resolve import "../../core/filesystem/watcher.js"` when the suite was collected, so all ten cases in the file — the two-instance state isolation regression from kilocode `b8984e468`, the idempotent-registration case, the `stop()` scoping case, the VCS-guard skip, the experimental-flag skip, the debounce-timer replace-and-cleanup case, and the backwards-compatible `startWatcher` shim delegation case — were failing at import-time rather than executing. Aggregate diff: `1 file changed, 1 insertion(+), 1 deletion(-)`. Pure test-import fix with zero runtime impact — the `InstanceWatcher` public surface (`start`, `stop`, `has`, `size`, `setDebounceTimer`, `dispose` on `src/core/filesystem/watcher.ts`), the module-level shims (`startWatcher`, `getDefaultWatcherInstance`), and the `isExperimentalFileWatcherEnabled` flag helper are all unchanged; the suite now correctly reaches them and re-runs the invariants added in the preceding `e42429f4` hardening pass (dead-assignment removal, `expect(w.size()).toBe(0)` post-dispose contract assertion). Consistent with the project-wide import convention documented in `AGENTS.md` under **ESM + import rules**: every local import ends in `.js` even from `.ts` files, and tests placed under nested `tests/**` subdirectories walk up to the repo root before descending into `src/` (`tests/core/filesystem/*` → `../../../src/`, `tests/tool/tools/*` → `../../../src/`, `tests/*` at the top of `tests/` → `../src/`). See `docs/TESTING.md#testing-instancewatcher-and-debounce-timer-cleanup` for the corrected code example and `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` under **Test import-path depth** for the general rule.
- **`InstanceWatcher` debounce-timer regression test hardened** (`tests/core/filesystem/instance-watcher.test.ts`, commit `e42429f4`, `fix(ci): remove unused assignment and apply prettier formatting [autohealing]`, 2026-08-21): The `'setDebounceTimer clears the previous timer for the same directory'` case at `tests/core/filesystem/instance-watcher.test.ts:137` previously declared a `let firstCleared = false` local, immediately reassigned it to `true`, and then asserted `expect(firstCleared).toBe(true)` — a tautology that produced an ESLint `no-unused-vars` warning (the value never left the test scope, and the assertion never observed a real state transition). CI autohealing removed the dead assignment and replaced the tautology with `expect(w.size()).toBe(0)` after `w.dispose()`, promoting the case from "does not hang the event loop" to "asserts the observable post-dispose contract of `InstanceWatcher` (`src/core/filesystem/watcher.ts:153`, `size()` returns the number of active watches on this instance): after two `setDebounceTimer('/tmp/x', ...)` calls followed by `dispose()`, the instance has zero registered watches." Aggregate diff: `1 file changed, 3 insertions(+), 5 deletions(-)`. Pure test-quality change: the `InstanceWatcher` public surface (`start`, `stop`, `dispose`, `size`, `has`, `setDebounceTimer` in `src/core/filesystem/watcher.ts`) and the `startWatcher` / `getDefaultWatcherInstance` / `isExperimentalFileWatcherEnabled` module-level exports are unchanged, and the other nine cases in the same file (idempotent-per-directory, VCS-guard skip, experimental-flag skip, two-instance isolation from kilocode `b8984e468`, `stop()` scoping, backwards-compatible shim) are unaffected. Test still guards the same load-bearing property — if `setDebounceTimer`'s second call were to leak the first timer, the 60s `setTimeout` would keep the Vitest event loop alive past the default 5s test timeout — but the new `size()` assertion now also guards the `dispose()` contract that `setDebounceTimer`-installed timers are cleared alongside the `watchers` map. See `docs/TESTING.md` under **Testing InstanceWatcher and Debounce-Timer Cleanup** for the full regression contract and `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` under **Autohealing** for the CI workflow that produced this commit.
- **CI auto-fix pass on `src/core/error-backoff.ts`, `src/mcp/index.ts`, `src/plugin/index.ts`, and `tests/core/filesystem/instance-watcher.test.ts`** (commit `363f574b`, `style(ci): auto-fix lint/format issues [alexi-bot]`, 2026-08-21): The CI auto-fix workflow ran Prettier across four files following the 2026-08-21 upstream sync (commit `d8b201cc`) and the two preceding hook-context feature merges (commits `cc2f76af`, `2b64a708`). Aggregate diff: `4 files changed, 10 insertions(+), 5 deletions(-)`. Concrete changes: (1) `src/core/error-backoff.ts` — removed a stray blank line between the `isRetryableError(err)` function body (`return isRateLimitError(err) || isXAICapacityError(err)`) and the following JSDoc block for the `Retry-After` header helper; the transient-vs-permanent classifier contract (rate-limit / xAI capacity errors return `true`, everything else `false`) is unchanged. (2) `src/mcp/index.ts` — stripped a trailing blank line after the experimental MCP Apps re-export block (`isMCPAppsEnabled`, `mcpAppsListResources`, `mcpAppsCallTool`, `MCPAppsError`, `MCP_APPS_ENV_FLAG`, `type MCPResource`); the barrel's exported surface is unchanged (`McpServerAdapter`, `createMcpServer`, `McpClientManager`, `getMcpClientManager`, `McpConnectOptions`, `loadMcpConfig`, `saveMcpConfig`, `McpServerConfig`, `McpConfig` remain on the primary line, and the experimental-apps exports remain feature-detectable via `isMCPAppsEnabled()` gated on `ALEXI_EXPERIMENTAL_MCP_APPS=1`). (3) `src/plugin/index.ts` — reflowed the final `export { CerebrasPlugin, CEREBRAS_MAX_COMPLETION_TOKENS, clampCerebrasMaxTokens, isCerebrasTarget } from './cerebras.js'` re-export from a single line onto a five-line multi-line form (`export {` + one name per line + `} from './cerebras.js'`), because the single-line version exceeded the 100-column `printWidth`. The four exported names, their source module (`./cerebras.js`), and the surrounding `// ============ Builtin Plugins ============` header comment are unchanged; callers using `getPluginManager().load(...)` continue to register the Cerebras plugin without importing the concrete module directly. (4) `tests/core/filesystem/instance-watcher.test.ts` — reflowed a single `w.setDebounceTimer('/tmp/x', setTimeout(() => {}, 60_000))` call in the `'setDebounceTimer clears the previous timer for the same directory'` test onto a three-line multi-line form (`w.setDebounceTimer(` + `'/tmp/x',` + `setTimeout(...)` + `)`), again to satisfy `printWidth: 100`. The test's assertion — that scheduling two 60-second timers on the same directory key and calling `w.dispose()` clears both (so the event loop is not kept alive) — is unchanged, and the surrounding `firstCleared` sentinel-observation pattern is preserved verbatim. Pure formatting change with zero runtime, type, or public-API impact on any of the four files. Companion to the upstream-sync commit and hook-context PRs that landed in the same day; no re-testing beyond the standard `lint -> typecheck -> format:check -> test:coverage -> build` gate is required. See `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` under **Style Auto-Fix** for the CI mechanism that produced this commit.
- **CI auto-fix pass on `src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts`** (commit `cd5bc5f0`, `style(ci): auto-fix lint/format issues [alexi-bot]`, 2026-08-20): The CI auto-fix workflow ran Prettier on the agent-manager tool module and collapsed two hand-authored five-line Zod field definitions onto single lines to satisfy the 100-column `printWidth`. Specifically, the `sessionId` and `worktreeId` fields on `AgentManagerParamsSchema` (`src/tool/tools/agent-manager.ts:14-15`) — each previously broken across five lines as `z.string()` -> `.nullable()` -> `.optional()` -> `.describe(...)` — were reflowed onto single-line `z.string().nullable().optional().describe('...')` chains. Both resulting lines fit comfortably within `printWidth: 100` (89 and 87 columns respectively), so Prettier prefers the compact form. Aggregate diff: `1 file changed, 2 insertions(+), 10 deletions(-)`. Pure formatting change with zero runtime, type, or public-API impact — the `AgentManagerParamsSchema` Zod schema still accepts `null` OR omitted values for both fields (the nullable-friendly contract documented in the leading comment: "strict providers (OpenAI structured output, SAP AI Core in strict mode) may omit optional fields entirely OR pass explicit `null`"), and the `agent_manager` tool's exported surface — `action: 'create' | 'list' | 'stop' | 'status'`, `permission: { action: 'admin', getResource: (params) => params.action }`, and the `AgentManagerResult` interface (`action`, `sessions?`, `session?`, `message?`) — is unchanged. The paired `config` object field on the same schema (which is nested and does not fit on one line at 100 columns) is untouched. See `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` under **Style Auto-Fix** for the CI mechanism that produced this commit.
- **CI auto-fix pass on `src/core/snapshot.ts`** (commit `665f7383`, `style(ci): auto-fix lint/format issues [alexi-bot]`, 2026-08-19): The CI auto-fix workflow ran Prettier on the session-snapshot module and removed a single trailing blank line at the end of the file, dropping the file from 335 to 334 lines. Aggregate diff: `1 file changed, 1 deletion(-)`. Pure formatting change with zero runtime, type, or public-API impact — the module's exported surface is unchanged: `writeSnapshot(sessionId, payload)`, `listSnapshots(sessionId)`, `readSnapshot(sessionId, id)`, `deleteSnapshot(sessionId, id)`, and `pruneSnapshots(sessionId, keep = 20)` all retain their signatures, return shapes, and semantics. In particular, `pruneSnapshots` (`src/core/snapshot.ts:316`) still sorts snapshot files by `mtimeMs` ascending, deletes the oldest `length - keep` entries with best-effort `fs.unlink` (swallowing `ENOENT`-style races), and returns the array of deleted file names — the ordering, the `keep = 20` default (matching the retention policy referenced by the `alexi revert` UX), and the silent-on-missing-file behaviour are all preserved. The trailing `// alexi_change end` sentinel comment on the final line is untouched; only the empty line that followed it was removed. No consumers in `src/undo/`, `src/core/sessionManager.ts`, or the `sessions` / `session-export` CLI commands need to change. See `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` under **Style Auto-Fix** for the CI mechanism that produced this commit.
- **CI auto-fix pass on `src/config/userConfig.ts`** (commit `576ea3d2`, `style(ci): auto-fix lint/format issues [alexi-bot]`, 2026-08-18): The CI auto-fix workflow ran Prettier on the MCP tool-display config module, collapsing a hand-authored three-line break of the `throw new Error(...)` in `setConfigMcpToolDisplay(display: McpToolDisplay)` onto a single line to satisfy the 100-column `printWidth` (`src/config/userConfig.ts:278`). Aggregate diff: `1 file changed, 1 insertion(+), 3 deletions(-)`. Pure formatting change with no runtime, validation, or type-safety impact — `setConfigMcpToolDisplay` still narrows `display` to the `McpToolDisplay = 'expanded' | 'collapsed'` union at compile time, still throws at runtime for any value outside the union (defence-in-depth against callers that erase the type via `as McpToolDisplay` or dynamic import), still emits the same error message shape (`mcpToolDisplay must be 'expanded' or 'collapsed' (got '<value>')`), and still delegates the persistent write to `setConfigValue('mcpToolDisplay', display)`. The paired reader `getConfigMcpToolDisplay()` (line 267) is unchanged: it still reads `config.mcpToolDisplay ?? config.mcp_tool_display` (accepting both the camelCase key and the legacy snake_case key emitted by upstream-serialized configs) and falls back to `'collapsed'` for any non-union value so a corrupt config never crashes the Ink TUI. The template-literal error string (`` `mcpToolDisplay must be 'expanded' or 'collapsed' (got '${String(display)}')` ``) fits at 92 columns, well within `printWidth: 100`, so Prettier prefers the single-line form. See `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` under **Style Auto-Fix** for the CI mechanism.

## [1.21.0] - 2026-08-17

### Added

- **MCP per-server timeout validation and documentation** (`src/config/mcp.ts`, `docs/mcp-servers.md`, PR #1427, commit `bcfe252d`, 2026-08-17): Introduced validation for per-server `timeout` configuration in `mcp-servers.json` to ensure values are positive integers. The config loader now rejects invalid timeout values (negative, zero, non-numeric) with a descriptive error message. Documentation in `docs/mcp-servers.md` clarifies the precedence order: per-server `timeout` field overrides the global default (3s), and the global default can be overridden via `ALEXI_MCP_TIMEOUT` environment variable. User-visible impact: invalid MCP server configs now fail fast at session startup rather than silently falling back to the default timeout.

### Changed

- **Dependency updates** (2026-08-17):
  - `@commitlint/cli`: `21.2.1` → `21.2.2` (PR #1423, commit `cf7b9a75`) — patch update; dev-only dependency used for commit message linting via the husky `commit-msg` hook. No runtime impact.
  - `puppeteer`: `25.5.0` → `25.7.0` (PR #1424, commit `09224c4a`) — patch update with no breaking changes; used by `src/tool/tools/webfetch.ts` for headless browser-based page fetching.
  - `@hono/node-server`: `2.1.0` → `2.1.1` (PR #1426, commit `b26f56cf`) — patch update; used by `src/server/index.ts` for the embedded HTTP server. No breaking changes.
  - `@typescript-eslint/eslint-plugin`: `8.66.0` → `8.67.0` (PR #1425, commit `14036e30`) — patch update; dev-only dependency for TypeScript-specific ESLint rules. No runtime impact.
  - `hono`: `4.13.1` → `4.13.2` (PR #1422, commit `dee8662b`) — patch update; used by `src/server/index.ts` for HTTP routing. No breaking changes.
  - `@commitlint/config-conventional`: `21.0.0` → `21.1.0` (PR #1420, commit `ea126671`) — minor version bump; dev-only dependency for Conventional Commits enforcement. No runtime impact.
  - `tsx`: `4.23.10` → `4.23.12` (PR #1421, commit `e61a1f7c`) — patch update; dev-only dependency used by `npm run dev`. No runtime impact.
  - `mammoth`: `1.12.0` → `1.12.1` (PR #1418, commit `d54ba9f3`) — patch update; used for Word document conversion in the document tool.
  - Dev dependencies group update (PR #1417, commit `f272037a`) — batch update of development tooling dependencies with no runtime impact.

- **Agent fleet maintenance** (2026-08-17):
  - Factory engineering workflow execution (`chore(agent): factory engineering run [agent]`, PR #1416, commit `dcf199a9`).
  - Factory architecture workflow execution (`chore(agent): factory architecture run [agent]`, commit `8cebe2a1`).
  - Factory consulting workflow execution (`chore(ci): factory consulting run [agent]`, commit `af287da2`).

### Added

- **`packages/alexi-mcp-warpgrep` — standalone MCP server for semantic code search** (`packages/alexi-mcp-warpgrep/`, commit `49692298`, 2026-08-13): New in-repo npm package that exposes the `codebase_search` (WarpGrep) tool over the Model Context Protocol using stdio transport. Public surface: `createWarpgrepServer({ repoRoot? })` returns a configured but unstarted `McpServer` (exported for tests); `main()` boots the server on stdio; `warpgrepExecute(params, { repoRoot? })` runs the search without going through the MCP layer and returns a `WarpGrepOutcome` discriminated union (`{ success: true; data: WarpGrepResult; hint? } | { success: false; error }`) that never throws; `WarpGrepParamsSchema` (Zod, `{ query: string }`) and the `WARPGREP_DESCRIPTION` string are shared with any downstream integrator. `@morphllm/morphsdk` is a **peer** dependency marked optional via `peerDependenciesMeta` — the server responds with a helpful `Search failed: WarpGrep requires @morphllm/morphsdk to be installed` message instead of crashing when the SDK is absent. Tests inject a stub constructor via `setWarpgrepClientLoader(loader)` / `resetWarpgrepClientLoader()`. Bin `alexi-mcp-warpgrep` (mapped to `dist/index.js`) is auto-invoked only when the module runs as the entry point (`import.meta.url === new URL(\\`file://${process.argv[1]}\\`).href`), so `import`-ing the module in tests does not spawn the transport. Registration guidance lives in `docs/mcp-servers.md#alexi-mcp-warpgrep---semantic-code-search`.

- **TUI tool-call disclosure and syntax-highlighted diffs** (`src/cli/tui/components/ToolRow.tsx`, `src/cli/tui/utils/formatToolOutput.ts`, `src/cli/tui/components/DiffView.tsx`, PR #1368, commit `e97a4645`, 2026-08-12): Introduced a new `ToolRow` component that replaces `ToolCallBlock` as the primary renderer for tool calls in the Ink TUI. Behaviour highlights: (1) rows auto-expand on `failed` status so users see errors without interacting, (2) bash tool output renders with a classic terminal-style `$ command` prefix produced by `formatBashCommand(command)`, (3) edit-tool diffs render through `DiffView`, which now applies best-effort syntax highlighting via `cli-highlight` when a language can be inferred from the file path (`ts`/`tsx` → `typescript`, `py` → `python`, `rs` → `rust`, `yml`/`yaml` → `yaml`, `sh`/`bash` → `bash`, etc.), falling back to plain text on any highlighter failure so a broken grammar never breaks the render. Row-level colors follow status via three new theme tokens: `colors.toolRunning`, `colors.toolCompleted`, `colors.toolFailed` (added to both `src/cli/tui/theme/dark.ts` and `src/cli/tui/theme/light.ts` and declared on `ThemeColors` in `src/cli/tui/theme/types.ts`). `ToolCallBlock` is retained as a thin backwards-compatible wrapper around `ToolRow` — its public prop type (`ToolCallBlockProps = ToolRowProps`) is preserved verbatim so existing callers (`src/cli/tui/components/MessageArea.tsx` and its tests) do not need to change. Pure string helpers (`formatBashCommand`, `truncateOutput`, `formatParamsPreview`, `formatDuration`, `guessLanguageFromPath`) live in `src/cli/tui/utils/formatToolOutput.ts` so they can be unit-tested without an Ink render harness (`tests/cli/tui/formatToolOutput.test.ts`).

- **Agent config permission expansion (`expandPermissions`)** (`src/agent/permissions-expand.ts`, 2026-08-12): New pure helper that normalizes agent permission entries against `$HOME` before they reach the permission matcher. Rules: `~` and `~/...` resolve against the user's home directory; absolute paths pass through untouched; relative paths pass through untouched (they resolve at match time against the workspace, not `$HOME`). The set of recognized path-shaped actions is exported as `PATH_ACTIONS = ['external_directory', 'read', 'edit'] as const`, with a derived `PathAction` string-literal union type. Entries whose `action` is not in `PATH_ACTIONS` pass through unchanged, so `execute`/`network`/other non-path actions are unaffected (their `path`/`resource` field is a command or host, not a filesystem location). Never mutates its input. Concrete `PermissionRuleSchema` (Zod) remains in `src/permission/index.ts`; this helper is intentionally structural (`AgentPermissionEntry { action: string; path?: string; [key: string]: unknown }`) so it does not force a schema on callers.

- **Config instance cache invalidation (`invalidateGlobalConfig`)** (`src/config/invalidation.ts`, 2026-08-12): New registry-based helper that ports kilocode upstream `19a2a3c4d`. Modules that maintain a config-derived cache (routing config, provider config, permission ruleset, etc.) register a disposer via `registerInstanceCache(dispose)` and receive a flush signal when `invalidateGlobalConfig()` is called. `updateGlobal(updates, { dispose: true })` in `src/config/userConfig.ts` now performs a dynamic import of `./invalidation.js` and calls `invalidateGlobalConfig()` after writing the updated config to disk, ensuring in-flight sessions see fresh SAP AI Core credentials, routing rewrites, and permission changes without a restart. `dispose: false` opts out. Errors thrown by individual disposers are caught and logged via `console.warn` so a misbehaving disposer never blocks the rest of the flush. `_instanceCacheCount()` is exposed as an `@internal` test helper.

- **Serialized concurrent database migrations (`applyMigrations`)** (`src/core/database/migration.ts`, 2026-08-12): New migration runner that ports kilocode upstream fix `2c2b0a2ff` (fix(core): serialize concurrent database migrations #13067). Prevents primary-key collisions when two processes (e.g. the SAP AI Core worker and the CLI) race to apply the same migration: the runner takes an IMMEDIATE write lock via `db.transactionImmediate` and re-checks `tx.has(migration.id)` inside the transaction before applying. Callers implement the narrow `MigrationDb` / `MigrationTx` interfaces so the module does not hard-couple to a specific SQL adapter (better-sqlite3, effect-sql, or raw pg). The initial snapshot from `db.completedIds()` skips already-applied migrations without paying the write-lock cost.

- **VCS-guarded filesystem watcher (`maybeStartFileWatcher`)** (`src/core/filesystem/watcher.ts`, 2026-08-12): New helper that only initializes the filesystem watcher when the workspace location has VCS metadata AND the experimental flag `ALEXI_EXPERIMENTAL_FILEWATCHER=1` is set. Prevents crashes and excessive polling in SAP AI Core sandboxed workspaces that may not be git repositories. The `subscribe` callback is injected so the module stays independent of the concrete watcher backend (chokidar, native `fs.watch`, or an Effect-based stream). Returns a disposer or `null` when the watcher was skipped for either reason. `isExperimentalFileWatcherEnabled()` is exported separately so tests can override `process.env` between cases.

- **Session retry with bounded exponential backoff + full jitter (`withRetry`)** (`src/core/session/retry.ts`, 2026-08-12): New retry helper that ports opencode commit `c789868`. Prevents runaway retry loops that exhaust SAP AI Core deployment quotas and burn tokens. Formula: `delay(attempt) = min(maxMs, baseMs * 2^attempt)`, then full-jitter (`random() * delay`) — see AWS "Exponential Backoff and Jitter" (2015) for the rationale (full jitter minimises thundering-herd collisions when many clients retry in the same window, e.g. after a 429 or 503 storm). Defaults are tuned for interactive chat: 8 attempts × 30s cap ≈ 4 minutes worst case. `computeDelay(attempt, opts)` is exported separately so tests can assert the backoff curve. Callers supply a `shouldRetry` predicate so the module stays classifier-agnostic; the transient-vs-permanent contract lives in `AGENTS.md` and is implemented in `src/core/error-backoff.ts`. `jitter: false` opts out for deterministic tests.

- **Reasoning-variant derivation and base/custom model merge** (`src/providers/transform.ts`, 2026-08-12): Two new helpers port kilocode upstream `031ea2feb`. (1) `deriveReasoningVariants<T>(model)` accepts a base model with a `reasoning.efforts: readonly string[]` array and returns the base model followed by one variant per effort (id suffixed with `-<effort>`, e.g. `gpt-5`, `gpt-5-low`, `gpt-5-medium`, `gpt-5-high`); the derived `defaultEffort` is set on each variant's `reasoning` object so downstream code can pick the effort explicitly. When the model has no `reasoning.efforts`, the base is returned unchanged. Never mutates its input. (2) `mergeProviderModels(base, custom)` merges a custom provider's model map on top of a base provider's model map without wiping base variants — custom entries win per-id; base variants (e.g. `-reasoning-high`) survive when the custom map does not redefine the same id. Fixes the previous behaviour where the custom map replaced the base map wholesale and reasoning variants disappeared. `ModelInfoLike` is exported as a deliberately loose structural type so callers using either the SAP orchestration model records or a custom `ModelInfo` shape can both use these helpers without a coercion.

### Changed

- **Compaction summary prompt hardened for cross-agent handoff** (`src/core/compaction.ts`, commit `49692298`, 2026-08-13): `SUMMARY_PROMPT` was rewritten from a permissive "summarize this conversation" instruction into a structured contract targeted at another coding agent consuming the summary. New requirements baked into the prompt: (1) preamble identifies the model as a "context summarization agent" whose consumer is another coding agent; (2) the five-section format (KEY DECISIONS, FILES CHANGED, CONTEXT, CURRENT STATE, USER INSTRUCTIONS) is now marked mandatory ("Always follow the exact output structure above. Keep every section, preserve exact file paths and identifiers when known, and prefer terse bullets over paragraphs"); (3) the FILES CHANGED section now explicitly demands "(preserve exact paths and identifiers)" so downstream `edit`/`read` calls do not lose target precision; (4) three explicit prohibitions block the common failure modes of a summarizing model: "Do not continue the conversation. Do not respond to any questions in the conversation. Do not mention that you are summarizing, compacting, or merging context." No API change — `SUMMARY_PROMPT` is a private module-level constant consumed by the existing `compactConversation` flow; `POST_COMPACT_TARGET_FRACTION` (0.8), `MAX_TOOL_OUTPUT_LENGTH` (50000), and `PRUNED_TOOL_MARKER` are unchanged.

- **Built-in `codebase_search` (WarpGrep) tool removed from the default registry** (`src/tool/tools/warpgrep.ts`, `src/tool/tools/index.ts`, `src/tool/warpgrep.ts`, commit `49692298`, 2026-08-13): The built-in semantic-search tool has been deleted from `builtInTools` in `src/tool/tools/index.ts`. `src/tool/tools/warpgrep.ts` now only exports `isWarpgrepAvailable()` (used by the tool registry to decide whether to append the "install `@morphllm/morphsdk` for semantic search" hint to `grep`'s description) and a no-op `_resetWarpgrepDeprecationWarning()` shim retained for test compatibility. Importing `warpgrepTool` fails at build time — that is the intentional signal for any leftover caller. Rationale: the built-in implementation relied on the proprietary Morph API and a free-period proxy (`https://api.kilo.ai/api/gateway`) that is not appropriate for Alexi's SAP AI Core context (data locality, licensing, and free-tier bootstrapping do not apply). **Migration:** register the new `alexi-mcp-warpgrep` MCP server in `mcp-servers.json` (see `docs/mcp-servers.md`). The tool name (`codebase_search`) and parameter shape (`{ query: string }`) are preserved so agent prompts and skills do not need updating. `src/tool/tools/glob.ts` and `src/tool/tools/grep.ts` had their inline "use `codebase_search` first" guidance updated to point at the MCP server registration instead of a built-in tool.

- **`src/cli/tui/components/MessageArea.tsx` renders `ToolRow` instead of `ToolCallBlock`** (commit `e97a4645`, 2026-08-12): Both the `msg.toolCalls.map(...)` and the top-level `activeToolCalls.map(...)` render paths now import and instantiate `ToolRow` directly from `./ToolRow.js`. Public prop shape unchanged (`ToolCallBlockProps = ToolRowProps`); `ToolCallBlock` remains exported as a thin wrapper for external consumers that import it by name.
- **`src/cli/tui/components/ToolCallBlock.tsx` is now a compatibility wrapper** (commit `e97a4645`, 2026-08-12): Reduced from ~193 lines of standalone rendering logic to a ~4-line component that delegates to `ToolRow`. Historically owned per-status colors, tool icons, bash-output framing, and diff rendering; those concerns now live in `ToolRow` so a single component owns disclosure state, terminal-style command output, and syntax-highlighted diffs, and can be reused by consumers that want per-call rendering.
- **`src/reference/index.ts` re-exports `canonicalizeRepoPath`** (2026-08-12): The `canonicalizeRepoPath` helper (already exported from `src/reference/repository-cache.ts` for Windows case-insensitive filesystem cache key normalization; ports kilocode upstream `33c45bd78`, `343491e35`, `712346975`, `e958d4486`) is now surfaced through the `src/reference/index.ts` barrel alongside `RepositoryCacheService`, `initRepositoryCache`, `getRepositoryCache`, and `RepositoryCache`. External consumers can now `import { canonicalizeRepoPath } from '@alexi/reference'` without reaching into `./repository-cache.js` directly.

### Removed

- **Built-in `warpgrepTool` export from `src/tool/tools/warpgrep.ts`** (commit `49692298`, 2026-08-13): The `warpgrepTool` binding and its `defineTool`-based implementation are gone. `src/tool/tools/index.ts` no longer imports or re-exports `warpgrepTool`, and the tool is no longer appended to `builtInTools`. The `zod`, `defineTool`, `ToolResult`, and `Telemetry` imports were removed from `src/tool/tools/warpgrep.ts`; only `isWarpgrepAvailable()` and the no-op `_resetWarpgrepDeprecationWarning()` shim remain. Consumers requiring semantic codebase search must register the `alexi-mcp-warpgrep` MCP server (see `docs/mcp-servers.md`). The `tests/tool/tools/warpgrep-deprecation.test.ts` suite pins this removal contract (`warpgrepTool` is `undefined`, `isWarpgrepAvailable` is a callable returning `false` under test, `_resetWarpgrepDeprecationWarning` is a callable no-op).

### Fixed

- **CI auto-fix pass on `tests/tool/tools/warpgrep.test.ts`** (commit `2b2e5830`, `style(ci): auto-fix lint/format issues [alexi-bot]`, 2026-08-13): The CI auto-fix workflow ran Prettier on the WarpGrep registry-contract test, collapsing a hand-authored three-line break of the `vi.importActual<T>()` generic type argument onto a single line to satisfy the 100-column `printWidth`. Aggregate diff: `1 file changed, 3 insertions(+), 3 deletions(-)`. Pure formatting change with no behavioural, assertion-semantics, mock-scope, or coverage impact — the `describe('WarpGrep built-in tool - removed from registry', ...)` suite still pins the same three-part contract for the retired `codebase_search` built-in tool: (a) it is absent from `builtInTools` when `@morphllm/morphsdk` is unavailable, (b) the `grep` tool description (both static and via `toFunctionSchema()`) still surfaces the `'Note: For semantic code search, install @morphllm/morphsdk'` install hint, and (c) it remains absent from `builtInTools` even when the SDK is present because semantic search is deliberately migrated to the standalone `alexi-mcp-warpgrep` MCP server (`packages/alexi-mcp-warpgrep/`). Affected file:
  - **`tests/tool/tools/warpgrep.test.ts`** (`+3/-3`, around line 49-52): The four-line multi-line form `const actual = await vi.importActual<\n  typeof import('../../../src/tool/tools/warpgrep.js')\n>('../../../src/tool/tools/warpgrep.js');` inside the `vi.doMock('../../../src/tool/tools/warpgrep.js', ...)` factory for the "remains absent from builtInTools even when morphsdk resolves" case (line 46-62) was collapsed onto the canonical two-line form `const actual = await vi.importActual<typeof import('../../../src/tool/tools/warpgrep.js')>(\n  '../../../src/tool/tools/warpgrep.js'\n);` — the `<...>` type-argument line fits at 96 columns, well within `printWidth: 100`. Prettier prefers to keep the generic type argument on the same line as the identifier and break only after the `(` for the runtime argument. Type argument (`typeof import('../../../src/tool/tools/warpgrep.js')`) preserved verbatim; the `...actual` spread and the `isWarpgrepAvailable: () => true` override still return the same mock module shape; the subsequent `await import('../../../src/tool/tools/index.js')` still resolves through the mocked module graph and still asserts `toolNames).not.toContain('codebase_search')` on the freshly-imported `builtInTools` array.

  `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run format:check`, `npm run build`, and `npm test` remain green on the branch — this pass is the mechanical follow-up to a hand-edit that had used multi-line formatting where Prettier's `printWidth: 100` policy prefers keeping the generic type argument on the same line as the call. See `docs/TESTING.md` under **Test File Formatting** (pattern 3, `vi.importActual<T>()`) for the standing pattern and `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` under **Style Auto-Fix** for the CI mechanism.

- **CI auto-fix pass on three test files under `tests/config/`, `tests/providers/`, and `tests/session/`** (commit `9e2b9ca6`, `style(ci): auto-fix lint/format issues [alexi-bot]`, 2026-08-12): The CI auto-fix workflow ran Prettier and ESLint `--fix` on three Vitest test files, collapsing multi-line imports and multi-line `await expect(...)` chains onto single lines to satisfy the 100-column `printWidth` and removing a stale `// eslint-disable-next-line no-console` comment above a `vi.spyOn(console, 'warn')` call (the `no-console` rule does not fire on `console` object property access via `vi.spyOn`, so the pragma was inert). Aggregate diff: `3 files changed, 4 insertions(+), 11 deletions(-)`. Pure formatting change with no behavioural, test-coverage, assertion-semantics, provider-routing, session-persistence, tool-registry, event-bus, or Ink TUI impact — every `it()` block still runs with identical inputs, asserts on identical outputs, and exercises the same public surface of `src/providers/transform.ts` (`deriveReasoningVariants`, `mergeProviderModels`) and `src/core/session/retry.ts` (`withRetry`, `computeDelay`). Affected files:
  - **`tests/config/global-invalidation.test.ts`** (`+1/-1`, around line 56-58): Removed a `// eslint-disable-next-line no-console` pragma directly above `const warn = vi.spyOn(console, 'warn').mockImplementation(() => {});` (replaced with a blank line). The `no-console` ESLint rule targets the `console.*` call surface, not `vi.spyOn(console, 'warn')` which manipulates the `console` object via property reference — the pragma was inert. The `warn` spy continues to silence the `console.warn` emitted by the swallowed error inside `invalidateGlobalConfig()` (canonical `registerInstanceCache` + `invalidateGlobalConfig` teardown pattern documented in `src/config/global-cache.ts`). Test still asserts `expect(() => invalidateGlobalConfig()).not.toThrow()`.
  - **`tests/providers/reasoning-variants.test.ts`** (`+1/-4`, around line 9): Collapsed the multi-line named-import block `import {\n  deriveReasoningVariants,\n  mergeProviderModels,\n} from '../../src/providers/transform.js';` onto a single line `import { deriveReasoningVariants, mergeProviderModels } from '../../src/providers/transform.js';` — the resulting line is 90 columns, well within `printWidth: 100`. Prettier prefers the single-line form whenever it fits. Import surface unchanged: still targets the same two named exports (`deriveReasoningVariants`, `mergeProviderModels`) from `src/providers/transform.ts` and still resolves under the project's `NodeNext` module-resolution policy with the mandatory `.js` extension.
  - **`tests/session/retry.test.ts`** (`+2/-6`, around lines 56 and 63): Collapsed two multi-line `await expect(...)` chains onto single lines. The transient-error case (`await expect(withRetry(fn, () => true, { maxAttempts: 3, baseMs: 1 })).rejects.toBe(err);`) and the permanent-error case (`await expect(withRetry(fn, () => false, { maxAttempts: 5, baseMs: 1 })).rejects.toBe(err);`) both fit under 100 columns as single lines. The tests continue to verify the bounded-retry contract from `src/core/session/retry.ts`: `withRetry(fn, shouldRetry, opts)` retries a transient failure up to `maxAttempts` times (default caller-supplied) and stops immediately when `shouldRetry` returns `false`. No change to the assertion (`.rejects.toBe(err)`) or the call-count expectation (`expect(fn).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(3)` / `expect(fn).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1)`).

See `docs/TESTING.md` under **Test File Formatting** for the standing pattern and `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` under **Style Auto-Fix** for the CI mechanism.

- **CI auto-fix pass on `src/permission/index.ts` and `src/providers/sapOrchestration.ts`** (commit `cf7e01de`, `style(ci): auto-fix lint/format issues [alexi-bot]`, 2026-08-11): The CI auto-fix workflow ran Prettier on two runtime source files, collapsing long multi-line expressions onto single lines to satisfy the 100-column `printWidth` while removing the multi-line breaks that had been introduced by hand-edits. Aggregate diff: `2 files changed, 9 insertions(+), 10 deletions(-)`. Pure formatting change with no behavioural, API, CLI-surface, runtime, provider-routing, session-persistence, tool-registry, event-bus, or Ink TUI impact. Affected files:
  - **`src/permission/index.ts`** (`+1/-4`, `matchedPattern` fallback chain inside the last-match rule provenance block, around line 626-629): The five-line `rule.tools?.[0] ?? rule.paths?.[0] ?? rule.commands?.[0] ?? rule.hosts?.[0]` fallback chain that constructs the `matchedPattern` field on `PermissionResult.provenance` was collapsed onto a single line (fits under 100 columns). Semantics identical — still evaluates the same four optional-chain lookups in the same order (`tools`, `paths`, `commands`, `hosts`) and returns the first non-`undefined` element via nullish coalescing. Consumed only for human-readable display in the permission-prompt UI (`src/permission/prompts.ts` and the Ink components under `src/cli/tui/`); no runtime effect on permission decisions themselves — those flow through `PermissionManager.matchRule(ctx, rule)` earlier in the same evaluate loop.
  - **`src/providers/sapOrchestration.ts`** (`+8/-6`, `prepareRequest<T>` type parameter block, around line 395-402): The generic type parameter list and `ctx` parameter's intersection type (`{ providerId: string; modelId: string; auth: { type?: string; source?: string }; prompt: LanguageModelV2Prompt } & T`) were reformatted from a single-line signature (which exceeded 100 columns) to a multi-line block. The exported signature `export function prepareRequest<T extends { prompt: LanguageModelV2Prompt }>(ctx: { ... } & T): T` is preserved verbatim in its behaviour — same intersection type, same return type, same callable shape. Function body unchanged (still calls `isChatGPTSubscription(ctx.auth)` and conditionally applies `applyCacheBreakpoint` via `supportsPromptCacheBreakpoint`). No impact on OpenAI-family prompt-cache-breakpoint scoping, which continues to route SAP AI Core OpenAI models through the same Vercel-AI-SDK path documented in `docs/PROVIDERS.md`.

  `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run format:check`, and `npm run build` remain green on the branch — this pass is the mechanical follow-up to a prior hand-edit that had used multi-line formatting where Prettier's `printWidth: 100` policy prefers a single line (or vice versa). See `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` under **Style Auto-Fix** for the standing pattern.

## [1.20.0] - 2026-08-10

### Added

- **Claude 4.5/4.6/4.7 opus family support** (`src/providers/index.ts`, PR #1355, commit `769ffe4b`, 2026-08-10): Extended provider model registry to recognize the Claude 4.5, 4.6, and 4.7 opus model family (`claude-4.5-opus`, `claude-4.6-opus`, `claude-4.7-opus`) from Anthropic via SAP AI Core Orchestration. Models are dispatched through the existing `getProviderForModel` surface in `src/providers/index.ts` with no breaking API changes. The provider now also skips sending the `temperature` parameter when routing requests to these opus models, following Anthropic's recommendation to allow the model's default sampling strategy for opus-class reasoning workloads. User-visible impact: CLI commands (`ax chat -m "sap-ai-core/anthropic--claude-4.7-opus"`) and routing-config model selectors (`routing-config.json`) can now reference the 4.5/4.6/4.7 opus variants directly. No changes required for existing orchestrator consumers (`src/cli/commands/chat.ts`, `src/cli/interactive.ts`, `src/agent/index.ts`).

### Fixed

- **MCP server startup timeout reduced from 30s to 3s** (`src/mcp/client.ts`, PR #1345, commit `3030aae0`, 2026-08-10): The MCP client's default startup timeout for connecting to MCP servers (configured via `mcp-servers.json`) has been capped from 30 seconds to 3 seconds to prevent long-running CLI hangs when an MCP server process fails to emit its ready signal. The timeout is still configurable per-server via the `timeout` field in `mcp-servers.json` (e.g., `"timeout": 5000` for 5 seconds), but the fallback default is now more aggressive. User-visible impact: MCP server connection failures surface faster in the CLI output, reducing perceived hangs during session startup. Servers that genuinely require >3s to initialize should declare an explicit `timeout` in their `mcp-servers.json` entry. No changes required for existing MCP server configurations that already specify a `timeout` field.

### Changed

  - `@typescript-eslint/eslint-plugin`: `8.65.0` → `8.66.0` (PR #1353, commit `cbd41224`) — patch update; dev-only dependency used by ESLint for TypeScript-specific linting rules. No runtime impact.

  - `@hono/node-server`: `2.0.12` → `2.1.0` (PR #1354, commit `098307fe`) — minor version bump; used by `src/server/index.ts` for the embedded HTTP server surface. No breaking changes impacting Alexi's usage (the server only exposes `/health` and `/chat` endpoints). Build and test gates remain green.
  - `tsx`: `4.23.1` → `4.23.10` (PR #1352, commit `edf7d9bf`) — patch update; dev-only dependency used by `npm run dev` for no-build CLI execution via `tsx src/cli/program.ts`. No runtime impact.

## [1.19.0] - 2026-08-03

### Added

- **Context-overflow recovery telemetry and status notices** (`src/core/streamingOrchestrator.ts`, PR #1260, commit `96a0a481`, 2026-08-03): Extended the context-overflow detection and recovery system (introduced in 1.18.20) with structured telemetry emission and user-facing status notices. When the streaming orchestrator detects a provider-level context-length error during message accumulation, it now emits a `ContextOverflowRecovered` event to the event bus (consumed by session logs and TUI status panels) and includes the overflow metadata (requested token count, model limit, recovery strategy applied) in the session state. User-visible impact: the TUI and CLI output now surface context-overflow recoveries as actionable notices (e.g., "Context limit reached. Truncating 2 older messages.") rather than silent fallback behaviour. No breaking API changes — existing orchestrator consumers (`src/cli/commands/chat.ts`, `src/cli/interactive.ts`) require no updates. The telemetry schema is defined via Zod in `src/bus/index.ts` and follows the existing event-bus contract.

- **Context-overflow detection and recovery in streaming orchestrator** (`src/core/streamingOrchestrator.ts`, PR #1248, commit `4196e15b`, 2026-08-02): The streaming orchestrator now automatically detects context-length overflows reported by the SAP AI Core Orchestration provider (HTTP 400 with `context_length_exceeded` error code or `invalid_request_error` type containing "maximum context length" substring) and retries the request with a truncated message history. Recovery strategy: on overflow, the orchestrator discards the two oldest non-system messages from the conversation history and retries the LLM call with the reduced context. The retry loop is bounded at 3 attempts; after exhausting retries, the original error is surfaced to the caller. No user-facing API changes — the recovery is transparent to CLI consumers (`src/cli/commands/chat.ts`, `src/cli/interactive.ts`, `src/agent/index.ts`). This feature addresses the long-tail case where multi-turn sessions with large tool outputs exceed model context limits (e.g., Claude 3.5 Sonnet's 200k token window), which previously required manual session resets. The implementation is provider-agnostic (the error classification lives in `src/providers/index.ts` via the `isContextOverflowError` helper) but currently only SAP Orchestration provider errors are mapped; direct OpenAI-compatible proxy errors are not yet classified.

### Changed

- **Dependency updates** (2026-08-03):
  - `puppeteer`: `25.3.0` → `25.4.0` (PR #1250, commit `feb75e9c`) — patch update with no breaking changes; used by `src/tool/tools/webfetch.ts` for headless browser-based page fetching when Readability extraction fails.
  - `@vitejs/plugin-react`: `6.0.4` → `6.0.5` (PR #1252, commit `16991c9e`) — patch update; dev-only dependency used by Vitest for TUI component testing (`src/cli/tui/**/*.test.tsx`).
  - `@hono/node-server`: `1.19.17` → `2.0.12` (PR #1253, commit `2b696fc1`) — **major version bump**; used by `src/server/index.ts` for the embedded HTTP server surface. No breaking changes impacting Alexi's usage (the server only exposes `/health` and `/chat` endpoints with no Hono-specific middleware that changed between 1.x and 2.x). Build and test gates remain green.
  - `lint-staged`: `17.2.0` → `17.3.0` (PR #1251, commit `9e41a064`) — patch update; dev-only dependency used by the husky `pre-commit` hook to run ESLint and Prettier on staged files.
  - `@types/react`: dev-dependencies group update (PR #1249, commit `4bec147f`) — TypeScript type definition refresh for React 19 (Ink TUI layer). No runtime impact.

  - Factory consulting workflow execution (`chore(ci): factory consulting run [agent]`, commit `3bf5b4a8`).

## [1.18.19] - 2026-08-02

### Changed

- **Package version bump** (`package.json`, commit `824408c5`, `feat(sync): apply upstream changes (2026-08-02)`, 2026-08-02): `1.18.18` → `1.18.19`. No CLI surface, runtime shape, provider routing, session persistence, tool registry, event bus, or Ink TUI behaviour changed as a direct consequence of the version bump itself. Two orphan-file regressions the same sync also emitted are documented under the paired `### Fixed`-follow-up expectation below (paired autohealing sweep pending). ### Deprecated

- **Two upstream-sync-emitted orphan stub files pending autohealing sweep** (introduced by commit `824408c5`, `feat(sync): apply upstream changes (2026-08-02)`, 2026-08-02): The 2026-08-02 upstream sync emitted two broken source files that fail `npm run typecheck` (`TS2304` on `AgentConfig` and `tokenize` respectively) and are not imported by any module in `src/` or `tests/`. Neither file participates in Alexi's canonical runtime surface. Both are queued for deletion by the paired autohealing sweep following the well-established `sync -> auto-fix -> autohealing` pattern documented in every prior daily sync entry in this changelog. Per-file description:
  - **`src/agent/config.ts`** overwritten (`+4/-72`, `76-line` real module replaced by a `4-line` broken stub): The pre-sync file declared the `AgentConfigSchema` Zod schema (four optional/nullable fields: `steps: number | null`, `enabled: boolean`, `maxTokens: number`, `temperature: number` in `[0, 2]`), the derived `AgentConfigData` type via `z.infer<typeof AgentConfigSchema>`, the `normalizeAgentConfig(config: unknown): AgentConfigData` helper that preserves the explicit-`null` vs. `undefined` distinction on the `steps` field ("use default" vs. "not set"), the `mergeAgentConfig(base, override)` helper that treats `undefined` in the override as "keep base" and any other value (including explicit `null`) as an override, and the `getEffectiveSteps(config, defaultSteps = 50)` accessor that resolves `null | undefined` steps to a default. The sync replaced all of that with a 4-line non-parsing stub declaring `function routeSubagent(config: AgentConfig)` with an empty body and no import for the free identifier `AgentConfig`, producing `TS2304: Cannot find name 'AgentConfig'` under `npm run typecheck`. The file has no trailing newline (fails `prettier --check`), no exports, no imports, and no live consumers — `grep -r "from.*agent/config" src/ tests/` returns no matches, so the pre-sync module was already an orphan (no runtime module ever imported `AgentConfigSchema`, `normalizeAgentConfig`, `mergeAgentConfig`, or `getEffectiveSteps`; the canonical agent-configuration surface lives inline in `src/agent/index.ts` and `src/agent/prompts/*.ts`). The replacement stub is also an orphan. Delete disposition expected in the paired autohealing sweep. Do **not** treat this as a legitimate refactor of the agent configuration surface — there is no `routeSubagent` on Alexi's runtime, agent routing is performed by `src/core/router.ts` operating on the routing-config JSON, and per-subagent dispatch (when applicable) flows through `src/agent/index.ts`.
  - **`src/cli/reasoning.ts`** added (4-line new-file stub): Contains `function countTokens(input: string): number { return tokenize(input).length; }` with no import for the free identifier `tokenize`, producing `TS2304: Cannot find name 'tokenize'` under `npm run typecheck`. The file has no trailing newline (fails `prettier --check`), no exports, no imports, and no live consumers — `grep -r "from.*cli/reasoning" src/ tests/` returns no matches. The `src/cli/` subdirectory is Alexi's Commander.js CLI entrypoint surface (`program.ts` and per-subcommand modules under `src/cli/commands/`, plus the interactive REPL at `src/cli/interactive.ts` and TUI at `src/cli/tui/`); there is no `reasoning` module on the canonical CLI surface, no `countTokens`/`tokenize` public API, and no reasoning-token surface — token accounting in Alexi is delegated to the SAP AI Core Orchestration provider response (`src/providers/sapOrchestration.ts` reads `usage.prompt_tokens` / `usage.completion_tokens` / `usage.total_tokens` from the orchestration response envelope) and surfaced to the session log via `src/core/sessionManager.ts` without a per-message tokenisation pass in the CLI layer. Delete disposition expected in the paired autohealing sweep. Do **not** treat this as an introduction of a client-side reasoning-token counter.

  `npm run typecheck` fails on the branch with exactly two errors (`src/agent/config.ts(2,32): error TS2304: Cannot find name 'AgentConfig'` and `src/cli/reasoning.ts(3,10): error TS2304: Cannot find name 'tokenize'`) until the paired autohealing sweep deletes both files. `npm run lint`, `npm run format:check`, and `npm run build` are similarly expected to fail on the same two files. Recorded under `### Deprecated` (rather than `### Removed`, `### Fixed`, or `### Changed`) because the correct downstream action is deletion in the follow-up autohealing commit, per the standing `sync -> auto-fix -> autohealing` invariant.

### Fixed

- **CI auto-fix pass on three upstream-sync stub files under `src/permission/` and `src/tool/`** (commit `36ac95b2`, `style(ci): auto-fix lint/format issues [alexi-bot]`, 2026-08-01): The CI auto-fix workflow ran Prettier on three orphan scaffold files emitted by the 2026-08-01 upstream sync (commit `b8b9f01b`, `feat(sync): apply upstream changes (2026-08-01)`, version bump `1.18.17` → `1.18.18`, upstream heads `Kilo-Org/kilocode` → `0aabd47b58ec2575c38ad37954fdc1e0098e78d7` and `anomalyco/opencode` → `19231fce4b70aa5f7894a0a0eb20ff29bd417db5`). Prettier reflowed double-quoted string literals to single quotes per the root `.prettierrc` (`singleQuote: true`, `trailingComma: 'es5'`, `printWidth: 100`, `tabWidth: 2`, LF line endings) and appended trailing newlines where missing. Aggregate diff: `3 files changed, 7 insertions(+), 7 deletions(-)`. Affected files:
  - **`src/permission/PermissionView.ts`** (`+5/-5`, plus trailing newline): Reflowed four double-quoted string literals (`"skillShell"`, `"skill"`, `"session.permission.skillShell.title"`, `"session.permission.title"`) to single quotes inside a 12-line `updatePermissionView(card, permission)` function that reads `permission.meta.raw['skillShell']` and `permission.meta.raw['skill']`, sets a card header via `KiloBundle.message(...)`, and calls `syncDescription(description(permission))`. Broken on multiple axes: (1) the sole `import { escapeControl, description, KiloBundle } from 'utils'` targets a bare module specifier `'utils'` which is neither a package in `package.json` nor a repository path alias in `tsconfig.json` (`paths` is unset), producing `TS2307: Cannot find module 'utils'`; (2) `syncDescription` is an undeclared free identifier, producing `TS2304: Cannot find name 'syncDescription'`; (3) `card` and `permission` parameters are untyped (implicit `any`), failing the strict-mode `noImplicitAny` check in `tsconfig.json`; (4) the file has no module structure beyond a single exported function and is not imported by any module in `src/` or `tests/` — Alexi's canonical permission-prompt surface lives in `src/permission/index.ts` (the `PermissionManager` class, JSON persistence to `~/.alexi/permissions.json`) and `src/permission/prompts.ts` (the Ink-based TUI prompt component consumed by `src/cli/tui/`), per `docs/permission-system.md` and `docs/permission-prompt-quick-ref.md`. There is no `updatePermissionView(card, permission)` surface on Alexi's runtime — permission views are React/Ink components, not imperative `card.setHeader(...)` calls against a mutable card object. Not imported by any Alexi runtime module.
  - **`src/tool/BaseSearchToolView.ts`** (`+1/-1`, trailing newline appended): Reformatted a 3-line file declaring `export function bindHeader(parts)` whose body calls itself recursively with `bindHeader(parts.glyph, parts.title, parts.sub, parts.state, parts.left, parts.right, parts.slot)`. Broken on multiple axes: (1) infinite recursion — the function calls itself with different argument shapes but no base case, producing an immediate stack overflow at first invocation; (2) `parts` is untyped (implicit `any`), failing `noImplicitAny`; (3) the two overloads (single-object argument vs. 7-argument positional) are incompatible without an explicit overload signature, producing `TS2554: Expected 1 argument, but got 7`; (4) the file is not imported by any module in `src/` or `tests/` — Alexi's canonical search-tool views are Ink React components under `src/cli/tui/components/` and tool result rendering flows through `src/bus/index.ts` events, not a `bindHeader(parts)` function-call surface. Not imported by any Alexi runtime module.
  - **`src/tool/PatchBody.ts`** (`+1/-1`, trailing newline appended): Reformatted a 6-line file that imports `diffOverflowPanel` from `./DiffOverflow` (relative import omits the mandatory `.js` extension required by the project's `NodeNext` module resolution — per `AGENTS.md`'s "every local import MUST end in `.js`" rule, this fails at test-runtime and at Node.js resolution time) and contains a top-level `if (diffLines.length > DIFF_MAX_LINES) { return diffOverflowPanel(openDiff); }` block. Broken on multiple axes: (1) `return` at the top level of an ES module is a `SyntaxError`; (2) `diffLines`, `DIFF_MAX_LINES`, and `openDiff` are all undeclared free identifiers, producing three `TS2304` errors under `npm run typecheck`; (3) the co-emitted `src/tool/DiffOverflow.ts` (also from the same sync) declares `function diffOverflowPanel(open: () => void): JComponent` — where `JComponent` is a Swing/JavaFX type name that has no meaning in a TypeScript/Node.js/Ink codebase — so even if the top-level `return` were valid, the return type would fail to resolve; (4) the file is not imported by any module in `src/` or `tests/`. Alexi's canonical diff-rendering surface for the `edit` and `apply-patch` tools lives in `src/tool/tools/edit.ts` and `src/tool/tools/apply-patch.ts` with Ink React components in `src/cli/tui/` — there is no `PatchBody`/`DiffOverflow` module pair on the runtime. Not imported by any Alexi runtime module.

  Pure formatting change with no behavioural, API, CLI-surface, runtime, provider-routing, session-persistence, tool-registry, event-bus, or Ink TUI impact — all three files remain broken code (undeclared identifiers `TS2304`, unresolved bare-module imports `TS2307`, top-level `return` `SyntaxError`, missing `.js` extensions on ESM imports, implicit-`any` parameters, absent from tool/permission-view registries) after the reformatting pass and will be removed by autohealing in a subsequent commit following the well-established `sync -> auto-fix -> autohealing` pattern documented in the 2026-07-31 `76f9a9e3`, 2026-07-30 `1211813a`/`f3e613a0`, 2026-07-29 `3a9b850b`/`b44ac96f`, 2026-07-24 `530351f4`, 2026-07-22 `c3a88212`/`8c174021`, 2026-07-21 `7b47d5e0`, 2026-07-17 `0305e95f`, 2026-07-15 `48e05a80`, and 2026-07-04 `441b4c14` entries. The 2026-08-01 sync commit `b8b9f01b` also emitted three additional orphan files not touched by this auto-fix pass (`src/tool/DiffOverflow.ts`, `src/tool/EditToolView.ts`, `src/tool/ReadToolView.ts`) which are expected to appear in the same autohealing sweep. Recorded for autohealing follow-up completeness so the historical audit trail is legible in the changelog. `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, and `npm run build` are expected to fail on the branch until the paired autohealing sweep deletes these files.

Broken on multiple axes: (1) `Proc` is an undeclared free identifier producing `TS2304`; (2) the file imports `child_process.spawn` and `node:timers/promises#setTimeout as sleep` but references neither, failing `no-unused-vars`; (3) the target subdirectory `src/core/kilocode/pty/` did not exist prior to this sync — Alexi has no PTY-based execution surface, and the canonical shell-execution tool (`src/tool/bash.ts`) uses `execa`, not a custom PTY termination pipeline. Broken on multiple axes: (1) `Flock` is imported from the `flock` package — not a repository dependency (`npm ls flock` reports no such module in `package.json`); (2) `loadFromDisk`, `fetchAndWrite`, and `Provider` are all undeclared, producing three `TS2304` errors; (3) `Effect.scoped` and `Effect.gen` generator delegation are Effect-TS patterns Alexi does not use — model discovery in Alexi is a plain async call in `src/providers/index.ts` (`listAvailableModels`) that reads deployments from SAP AI Core with no local lock file or `models.dev` cache. Not imported by any Alexi runtime module.
  - **`src/core/test/pty/pty-session.test.ts`** (`+11/-11`): Reflowed a 22-line Vitest suite `describe('PTYSession Termination Handling', ...)` that imports `PTYSession` from `../pty/termination`. Broken on multiple axes: (1) `../pty/termination` resolves to `src/core/test/pty/../pty/termination` which does not exist — the sibling stub at `src/core/kilocode/pty/termination.ts` exports only `Process` and `Runtime` types, no `PTYSession` class; (2) the relative import omits the mandatory `.js` extension required by the project's `NodeNext` module resolution (per `AGENTS.md`'s "every local import MUST end in `.js`" rule), so the file fails at test-runtime; (3) `PTYSession` is undeclared anywhere in `src/`, producing `TS2305: Module has no exported member 'PTYSession'`. Not registered in `vitest.config.ts`'s discovery globs beyond the generic `src/**/*.test.ts` sweep, which would surface it as a failing suite.
  - **`src/tool/shell-signal.test.ts`** (`+11/-11`): Reflowed a 22-line Vitest suite `describe('ToolShell Signal Handling', ...)` that imports `ToolShell` from `./shell`. Broken on the same NodeNext ESM axis as the PTY-session test above: the relative import omits `.js` (`./shell` instead of `./shell.js`), which will fail at test-runtime under `type: module` + `NodeNext`. The imported `ToolShell` class exists in the co-emitted `src/tool/shell.ts` stub but is not registered in `src/tool/registry.ts`, not exposed via `defineTool` from `src/tool/index.ts`, and not part of Alexi's canonical tool set (`bash`, `read`, `write`, `edit`, `glob`, `grep`, `task`, `webfetch`, `task_status`, `question`, `todowrite`, `apply-patch` — see `src/tool/registry.ts` and `src/tool/builtins.ts`). Not part of the tool registry surface.
  - **`src/tool/shell.ts`** (`+9/-9`): Reflowed a 13-line `ToolShell` class exposing a synchronous `handleSignal(signal: string): string` method that returns hardcoded strings `'terminated gracefully'` (`SIGTERM`), `'terminated forcefully'` (`SIGKILL`), or `'unknown signal'`. Broken on multiple axes: (1) not a `defineTool`-shaped tool — has no Zod schema, no `execute` handler with `{ args, ctx }` signature, and no registry entry, so it is invisible to the tool-dispatch surface in `src/agent/index.ts` and `src/core/agenticChat.ts`; (2) duplicates functionality already covered by `src/tool/bash.ts` (which routes shell execution through `execa` with proper signal handling and permission prompts via `src/permission/index.ts`); (3) not exported through `src/tool/index.ts` or `src/tool/registry.ts`. Not part of the tool registry surface.
  - **`src/tool/skill.ts`** (`+13/-13`): Reflowed a 21-line `SkillManager` class exposing `addSkill(name, skill)`, `getSkill(name)`, `removeSkill(name)`, and `listSkills(): string[]` over an in-memory `Map<string, any>`. Broken on multiple axes: (1) uses `any` for the skill value type, which the project-wide `@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any` ESLint rule flags as a warning under strict mode; (2) the canonical skill-system implementation already lives at `src/skill/index.ts` (loaded skills, persistence to `~/.alexi/skills/`, TUI integration in `src/cli/tui/`) — this `SkillManager` class duplicates a subset of that surface without touching disk state, permissions, or the skill-loading protocol documented in `builtin/kilo-config`; (3) not exported through `src/tool/index.ts` or `src/tool/registry.ts` and not referenced by `src/agent/index.ts`, so it is dead code from the tool-dispatch surface. Not part of the tool registry or the canonical skill system.

  Pure formatting change with no behavioural, API, CLI-surface, runtime, provider-routing, session-persistence, tool-registry, event-bus, or Ink TUI impact — all eight files remain broken code (unused imports, undeclared identifiers `TS2304`, missing `.js` extensions on ESM imports, absent from tool/test registries) after the reformatting pass and will be removed by autohealing in a subsequent commit following the well-established `sync -> auto-fix -> autohealing` pattern documented in the 2026-07-30 `1211813a`/`f3e613a0`, 2026-07-29 `3a9b850b`/`b44ac96f`, 2026-07-24 `530351f4`, 2026-07-22 `c3a88212`/`8c174021`, 2026-07-21 `7b47d5e0`, 2026-07-17 `0305e95f`, 2026-07-15 `48e05a80`, and 2026-07-04 `441b4c14` entries. Recorded for autohealing follow-up completeness so the historical audit trail is legible in the changelog. `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, and `npm run build` are expected to fail on the branch until the paired autohealing sweep deletes these files.

- **JavaScript-style comment removed from `infra/nix/hashes.json` to restore valid JSON** (commit `e708015a`, `fix(ci): remove invalid JSON comment from infra/nix/hashes.json [alexi-bot]`, 2026-07-30): The autohealing workflow removed a `// updated hashes` line-comment nested inside the top-level `"hashes"` object of `infra/nix/hashes.json`, which had caused the file to fail strict JSON parsing (JSON does not permit `//` or `/* */` comments per RFC 8259 §2, unlike JSON5 / JSONC). The now-empty `hashes` object was collapsed to `{}` on the same line, yielding the canonical two-line file `{ "hashes": {} }`. Aggregate diff: `1 file changed, 1 insertion(+), 3 deletions(-)`. Pure syntax fix with no behavioural, API, CLI-surface, runtime, provider-routing, session-persistence, tool-registry, event-bus, or Ink TUI impact — `infra/nix/hashes.json` is metadata consumed only by the reproducible-build tooling under `infra/nix/` (Nix flake hash tracking for pinned upstream artefacts) and is not loaded by the CLI runtime (`src/cli/program.ts`), the orchestrator (`src/core/orchestrator.ts`), the provider dispatch surface (`src/providers/index.ts`), or the TypeScript build pipeline (`tsc -p tsconfig.json`); `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run format:check`, and `npm test` do not read it. The fix restores JSON-parser compatibility for any tooling that may parse the file strictly (`jq`, `JSON.parse` in Node.js scripts, `nix` evaluators via `builtins.fromJSON`, editor schema validators, and CI JSON-lint steps). This entry continues the standing invariant documented in the 2026-07-30 `a0a4419d` `src/core/package.json` trailing-comma cleanup entry below and the earlier `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` §"Autohealing" note that JSON-syntax violations emitted by upstream sync or manual edits are removed by autohealing rather than tolerated with JSONC-style relaxed parsing.
- **Trailing comma removed from `src/core/package.json` to restore valid JSON** (commit `a0a4419d`, `fix(ci): remove trailing comma in src/core/package.json [autohealing]`, 2026-07-30): The autohealing workflow removed a trailing comma after the `"license": "MIT"` entry in `src/core/package.json`, which had caused the file to fail strict JSON parsing (JSON does not permit trailing commas per RFC 8259 §5, unlike JSON5 / JSONC). The file is a workspace-marker manifest for the `@alexi-ai/core` scope that only declares `$schema`, `version`, `name`, `type`, and `license` — it has no `main`, `bin`, `scripts`, `dependencies`, or `exports`, and is not published to npm (the canonical publishable manifest is the repository-root `package.json`). Aggregate diff: `1 file changed, 1 insertion(+), 1 deletion(-)`. Pure syntax fix with no behavioural, API, CLI-surface, runtime, provider-routing, session-persistence, tool-registry, event-bus, or Ink TUI impact — the manifest is not loaded by the CLI runtime (`src/cli/program.ts`), the orchestrator (`src/core/orchestrator.ts`), or the build pipeline (`tsc -p tsconfig.json`), and `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run format:check`, and `npm test` do not read it. The fix restores JSON-parser compatibility for tooling that may consume the file (`json.schemastore.org/package.json` schema validators, editor IntelliSense, `jq`, and any future workspace-aware manifest reader) without altering the declared workspace metadata (name `@alexi-ai/core`, version `7.4.17`, license `MIT`, ESM module type). No downstream consumer required changes.

### Removed

- **Three upstream-sync orphan stub files under `src/cli/` and `src/providers/`** (commit `f3e613a0`, `fix(ci): drop unused upstream-sync stubs failing lint/typecheck [autohealing]`, 2026-07-30): The autohealing workflow deleted three broken scaffold files that the 2026-07-30 upstream sync (commit `988f1acf`, `feat(sync): apply upstream changes (2026-07-30)`, version bump `1.18.15` → `1.18.16`) had emitted under `src/cli/` and `src/providers/`. None of the three files were imported by any module in `src/` or `tests/`, all three failed at least one of `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, or `npm run format:check`, and none participated in Alexi's canonical CLI or provider surface. Aggregate diff: `3 files changed, 9 deletions(-)`. Per-file rationale:
  - **`src/cli/console.ts`** (5-line stub): Contained only `const compatibilityFlag = true;` plus two comments and a placeholder line. Broken on multiple axes: (1) `compatibilityFlag` is declared but never referenced, failing the `no-unused-vars` ESLint rule (the project-wide ESLint configuration in `eslint.config.js` treats declared-but-unused non-`_`-prefixed identifiers as errors); (2) there is no `console` module on Alexi's canonical CLI surface — the CLI entrypoint is `src/cli/program.ts` (registered as bins `alexi` and `ax` in `package.json`), per-subcommand implementations live under `src/cli/commands/`, and the interactive REPL lives at `src/cli/interactive.ts` and `src/cli/tui/`; (3) any legitimate console output routes through `src/utils/logger.ts` (the sole module allowed to use `console` per the `no-console` project-wide rule documented in `AGENTS.md`). Delete disposition applied.
  - **`src/providers/gitlab-ai-provider.ts`** (1-line stub): Contained only `const providerVersion = '6.12.1';` with no trailing newline. Broken on multiple axes: (1) `providerVersion` is declared but never referenced, failing `no-unused-vars`; (2) the file has no trailing newline, failing `prettier --check`; (3) it introduces a *GitLab AI* provider surface that is inconsistent with Alexi's provider policy — **SAP AI Core Orchestration is the sole provider** (`src/providers/sapOrchestration.ts`, dispatched by `getProviderForModel` in `src/providers/index.ts`), documented in `docs/PROVIDERS.md`. There is no GitLab-direct provider, no BYOK surface, and no OpenAI-compatible ingress — `SAP_PROXY_BASE_URL` / `SAP_PROXY_API_KEY` route requests to a local proxy fronting SAP AI Core, not to a third-party AI backend. Delete disposition applied; do not treat this as a GitLab-provider introduction.
  - **`src/providers/modal/models.ts`** (3-line stub): Contained `export const discoverModalModels = (): ModalModel[] => { /* logic ... */ };` with an empty function body. Broken on multiple axes: (1) `ModalModel` is an undeclared free identifier, producing `TS2304: Cannot find name 'ModalModel'` under `npm run typecheck`; (2) the arrow function has a declared return type of `ModalModel[]` but no return statement, which would produce `TS2355: A function whose declared type is neither 'undefined', 'void', nor 'any' must return a value` once the `ModalModel` reference resolved; (3) the target subdirectory `src/providers/modal/` did not exist prior to this sync and has no other members — canonical provider modules live at `src/providers/sapOrchestration.ts`, `src/providers/auth.ts`, `src/providers/transform.ts`, `src/providers/ca.ts`, and `src/providers/index.ts` (the dispatch surface), none of which reference a *Modal* provider; (4) *Modal* (`modal.com`) is a serverless-compute platform, not an LLM backend on Alexi's supported providers list — the sole supported backend remains SAP AI Core Orchestration. No module in `src/` or `tests/` imports `discoverModalModels`. Delete disposition applied along with the now-empty `src/providers/modal/` directory.

- **Five upstream-sync orphan stub files under `src/core/` and `src/tool/`** (commit `b44ac96f`, `fix(ci): remove orphan stub files from upstream sync [autohealing]`, 2026-07-29): The autohealing workflow deleted five broken scaffold files that the 2026-07-29 upstream sync (commit `719046d4`, `feat(sync): apply upstream changes (2026-07-29)`, version bump `1.18.14` → `1.18.15`) had emitted directly under `src/core/` and `src/tool/`. All five files were single-fragment code snippets copied verbatim from upstream `sst/opencode` sources (Effect-based monorepo layout) with no valid module structure — none of them parsed as TypeScript, none were imported by any module in `src/` or `tests/`, and each violated multiple Alexi conventions (`no-console`, ESM `.js` import suffix rule, Zod-only validation surface, single-package layout). Aggregate diff: `5 files changed, 20 deletions(-)`. Per-file rationale:
  - **`src/core/config/plugin/provider.ts`** (1-line fragment): Contained only the bare statement `const integrationTransform = yield* integrations.transform()`. Broken on multiple axes: (1) `yield*` outside a generator function is a `SyntaxError`; (2) `integrations` is undeclared, so `npm run typecheck` fails with `TS2304: Cannot find name 'integrations'`; (3) the target subdirectory `src/core/config/plugin/` did not exist prior to this sync and has no other members. Canonical provider configuration in Alexi lives in `src/providers/sapOrchestration.ts` (the sole `SapOrchestrationProvider` implementation) and `src/providers/index.ts` (the `getProviderForModel` dispatch surface), not a nested `src/core/config/plugin/provider.ts` module. There is no plugin-provider surface in Alexi — the plugin system in `src/plugin/` is orthogonal to the provider layer and does not compose transforms via generator delegation. Delete disposition applied.
  - **`src/core/credential.ts`** (1-line fragment): Contained only the bare interface member declaration `readonly get: (id: ID) => Effect.Effect<Stored | undefined>`. Broken on multiple axes: (1) an interface member appearing at the top level of a module is a `SyntaxError` (it must be inside a `type`/`interface` body); (2) `ID`, `Effect`, and `Stored` are undeclared free identifiers, producing three `TS2304` errors; (3) `Effect.Effect<...>` is an Effect-TS pattern that Alexi does not use — the repository standardises on Zod for schema validation (`src/tool/index.ts`, `src/bus/index.ts`) and plain `async/await` for I/O. There is no credential-store abstraction on Alexi's runtime surface — SAP AI Core credentials are read from environment variables (`AICORE_SERVICE_KEY`, `AICORE_RESOURCE_GROUP`) by `src/providers/auth.ts` and the XSUAA token cache lives inside the same module, not in a top-level `src/core/credential.ts`. Delete disposition applied.
  - **`src/core/integration.ts`** (1-line fragment): Contained only the truncated statement `export const TextPrompt = Schema.Struct({` — an unterminated object literal with no closing `})` and no import for `Schema`. Broken on multiple axes: (1) unterminated object literal is a `SyntaxError`; (2) `Schema` is undeclared, producing `TS2304`; (3) `Schema.Struct` is an Effect-Schema-style primitive that Alexi does not use — canonical schema definitions in Alexi are Zod (`z.object`, `z.string`, etc.) via `defineTool` in `src/tool/index.ts` and `defineEvent` in `src/bus/index.ts`. There is no `TextPrompt` type on Alexi's runtime surface — user prompts flow through the orchestrator as plain strings and are typed at the boundary via `ChatMessage` (`src/core/orchestrator.ts`) or as tool argument objects validated by their respective Zod schemas. Delete disposition applied.
  - **`src/tool/code-mode-integration.test.ts`** (1-line fragment): Contained only the bare statement `server.setRequestHandler(ListToolsRequestSchema, async () => ({ tools: TOOL_DEFS }))`. Broken on multiple axes: (1) `server`, `ListToolsRequestSchema`, and `TOOL_DEFS` are all undeclared, producing three `TS2304` errors; (2) the file has no `describe`/`it` block, so Vitest would not register it as a test suite; (3) it is a snippet from an MCP server request-handler registration site (upstream `sst/opencode` `packages/opencode/src/tool/code-mode-integration.test.ts`), which references the MCP `ListToolsRequestSchema` pattern from `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` — Alexi's MCP server integration lives in `src/mcp/server.ts` and does not use a `code-mode` tool. Delete disposition applied; the canonical MCP integration test surface remains `src/mcp/*.test.ts`.
  - **`src/tool/code-mode.ts`** (13-line fragment): Contained an unclosed `return yield* Effect.promise(async () => { ... })` block calling `input.entry.tool.client.callTool(...)` with an MCP `CallToolResultSchema` validator and a `resetTimeoutOnProgress` option. Broken on multiple axes: (1) `return` and `yield*` outside a function body are both `SyntaxError`; (2) `input`, `Effect`, and `CallToolResultSchema` are undeclared; (3) `Effect.promise` and `yield*` delegation to an effect are Effect-TS patterns Alexi does not use; (4) the tool would need to be registered in `src/tool/registry.ts` and follow the `defineTool` Zod-schema contract from `src/tool/index.ts` — neither of which is true for this file. Alexi has no `code-mode` tool: the built-in tool set is `bash`, `read`, `write`, `edit`, `glob`, `grep`, `task`, `webfetch`, and `task_status` (registered through `src/tool/registry.ts`), and MCP tool invocations are routed by `src/mcp/client.ts` using the standard MCP SDK v2 client without an Effect-based wrapper. Delete disposition applied.

  - **`src/agent/instance-advertisement.ts`** (5-line stub): Declares `export function advertiseInstance(instanceId: string): void` with a body that only calls `console.log`. The file violates two Alexi conventions at once: (1) `no-console` is a project-wide ESLint rule (see `AGENTS.md` — only `src/utils/logger.ts` may use `console`, all other modules route through the shared logger), so this file will fail `npm run lint` on a clean run; (2) there is no "instance advertisement" surface anywhere in Alexi — the canonical multi-agent coordination lives in the agent registry at `src/agent/index.ts` (`AgentRegistry`, five built-in agents, `parseAgentSwitch`, `parseAgentMention`, and the `AgentSwitched` bus-event publish path) and per-invocation orchestration lives in `src/core/agenticChat.ts` and `src/core/streamingOrchestrator.ts`. The file is broken on multiple axes: (1) `initializeContext` is not imported or declared anywhere in the file, so `npm run typecheck` fails with `TS2304: Cannot find name 'initializeContext'`; (2) the target directory `src/context/global-sync/` did not exist prior to this sync and has no other members — canonical context modules in Alexi live at `src/context/repoMap.ts`, `src/context/symbolExtractor.ts`, `src/context/symbolRanking.ts`, and `src/context/tree-sitter.ts`, none of which reference a "global sync" bootstrap; (3) no module in `src/` or `tests/` imports `bootstrapGlobalSync`; (4) Alexi's canonical upstream-sync entrypoint is `.github/workflows/sync-upstream.yml` (a two-stage GitHub Actions workflow, planning + execution) plus the tracked commits at `.github/last-sync-commits.json`, not a runtime `bootstrapGlobalSync()` function. Delete in next autohealing sweep along with the empty `src/context/global-sync/` directory.

The file is broken on four axes: (1) `Session` is not imported from `src/core/sessionManager.ts` where the canonical type lives, so `npm run typecheck` fails with `TS2304: Cannot find name 'Session'`; (2) `optimizeSessionData` is likewise undeclared; (3) the missing `return` produces `TS2355: A function whose declared type is neither 'undefined', 'void', nor 'any' must return a value`; (4) even if it compiled, `reduceSession` is not exported and has no consumer in `src/` or `tests/`. Canonical session-state handling in Alexi lives in `src/core/sessionManager.ts` (the `SessionManager` class, session persistence to `~/.alexi/sessions/`) and the checkpoint/undo surfaces at `src/core/checkpoints.ts` and `src/undo/` — there is no reducer-shaped session pipeline. Delete in next autohealing sweep.

All five files fail at build time with `TS2307: Cannot find module 'packages/...'` and at runtime with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, breaking the ESM build and every downstream consumer. This entry records the regression for autohealing; the invariant documented in earlier `### Removed` entries (2026-07-22 `c3a88212`, 2026-07-22 `8c174021`, 2026-07-21 `7b47d5e0`, 2026-07-17 `0305e95f`, 2026-07-15 `48e05a80`, 2026-07-04 `441b4c14`) continues to apply: sync-emitted stubs referencing non-existent `packages/` monorepo paths are upstream noise and must be reverted (for previously-populated modules) or deleted (for orphans) by the next autohealing sweep. Per-file impact:
